> **Predictions first.** This file is on the README's *Predictions first* list: until a module's predictions are committed (`scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --check`), do not open it, and keep it closed during an exit. It names every prediction, hole and exit item of every module (an exit item by its id, form, strand, language and minutes only: exit statements and prompts are author-only, DELTAS Δ133), and never an answer: no answer to a hole, slot or prediction of any module, and no description of a tool behaviour that any prediction asks about.

## 0. How to read this plan

**Item ids** follow the one item grammar (`scripts/course_items.py`): `<ID>.<K><n><lang>` with

| K | kind | checked by | minutes line |
|---|---|---|---|
| W | worked example (given, finished, live) | the gate (it verifies) | worked |
| C | card: `write:<slug>`, `recognise:<slug>` or `diverge:<slug>` | WRITE: a success in the file and a pinned failure demo; divergence and false-friends cards: pinned demo specimens | worked (WRITE cards) / cards |
| P | prediction (`value`, `obligation`, `verdict`) | `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --check` | predicts (Q03's diagnoses: fake-green specimens) |
| H | one-hole item | the done gate (zero tool-counted holes) | holes |
| X | closed-book exit item | `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --exit` | exit |
| T | pretest / test-out item | the done gate: Q02 through the block's commented check, Q03 through its hole. `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --check` records a code T item's first try: the first `--check` at which its block is complete (the learner's own code beyond what shipped, no hole form left), with the block's commented checks enabled in a scratch copy; the record is `predict` only while those checks are still commented (DV2a fix round, §6 Q4) | test-out |
| A | agent-ladder run | the contract's acceptance lines | agent |

`<lang>` is `d` (Dafny file), `v` (Verus file) or `dv` (one item over both verifiers: a cross-language card or a `D:<v> V:<v>` prediction). `pair:` links an H, X or T item to its twin in the other language (design §6.2 C1). `uses:` names the WRITE or RECOGNISE construct an H, X or T item exercises. `need:` (X only) quotes the item's declaration (name and parameters) and, where the block ships one, its use-site check: never anything the learner must write.

**Exit block forms** (spec §3.10). *Statement-shipped*: the statement ships with a hole; `need:` quotes the shipped statement. *Prompt-as-heading*: the prompt is the heading, the block holds no code, the answer is at most three lines; where the item asks for a specification, the block ships a commented use-site check under `Uncomment as you go:` that verifies only with an adequate one, and a `need:` line quotes that check, so leaving it commented or deleting it fails the block. *Verdict*: a `// SLOT: CHECK` switch and a `slots/<ID>.slots` line. In Verus files a heading is a `//` comment (a `///` doc comment needs an item after it).

**Hole forms (tool-counted only).** Dafny proof hole: a bodyless `lemma` (spec §3.3). Dafny exec hole: a body `{ assume false; }`. Dafny annotation hole: the code and its `decreases` given, the annotations the item names missing, one `assume false;` as the first statement. Verus proof hole: `#[verifier::proof_note("<item id>")] assume(false);`. Verus exec hole: `#[verifier::external_body]` over an `unimplemented!()` body. Verus annotation hole (Δ52): the code, the `decreases`, the ghost updates and every proof call given, the loop invariant missing, one labelled `assume(false)` before the loop. A hole is admitted only once `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --empty-body` (author-only) shows it failing in its shipped configuration; the evidence per hole is the author's.

**Order inside an exercise file.** Header; T items; the **prediction block** (every P item whose marker is in the file); W items; WRITE cards; RECOGNISE cards; H items; a **final section** with the divergence and false-friends cards. Commit every prediction of the module before reading past the prediction block, and open a card demo specimen only after that.

**Verdict scenarios.** A verdict item's scenario is quoted as commented code in its docstring, never live (an exercise file stays green), unless the table says *specimen*: a specimen scenario carries the item's CHECK switch, and `scripts/vcheck` refuses to run it until its first try is recorded (H5). Every verdict docstring fixes its answer form token by token. Every obligation specimen's answer form is `FAILS` followed by the failing labels in label order, separated by single spaces, or `FAILS none`.

**Paths.** Every module follows the registry conventions (`MODULES.toml` header, `scripts/gen_units.py`):

| artifact | path |
|---|---|
| Dafny exercise file (unit `core`) | `dafny/Q/<ID>_<Name>/core/<ID>_<Name>.dfy`, module `<ID>_<Name>` |
| Dafny value-predict check unit (given-only) | `dafny/checks/<ID>_checks.dfy`, module `<ID>_Checks` |
| Dafny exit / agent file | `dafny/Q/<ID>_<Name>/exit/<file>.dfy`, `dafny/Q/<ID>_<Name>/agent/<file>.dfy` |
| Verus crate (exercise) | `verus/q/<id>_<name>/src/lib.rs` (crate `<id>_<name>`) |
| Verus exit / agent file (never `mod`-declared) | `verus/q/<id>_<name>/src/exit.rs`, `src/agent.rs` |
| prediction specimens (IDE-ignored) | `specimens/<ID>/<item>.dfy.specimen`, `<item>.rs.specimen` |
| card demo specimens | `specimens/<ID>/card-<item>.<ext>.specimen` (a one-language card), `card-<item>-d.dfy.specimen` and `card-<item>-v.rs.specimen` (a `dv` card, or a card with a demo in each language), `card-<item>-<row>-<d\|v>.<ext>.specimen` (a false-friends row). Named by item id only; a demo states no outcome in prose |
| demo pins | WRITE-card failure demos: in-file `// EXPECT @<line> "<fragment>"` lines. Divergence and false-friends demos: `.expect` sidecars (`expected/specimens/<ID>/<file>.expect`, written by `scripts/pins.py --update`). A demo whose output settles a prediction carries `// after: <item id>` in its header: `scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/vaudit` and `scripts/vcheck --test` refuse it until that item's first try is recorded (H5), and its sidecar is sealed (DV2a fix round, §6 Q10) |
| verdict answers (learner-owned) | `slots/<ID>.slots`, one `<K><n><lang> = ???` line per verdict item |
| pins (author-written) | `expected/<ID>.pins` (salted hashes only, Δ117), `expected/<ID>.counts` (schema 2: the units' verified counts and every exercise, check, exit and agent file's shipped verdict, holes and errors, Δ115), `expected/<ID>.freeze`, `expected/<name>.audit` |
| reference twins (author-only) | `Solutions/<same path>` for every exercise, check, exit and agent file, `Solutions/specimens/<ID>/…`, `Solutions/slots/<ID>.slots` |

**Where value predictions run (Δ94, Δ102).** Dafny value slots live in a standalone, given-only check unit `dafny/checks/<ID>_checks.dfy` (listed in the module's `exercises`; `gen_units.py` generates no check unit; `dvl.dafny` verifies and tests it on the module's given chain). Verus value slots live in specimens (`by (compute_only)` for spec values), never in the hole-bearing crate.

**Minutes** use the design rates (spec §2.5): WRITE card 2.5 (inside "worked"), RECOGNISE or divergence card 1.5–4 (the "cards" line), prediction 2–2.5, intro hole 4, exit item 1.25–4, test-out item 5. Agent wall-clock time is outside every budget.

---

## 1. The BigNat spine: layers L1 and L2

### 1.1 Rules (design §10.1; spec §2.6; Δ22, Δ51, Δ52, Δ54, Δ94)

1. Layers are author-written, complete, hole-free, frozen (guard rule 2f) and imported, never copied. Holes are leaves in a module's own files.
2. A module imports its tier's layer **and every lower layer** (Verus: a dependency on each layer crate of the chain, Δ22). Exercise, check, exit and agent files import given layers only (re-supply, spec §3.3).
3. **The tier law.** An answer to a hole, exit or test-out item of tier *k* appears only in `Solutions/` and in layers of tier > *k*. Because a layer is imported by every later module through the chain, **nothing given in a layer may ever be posed as a hole, exit or test-out item in a module that imports it**: L1 constrains Q01–Q18, L2 constrains Q02–Q18. Because the given spine may stay open during a closed-book exit (spec §3.10), **no layer, of any tier, holds the whole answer of an exit or test-out item of Q01–Q03, and no layer holds the answer of any item of a module that imports it**. A layer of a higher tier may hold the whole answer of a hole of a lower-tier module, which it then re-supplies (§1.5, §8.5): L3 holds the proofs of some Q02–Q03 holes. So the leak patterns' rule 2 (a pattern matches nothing public) exempts a given layer of a tier above the pattern's module, and a leak check of an instructor session for a module applies only the patterns of modules at that module's tier or higher: lower-tier answers are re-supplied, public by design (amended 2026-10-05: §6 Q15, DELTAS Δ130).
4. Learner-visible files (exercise, check, exit and agent files, specimens, slots, `expected/`, this plan, the registry) never hold an answer to any hole, slot or prediction of any module.
5. A layer's own code holds no live check whose value a later prediction asks for (spec §2.6 rule 6). L1 and L2 hold no tests at all.
6. Every recursive declaration of a layer states its `decreases` explicitly, in both languages.

### 1.2 L1 (tier: survey; first module Q01; imported by Q01–Q18)

**Dafny** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L1_BigNat.dfy`, `module QG_L1_BigNat`, import tier `core`. Definitions and the worked lemma only (Δ51):

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `const BASE: nat := 18446744073709551616` | the literal 2^64 (multiplying by `BASE` stays linear) |
| `predicate Valid(s: seq<nat>)` | every limb is below `BASE` |
| `function ToNat(s: seq<nat>): nat` (`decreases \|s\|`) | Horner abstraction, least significant limb first |
| `function Pow(n: nat): nat` (`decreases n`) | `BASE^n` by repeated multiplication by the literal |
| `function Head(s: seq<nat>): nat` | total head (0 for the empty sequence) |
| `function Tail(s: seq<nat>): seq<nat>` | total tail |
| `function Drop(s: seq<nat>, i: nat): seq<nat>` | suffix from position `i`, empty past the end |
| `lemma ToNatAppendZero(s: seq<nat>) ensures ToNat(s + [0]) == ToNat(s)` (`decreases \|s\|`) | the worked lemma, proved |

**Verus** crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l1` (`Cargo.toml`: `vstd = "=0.0.0-2026-09-20-0158"`, `build = false`, `[package.metadata.verus] verify = true`), `src/lib.rs`, import tier `prelude`:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `pub spec const BASE: nat = 0x1_0000_0000_0000_0000;` | the literal 2^64 |
| `pub open spec fn seq_to_nat(s: Seq<u64>) -> nat` (`decreases s.len()`) | Horner abstraction |
| `pub open spec fn seq_wf(s: Seq<u64>) -> bool` | canonical: no most-significant zero limb |
| `pub struct BigNat { pub limbs: Vec<u64> }` | the exec representation |
| `impl View for BigNat { type V = Seq<u64>; open spec fn view(&self) -> Seq<u64> }` | the spec view |
| `impl BigNat { pub open spec fn to_nat(&self) -> nat; pub open spec fn wf(&self) -> bool }` | value and well-formedness |
| `pub proof fn lemma_seq_to_nat_push_zero(s: Seq<u64>) ensures seq_to_nat(s.push(0)) == seq_to_nat(s)` (`decreases s.len()`) | the worked proof fn, proved |

The two columns differ on purpose (design §10.1 "early surprises survive"): Dafny limbs are `nat` and need `Valid`; Verus limbs are `u64`, and `wf` means canonical. Dafny's canonical form is an L2 definition.

### 1.3 L2 (tier: intro; first module Q02; imported by Q02–Q18)

**Dafny** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L2_BigNat.dfy`, `module QG_L2_BigNat`, `import opened QG_L1_BigNat`, import tier `core`.

*Re-supplied survey-tier helpers* (Δ51 moves them from L1 to L2; all proved, none is a hole anywhere):

| declaration |
|---|
| `lemma ToNatSingleton(d: nat) ensures ToNat([d]) == d` |
| `lemma ToNatCons(d: nat, s: seq<nat>) ensures ToNat([d] + s) == d + BASE * ToNat(s)` |
| `lemma ToNatHeadTail(s: seq<nat>) ensures ToNat(s) == Head(s) + BASE * ToNat(Tail(s))` |
| `lemma ValidTail(s: seq<nat>) requires Valid(s) ensures Valid(Tail(s)) && Head(s) < BASE` |
| `lemma ValidCons(d: nat, s: seq<nat>) requires d < BASE && Valid(s) ensures Valid([d] + s)` |
| `lemma DropStep(s: seq<nat>, i: nat) ensures Head(Drop(s, i)) == (if i < \|s\| then s[i] else 0) && Tail(Drop(s, i)) == Drop(s, i + 1)` |
| `lemma {:induction false} ToNatBound(s: seq<nat>) requires Valid(s) ensures ToNat(s) < Pow(\|s\|)` (`decreases \|s\|`) |

*Intro-tier definitions* (the targets of Q02–Q03 are lemmas and loops **about** these; the definitions themselves are given):

| declaration |
|---|
| `predicate Canonical(s: seq<nat>)` — `Valid(s)` and no most-significant zero limb |
| `function Normalize(s: seq<nat>): seq<nat>` (`decreases \|s\|`) — strip high zero limbs |
| `function FromNat(n: nat): seq<nat>` (`decreases n`) — base-2^64 digits, least significant first |
| `function AddLimb(s: seq<nat>, d: nat): seq<nat>` (`decreases \|s\|`) — add one limb with carry |

**Verus** crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l2` (dependencies `vstd` and `qg_bignat_l1`, `build = false`, `verify = true`), `src/lib.rs` with `pub use qg_bignat_l1::*;`, import tier `prelude`:

| declaration |
|---|
| `pub open spec fn limb_at(s: Seq<u64>, j: int) -> nat` — limb `j`, reading past the end as 0 |
| `pub proof fn lemma_add_limb_lift(a: Seq<u64>, r: Seq<u64>, c: Seq<nat>)` — carry lifting: per-limb relations `limb_at(a, j) + c[j] == r[j] + BASE * c[j + 1]` for every `j < r.len()`, with `a.len() <= r.len()`, `c.len() == r.len() + 1` and `c[r.len()] == 0`, give `seq_to_nat(r) == seq_to_nat(a) + c[0]` (`decreases r.len()`); given for Q03's `add_limb` (Δ52) |

No L1 or L2 crate holds an `external_body`, an `assume` or an `admit`, so the trust allowlist `expected/verus_trust.toml` stays absent and `scripts/vaudit --check-trust` passes on L1–L2 (Δ95); the Dafny layers hold no `{:axiom}`, no abstract module and no test (`vaudit --mode given` reports 0, Δ97). Verus L2 adds no inherent methods to `BigNat` (rustc E0390, Δ54): every L2–L4 target is a free function. Neither L2 crate holds `normalize`, `from_u64` or `add_limb`: those are Q02–Q03 targets, re-supplied by L3 (§1.5).

### 1.4 Who imports what

| layer | tier | declared by (`[module.imports]`) | imported by | Dafny unit tier | Verus tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | survey | Q01 | Q01–Q18 (chain) | core | prelude |
| L2 | intro | Q02 | Q02–Q18 (chain) | core | prelude |

Q01's units reach L1 only; Q02's and Q03's reach L1 and L2. Generated units (Δ78): `dafny/Q/Q0x_<Name>/core/dfyconfig.toml` with the prefix globs `../../../given/Q/QG_L1_*.dfy` (and `QG_L2_*.dfy` for Q02–Q03) and the `[options]` of `dafny/options/core.toml`; `verus/q/q0x_<name>/Cargo.toml` depending on `vstd`, `qg_bignat_l1` (and `qg_bignat_l2`).

### 1.5 What each layer re-supplies

- **L1** re-supplies nothing (it is the first layer).
- **L2** re-supplies the survey tier's spine helpers listed in §1.3 (Δ51). Q01's holes are deliberately **standalone** (§2): no later file needs them, so no layer re-supplies them, and no later module may re-pose them.
- **L3** (DV3a, forward contract) must re-supply, as proved given code, every Q02–Q03 target a later module or layer needs: Dafny `ToNatDropLastZero`, `NormalizeToNat`, `NormalizeCanonical`, `NormalizeLoop`, `FromNatCorrect` (L4 uses it), `FromNatCanonical`, `AddLimbToNat` with the validity half (`AddLimbCorrect`); Verus `lemma_seq_to_nat_drop_last_zero`, `normalize`, `from_u64` (L4 and the extras use it), `add_limb`. By the tier law, L3 may re-supply only what no Q04–Q18 module poses again (see §1.7).

### 1.6 Proof that no layer answers an item of a module that imports it

1. **The layers are listed exhaustively** in §1.2–§1.3. L1 holds definitions and one worked lemma per language; L2 holds definitions, the survey-tier helper lemmas and one Verus lifting lemma.
2. **No layer declaration has the statement of an item.** For every hole, test-out and exit item of Q01–Q03 (§2–§4), no L1 or L2 declaration has the same or a stronger `ensures`.
3. **No layer proof holds an item's whole answer.** No proof in L1 or L2 is, line for line, the answer of a Q01–Q03 item (rule 3: the spine may be open during an exit).
4. **No layer holds code an annotation or exec hole asks for.** L1 and L2 contain no exec function, no method and no loop (the Verus `BigNat` struct and its spec view are data, not code).
5. **Imports bound the exposure.** Q01 imports L1 only; Q02 and Q03 import L1 and L2 (§1.4).

| item(s) | kind of target | same statement in L1 / L2 | layer code answering it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q01.H1d / H1v | exec function | no / not imported | none (no exec code in L1) |
| Q01.H2d / H2v | lemma, two appended zero limbs | no (L1's worked lemma is the one-limb fact) / not imported | none |
| Q01.H3d / H3v | recursive lemma, all-zero limbs | no / not imported | none |
| Q01.X1v, X2d, X7v | spec-and-body items (prompt-as-heading) | no / not imported | none |
| Q01.X3v, X4d | exit items, statement-shipped (statements in the author notes) | no / not imported | none |
| Q01.X5dv–X13v (verdicts) | — | — | none |
| Q02.T1d / T1v | recursive definitions (prompt-as-heading) | no / no | none |
| Q02.H1d / H1v | lemma, one dropped high zero limb | no / no | none |
| Q02.H2d / H2v | loop annotations of normalize | no / no (`Normalize` is a definition; no loop is given) | none |
| Q02.H3d, H4d | lemmas about `Normalize` | no / no (no lemma about `Normalize` is given) | none |
| Q02.H5d / H5v, H6d / H6v | exec loops (zero fill, clear from `k`) | no / no | none |
| Q02.H7v, H8v | Verus lemmas (positive value; `k` zero limbs) | no / no | none |
| Q02.X1d–X3v | exit items | no / no | none |
| Q03.T1d / T1v | exec loop with a ghost witness | no / no | none |

The DV2 integration lane re-runs this table against the layer files as built, and the reference lint keeps the layers frozen.

### 1.7 Forward constraints (for DV3a–DV7)

- **F1.** No module Q02–Q18 poses as a hole, exit or test-out item anything L2 gives: `ToNatSingleton`, `ToNatCons`, `ToNatHeadTail`, `ValidTail`, `ValidCons`, `DropStep`, `ToNatBound`, `lemma_add_limb_lift`. No module Q01–Q18 poses L1's worked lemmas.
- **F2.** Q01's holes and exit targets (`Max2`/`max2`, `ToNatAppendTwoZeros`/`lemma_append_two_zeros`, `AllZeroToNat`/`lemma_all_zero`, and the exit targets the author notes name) are Q01-owned: never re-supplied, never re-posed, and no later learner-visible file contains their proofs.
- **F3.** Whatever L3–L7 re-supply from Q02–Q03 cannot be re-posed in a module that imports that layer. The DV0 "planned homes" re-pose three Q02–Q03 targets in practice modules (Dafny `NormalizeLoop` and `FromNatCorrect` as Q10 pairs; Verus `normalize` as Q10's invariant pair; the `normalize` helper as a Q10 helper). Either those targets stay out of L3–L5 (re-supplied only from L6 on, or kept module-local in Solutions) or Q10 poses fresh twins. DV3a decides with DV5 (§6 Q1).
- **F4.** Q02 and Q03 are one tier: neither module's files may hold the other's answers. Q03's given code (the `add_limb` loop body and ghost updates, the `Limb` subset type, the `Opt` datatype, the worked examples) answers no Q02 item, and Q02's given code (the `NormalizeLoop` and `normalize` loop code, the first-zero worked examples) answers no Q03 item. Q03's exit items share no code with Q02's worked examples (the author notes record the check).

- Checked uses per slug and language (≥ 2): ensures d H1d, X2d · v H1v, X1v, X7v; assert d H2d, X4d · v H2v, X3v; lemma-call d H2d, H3d, X4d · v H2v, H3v, X3v. RECOGNISE uses: requires v X1v; ghost v X7v.

### 2.4 Agentic file (rung 1: repairer + three doors)

- **Shape:** planted-error repair (spec §3.11.1). Each file is RED with exactly **4** documented errors, one diagnostic each, each in its own declaration under a neutral heading `### Bug 1` … `### Bug 4`; doc comments state the intended behaviour; every fix is one line.
- **CONTRACT** (line 1, three keys, spec §3.11.2): `goal:` make the file verify by fixing the four bugs; `allowed-files:` the one agent file of that run; `acceptance:` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line · `git diff` shows only body lines (no `requires`/`ensures`/`decreases`/doc line changed) · every fix is one line · re-verify after every edit, never stacked · zero holes · `scripts/vaudit --compare expected/Q01_AgentRepair.audit <file>` (Verus: `expected/q01_hello_agent.audit`) reports no difference.
- **Learner follow-up (the exercise, 25 min with the three doors):** run the file first and read every message; run `/contract Q01` in a fresh session; tally the transcript (re-ran after every edit? touched a spec line? reached for an escape hatch?); check `git diff` and the audit yourself. **Three doors:** in a fresh study session, ask the AI for the Q01 reference three ways (a file read, a shell command, a language-server lookup) and record which guard layer refuses each (layer-1 deny rule or hook rule, with its id) in the `THREE DOORS` comment block at the end of the Dafny agent file (learner-owned prose).

- `recognise_d = recognise_v = ["old", "exists"]`.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): assert-by d H1d, X3d · v H1v, X3v; invariant d H2d, H5d, H6d · v H2v, H5v, H6v, X1v; decreases d T1d, H5d, H6d, X1d · v T1v, H5v, H6v, X1v; quantified-ensures d H5d, H6d, X2d · v H5v, H6v, X1v; frame d H6d, X2d.

### 3.4 Agentic file (rung 2: implementer vs checks, spec-first)

- **Shape:** spec-first (spec §3.11.1), over transparent definitions so that checks can be static (when the file was built no course command ran tests; `scripts/vcheck --test` exists since the DV2a fix round, and this file keeps its static checks, §6 Q3). Dafny: three compiled `function`s with prose doc contracts, each a bodyless function with one partial `ensures` (W-D02); Verus: three `uninterp spec fn`s (W-V08) shipped with their `decreases` clause and prose contracts, plus one bound `proof fn` hole per definition stating the same partial contract. A shipped check suite of six static checks, each in its own lemma / proof fn (Dafny `assert` lemmas; Verus `assert(…) by (compute_only)` proof fns), two per definition, none implied by the partial formal contract, all failing over the holes.
- **The three definitions** (the same in both languages, over every limb sequence: any `seq<nat>` in Dafny, any `Seq<u64>` in Verus): the number of zero limbs; the index of the most significant non-zero limb, or −1 when there is none; the sum of the limbs (not the value). The prose contracts are stronger than the formal `ensures` and the shipped checks.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` define the three functions so every shipped check holds · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` per edit only the fast channel `scripts/vcheck --symbol <Module.Name> <file>` (Verus `scripts/vcheck --symbol <fn> <file>`, as the shipped CONTRACT block writes it); exactly one full `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` run, last, exit 0, summary line only · zero holes · `git diff` shows only the bodies of the three definitions and of the bound lemmas (Verus: `uninterp spec fn … decreases …;` becomes `open spec fn … decreases … { … }`; no other line) · `scripts/vaudit` reports no finding.
- **Verus checks report one at a time.** The Verus agent file shows one failing `by (compute_only)` check at a time, and the fast channel cannot isolate a check. The Verus agent header documents this limit of the pinned Verus (DV2a fix round), and the chapter (DV2b) repeats it.
- **Learner follow-up (15 min):** read the contracts; run the contract; fill the `LATENCY LOG` comment block (fast-channel vs full-gate wall times, from the transcript); then write adversarial checks in the trailing `ADVERSARIAL CHECKS` block, cases the shipped suite never exercised, judged against the prose contract.

Before running any instrument on it, name its species and the instrument that exposes it. Answer form `<species> <instrument>`. Species (each specimen's docstring repeats these definitions): `hole` — it holds an unfinished placeholder that the course's hole counter reports; `trusted` — it rests on an assumption or axiom that no hole counter reports; `unchecked` — part of it was never verified (skipped, filtered, outside the verified build); `vacuous` — its statement is proved but cannot apply (contradictory assumptions); `weak` — verified and honest, but the statement says too little to be useful; `healthy` — none of these. **Marker-level check.** Marker-only stubs of every item above (ids, strands, formats, `uses:`, `need:`, `pair:`, slots, the card slugs as listed), at these paths in a scratch copy of the registry with the §2.6, §3.6 and §4.6 fields applied, pass `check_course_map.py --only registry` and `--only coverage` (slug counts, `dv` counts, exit floors, pairs, card slugs, D4). The full checker on the real files is the integration lane's.

**Ordering and naming rule for cards.** A divergence or false-friends card sits in its file's final section, under a heading that tells the learner to record the module's predictions first (Q01.C4dv, Q02.C6v, Q02.C7dv, Q03.C6dv, Q03.C7dv, Q03.C8dv). Its slug and its demo files are named by item id, never by topic, and its demos state no outcome in prose (the outcome lives in the `.expect` sidecar). A demo whose output settles a prediction, a WRITE-card failure demo included, also carries `// after: <item id>`, and the tools refuse it until that prediction's first try is recorded (DV2a fix round). WRITE cards and worked examples sit after the prediction block.

## 6. Open questions (for the orchestrator and later phases)

*Each item ends with its resolution from the DV2a fix round (2026-10-04): resolved, or deferred to its owner, with a pointer. The DELTAS entries hold the full decisions.*

1. **Re-posed targets (F3).** DV0's planned homes re-pose `NormalizeLoop`, `FromNatCorrect` and Verus `normalize` as Q10 pairs; by the tier law they cannot also be re-supplied in L3–L5. DV3a and DV5 decide which. **Deferred** to DV3a and DV5 (Δ119): F3's two options (§1.7) stand until DV3a decides with DV5. **Decided** for DV3a's part (DV3a, 2026-10-05; Δ135): the DV3a plan took F3's first option for the normalize targets and its second for `FromNatCorrect` (§8.5): Dafny `NormalizeLoop` and Verus `normalize` stay out of L3–L5, `FromNatCorrect` is in L3, and Q10's round-trip pair takes a fresh Dafny twin. DV5 confirms with Q14.
2. **Q03's registry record.** `agentic = []` and `engines = []` (this plan) replace the two agent paths DV1a listed, so that `/contract Q03` refuses through the registry. If the orchestrator prefers a refusal that names contract v0, the contract skill needs a Q03 rule instead. **Resolved** (Δ114): both. Q03 keeps `agentic = []` and `engines = []`, and the contract skill's Step 0 item 4 refuses `/contract Q03` with a line that names contract v0, written by hand below the learner marker in `CLAUDE.md`.
3. **No course command runs tests.** `dvl.dafny.test` and `dvl.verus.test` exist, but `scripts/vcheck` has no test mode, so a learner or contractor cannot run `@Test` / `#[test]` checks (value slots run only through `firsttry`/`pins.py`). Q02's agent file therefore uses static checks over transparent definitions. A `vcheck --test` would allow executable checks. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ119). Until a test mode exists, Q02's agent file keeps its static checks. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): `scripts/vcheck --test [--json] [--name N …] <unit | crate | file | ID [d\|v]>` runs the compiled checks and prints PASSED/FAILED lines. Q02's agent files keep their static checks; a later agent file may use executable checks.
4. **Code T items have no first-try recorder (tools owner).** `firsttry --check` records only T items that carry a slot, and `--report --strict` does not treat an empty prompt-as-heading T block as unfilled. Q02's T blocks ship a commented check, so the strict gate fails until the learner writes the target and uncomments the check; Q03's T items are holes, so the done gate fails until they are filled. The *first run* is still the learner's own note in the CALIBRATION block (honour system), so "skip a section only if every first run is correct" rests on that note until `firsttry` records code T items. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open; Δ119). Until then the CALIBRATION note is the first-run record. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): `firsttry --check` records a code T item's first try (§0's T row), `--report` shows each test-out's state, and `--report --strict` fails on an unattempted or unrecorded test-out. The Q02 and Q03 T prompts and file headers use the recorder, and Q02's CALIBRATION block no longer carries a T line.
5. **Empty-body gate cost on Verus `Seq` goals.** The `BV` mode spins on Verus proof-fn candidates whose goal mentions `seq_to_nat` (Δ58's front-end hang) and is killed by the wall clock; at the default 1800 s net and three seeds this is about 1.5 h per hole in `scripts/ci.sh --job empty-body`. The tools owner should bound `BV` (a short per-mode wall clock, or skip `BV` when the goal mentions a `Seq`). **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open, CI wall time; Δ119). **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): `scripts/dvl/pins.toml` bounds every `BV` variant job at 120 s (`eb_mode_timeout_s`), and a kill counts as the variant failing (Δ58). §7.7 gives the job's wall time.
6. **Annotation holes and the gate (Δ98; tools owner, DV3a).** `dvl.dafny.empty_body` empties the whole body, so for an annotation hole (Q02.H2d) it tests only that the method is not vacuous. The twins of Q02.H2d, Q02.H2v and Q03.H1v carry `@hole-begin <id>` / `@hole-end <id>` markers around exactly the regions the learner writes; a region mode of the gate should honour them. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open; Δ119). The twins keep their markers for that mode. Still open after the fix round. **Decided** (DV3a, Δ135): built in DV3a with Q18's region mode, which honours these markers. Open until the tools lane's build is recorded.
7. **Statement freeze of a Verus spec-fn hole.** `specfreeze` pins a shipped `uninterp spec fn` without its body and without `uninterp`, but a filled `open spec fn` still hashes differently, so a learner who runs `specfreeze --check Q02` after the Q02 contract would see a false `freeze-changed` on the agent file. The comparison should also drop `open`/`closed` for an open-body symbol (tools owner). CI's `specfreeze --check` runs on the shipped files and is unaffected. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open; Δ119). **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): for a Verus symbol listed in `open_bodies`, `specfreeze` compares the statement without `uninterp`, `open` and `closed`; no existing freeze pin changed.
8. **The stated hole count (Δ104).** This plan fixes each file's shipped hole count, exit files included (§2.1, §3.1, §4.1). Where that count is pinned against the tool count (a chapter fence or a registry field) is the DV2 integration lane's call. **Resolved** (Δ115): each file's shipped hole count is pinned in `expected/<ID>.counts` beside the verified counts, and the tools check it. The chapters (DV2b) quote the pinned number. §7.5 gives the current counts. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): `expected/<ID>.counts` schema 2, written by `vcheck --ref --pin-counts <ID>`, compared by `vcheck --shipped <ID>` (`scripts/ci.sh`'s verify jobs; it checks the course as shipped, so it fails once a file is worked on) and checked for structure by `pins.py --ci`. Q01–Q03 are re-pinned.
9. **The Verus E0 variant of a ghost-carrying exec fn (tools owner).** On Q03.H1v as shipped, the E0, E0H and E1 variants do not reach verification (`cannot find value c in this scope`): the variant strips `let ghost mut c` but keeps two `proof { }` blocks that use it. The gate's verdict is therefore `error`, and `firsttry.sh Q03 --empty-body` cannot pass until `dvl.verus` strips proof blocks consistently. The INV variant (the decisive one for an invariant-only hole) fails as required. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open; Δ119). Q03.H1v's reorder (§7.4) remains the workaround. **Fixed** in the fix round (Δ120): `dvl.verus_spec` ends a block-like statement at its closing brace, so a `proof { }` block after an `if … else …` is a statement of its own and the variants strip it with the ghost `let` it uses. Q03.H1v keeps its harmless reorder; on the shipped form E0, E0H, E1 and INV all reach verification and fail.
10. **Card demos and H5 (tools owner).** `vcheck` refuses a specimen whose slots have no recorded first try, but a card demo has no slot, so a learner can run a divergence demo before recording the prediction it settles. A marker such as `// after: <item id>` in the demo, honoured like H5, would close this; until then the demo's header line and the final-section rule are the guard. **Deferred** to the tools owner (Δ113 Open; Δ119). **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): a specimen's `// after: <item id>` lines make `vcheck`, `vaudit` and `vcheck --test` refuse it until each named item's first try is recorded, and `pins.py --update` seals its sidecar. Q01, Q02 and Q03 mark their demos (§2.1, §3.1, §4.1). A sealed sidecar still shows its normalized diagnostics, so `expected/` stays closed until the predictions are committed (§7.7).
11. **Exit thresholds outside Q01–Q15 (Δ102; orchestrator).** `firsttry` pins no threshold for the practice tiers (Q10–Q18) or for R06 and R11, and prints the score with exit 0 there. DV2 owns the decision; it must be made before DV5 arms Q10 and before R06 is armed. **Resolved** (Δ116). The thresholds are:
    - Q10–Q12: at least 1 of the 2 timed items;
    - Q13–Q15: the 1 timed item;
    - Q16–Q18: no numeric threshold; the exit is the verifier part plus the reviews;
    - R06 and R11: at least 3 of 4, with at least 1 in each language.

    `firsttry`'s threshold table is the tools owner's to build before DV5 arms Q10. **Built** in the fix round (Δ120): `firsttry`'s `EXIT_TIERS` and `EXIT_MODULES`; for Q16–Q18 `--exit` names the verifier part plus the reviews instead of a threshold.
12. **`dafny/options/core.toml` comment (integration lane).** The comment above the `Q03-Q18` override says more than the option it documents; it should read only "Manual lemma induction from Q03 on (Δ45)". **Resolved** (Δ119): the comment now reads only that sentence.

*Q13–Q25 were added by the DV3a plan (2026-10-05, revised after review). Q15, Q16, Q19, Q21 and Q22 blocked a module lane's build or arming, as each says. Each item now ends with the orchestrator's disposition, written by the DV3a records lane (2026-10-05): resolved, decided and built in DV3a, or deferred to its owner. DELTAS Δ130–Δ135 hold the decisions.*

13. **Std tier for Dafny files outside a unit (tools owner).** `dvl.dafny` builds an exit, check or agent file as std tier only when the file itself imports a `Std` module; a file that reaches L3 without one fails at stage `resolve` with "module QG_L3_BigNat does not exist", because L3 imports `Std.Arithmetic.Power`. The builder should take the tier from the file's `[module.imports]` entry. Until then every such file of Q04–Q18 imports one `Std` module itself (§8.0). **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane, before Q04 is armed. Until the build is recorded, §8.0's import workaround stands.
14. **F3 decided; `CanonicalUnique` and `cmp` homed (DV5 to confirm).** §8.5: Dafny `NormalizeLoop` and Verus `normalize` stay out of L3–L5; `FromNatCorrect` and `from_u64` are in L3, so Q10's round-trip pair needs a fresh Dafny twin. §8.7 F10: `CanonicalUnique` is Q06's, so Q13's uniqueness bundle (Δ53) changes. §8.7 F6: once L4 re-supplies Verus `cmp`, design §7.5's Q16 H2 ("`cmp` with invented invariant") poses DV0's non-canonical `cmp_any` variant instead (Δ53 measured it at 39 lines). DV5 confirms or re-plans Q10, Q13 and Q16; this closes §6 Q1 for DV3a's part. **Deferred** to DV5 (Δ135): DV5 confirms F3's decision, `CanonicalUnique`'s home and Q16 H2's switch to `cmp_any` when it plans Q10, Q13 and Q16.
15. **§1.1 rule 3's last sentence and the leak patterns' rule 2 (orchestrator; blocks L3).** Literally, §1.1 rule 3 says that no layer, of any tier, holds the whole answer of an item of Q01–Q03, and the leak-pattern README's rule 2 counts every given layer as public. §1.5's forward contract and Δ90 ("later layers re-supply earlier targets") require L3 to hold the proofs of Q02.H1d, H3d, H4d, H1v and Q03.H1d, H2d, H1v, H2v, and three existing Q02–Q03 leak patterns match L3's text (the author notes list them). Proposed decision, as a DELTAS entry before L3 is written: (a) §1.1 rule 3 forbids a layer to hold the answer of an **exit or test-out** item of Q01–Q03, and the answer of any item of a module that **imports** the layer; (b) the leak-pattern verifier's rule 2 exempts the given layers above the pattern's module's layer (consistent with Δ90's instructor rule); then the verifier is re-run. The alternative is that L3 drops its five Dafny and three Verus re-supplied proofs and every later layer and module proves them again. **Resolved** (Δ130): L3 keeps its re-supplied proofs. Rule 3 of §1.1 now forbids a layer to hold the answer of a Q01–Q03 exit or test-out item, or of any item of a module that imports it. The leak patterns' rule 2 exempts a given layer of a tier above the pattern's module, and a leak check of an instructor session for module M applies only the patterns of modules at M's tier or higher (lower-tier answers are re-supplied, public by design). The leak-pattern README and its verifier, the calibration harness and the simulation scan follow (Δ130, Open).
16. **`vstd::calc` and the Verus import tiers (tools owner; blocks Q04's Verus tiers).** `calc!` is a root-level macro of `vstd` (`use vstd::calc;`, defined in `vstd::calc_macro`), and `check_course_map.py`'s Verus tiers admit no root-level `vstd` item below `all`: `uses vstd::calc but its tier is prelude`. Admit `calc` and `calc_macro` in the `prelude` tier (a macro, not a lemma); until then Q04's `lib.rs`, `library.rs` and `exit.rs` are declared `all`, and Q04's library staging rests on the files as written (§9.3). **Resolved** (Δ131): `vstd::calc` and `vstd::calc_macro` are admitted in the `prelude` tier (a macro, not a lemma). The tools lane builds it in `check_course_map.py`; Q04's files then keep §9.7's tiers.
17. **Recorded runs for the chapter (Q04 chapter lane).** Of §9.5's mismatch lab, the plan captured, through the course's capped job runner, the default Dafny run, the Dafny run with the other bundled Z3, and the Verus runs with the pinned and with the default `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN`; `VERSIONS.md` §1 records the `VERUS_Z3_PATH` run. The `--solver-path` run on a path that does not exist is refused by the guard in a Claude session (rule 2g), so the chapter lane quotes it as a recorded run captured in a plain terminal (Δ121's recorded quotes). **Deferred** to the Q04 chapter lane (Δ135), which captures the run in a plain terminal and quotes it as a recorded run. **Built** (Δ142): the chapter quotes it, with the other lab runs no entry can express, as recorded runs.
18. **The empty-body gate and region holes (tools owner).** `dvl.dafny.empty_body` returns `error` on an item whose hole sits in a `function … by method` body (Q05.H3d, Q05.X1d); the plan's evidence for both is a bare probe (the by-method body without the learner's part fails its postcondition). Dafny annotation and code holes (Q05.H1d, H2d, H3d, H4d, H5d) are still gated by emptying the whole body (§6 Q6), and Q04.H4v's two regions by the Verus modes on the shipped form. A region mode honouring `@hole-begin`/`@hole-end`, and by-method bodies, would close both. **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane (with Q6), before Q05 is armed.
19. **The Python adapter and the `fvh` core (DV4).** §11.8 lists what Q06 needs: the `harness/` project, `fvh.stats`, `fvh.records`, `fvh.holes`, the Python branches of `vcheck`, `firsttry` and `ci.sh`, the `.gitignore` entries, and the Python file in `expected/Q06.counts`. Q06 cannot be armed before they exist. **Decided** (Δ134): the `fvh` core and the Python adapter of §11.8 are built in DV3a, before Q06 is armed; DV4 keeps the rest of the core.
20. **Property-test suites in the done gate (tools owner, low).** Q06's checkers have two-sided postconditions, so the verifier alone rejects a wrong or constant checker and `firsttry --exit` scores X1d and X1v soundly. The suites still belong to "done" for H4d and H4v: `firsttry --report --strict` counts a commented suite as unfinished but runs no test; it should run `scripts/vcheck --test` on a unit whose items use `property-test`. Until then the test run is the learner's own step, stated in each block. **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane.
21. **Resource attributes at the exit and in the strict report (tools owner; before Q06 is armed).** A budget item asks for a proof at the pinned limit with no resource attribute, but `firsttry --exit` and `--report --strict` run no audit, so `{:rlimit N}`, `{:resource_limit N}` or `#[verifier::rlimit(N)]` would pass them. Both modes should run `vaudit`'s `resource-attribute` and `rlimit-inflation` lint on every block whose `uses:` names `rlimit` and fail the item on a finding. **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane, before Q06 is armed.
22. **The statement freeze of a frame annotation hole (tools owner; before Q05 is armed).** Q05.H2d's learner adds a `modifies` clause and an `ensures` clause to `Pop`, so `specfreeze --check Q05` reports `freeze-changed` on a correct answer, and a rubric gate line on `specfreeze --check` (as Q03's A5) would fail it. Proposed: a pin field `open_frames` for such symbols, under which `specfreeze` compares the signature, `requires`, `reads` and `decreases` exactly, requires every shipped `ensures` clause verbatim and in order, and accepts added `modifies` and `ensures` clauses. **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane, before Q05 is armed; the tools lane records the pin field as built.
23. **Predictions before test-out items (tools owner, medium).** The moderate tier puts the prediction block before the T block (§8.0), because a T item can settle a prediction of its module (Q05's and Q06's T items are about measures and triggers). `firsttry --check` should refuse to record a T item's first try until every P item of the module has a recorded first try; until then the file headers and the T blocks say so, and the honour system holds. **Decided** (Δ135): built in DV3a by the tools lane. Until the build is recorded, the file headers and the T blocks carry the rule.
24. **Predicted audit rows (orchestrator, low).** Spec §2.3.3's moderate row lists "predicted audit rows confirmed" as a tier-exit check; §11.7 keeps the rows advisory (the viva opens on a row that did not hold). Record the deviation in a DELTAS entry, or make `firsttry --exit` require the rows for Q06. **Resolved** (Δ132): the rows stay advisory, and `firsttry --exit` does not require them. Spec §3.10 already lists them among the advisory tier-gate checks.
25. **Exit statements in this plan (orchestrator, low).** The exit tables of §9.2, §10.2 and §11.2 (and §3.2, §4.2) give each module's unseen exit statements to a learner who reads this plan after committing the predictions and before the exit. §8 carries a banner; moving the exit statements to the author notes, with this plan naming only each item's id, strand, uses and form, would remove the exposure. **Resolved** (Δ133) and done by the DV3a records lane: every exit table of §2.2–§4.2 and §9.2–§11.2 now names each item by its id, form, strand, language, file and minutes. The statements, prompts, pairs and `uses:` and `need:` lines moved verbatim to the author notes (`Q01-Q03-author-notes.md` §2.9, §3.6, §4.5; `Q04-Q06-author-notes.md` §2.8, §3.5, §4.7), together with the other lines of this plan that described an exit statement (§1.6, §1.7, §4.1, §5.1, §8.0, §8.6, §8.7, §10.1, §11.1).

*Q26–Q42 were added by the DV3b plan (2026-10-06), for the orchestrator's decisions before the detailed tier is built, as Q13–Q25 were for DV3a; Q43–Q46 by its revision after the plan review the same day, which also rewrote Q31, Q33–Q35, Q40 and Q42. Each names its owner; those that block a build or an arming say so. Each item now ends with the orchestrator's disposition, written by the DV3b records lane (2026-10-06): resolved by a DELTAS entry, assigned to the lane that builds it, or deferred, with a pointer. Δ145–Δ158 hold the decisions, and Δ159 lists every item and which of `docs/design/DV4-PLAN.md` §14's proposals the orchestrator adopted.*

26. **Q07's WRITE and RECOGNISE lists (orchestrator; blocks Q07's registry record).** Design §7.4 and the DV1a record list Dafny `isolate` and Verus `recommends` as Q07 WRITE constructs. The plan's probes found that neither yields the two checked hole uses a WRITE construct needs on the pinned toolchain (the evidence is in the author notes §0.3; it bears on two Q07 predictions, so this plan does not describe it). Proposed (§15.3): `write_d = ["fuel", "arithmetic-library"]`, `write_v = ["fuel", "arithmetic-library", "bit-vector"]`, `recognise_d = ["isolate", "bv-types"]`, `recognise_v = ["recommends"]`, recorded as a DELTAS entry that amends design §7.4's Q07 row. **Resolved** (Δ157): the lists are built as proposed, and they amend design §7.4's Q07 row.
27. **L4's contents and what they cost Q16 (orchestrator and DV5).** §14.2–§14.3 put addition and multiplication by one limb, with their proofs, into L4, as design §10.1's L4 row says ("`Add` with proof; `MulLimb`; …"). By the tier law (§1.1 rule 3) design §7.5's Q16 H1 ("generalize before induction via the add carry") and Q16 H3 (`mul_limb`, with a Dafny mirror) can then no longer be posed (§14.7 F12). The alternative, keeping both proofs out of L4–L6 as §8.5 did for `normalize`, would leave L4 with definitions only and move the detailed tier's spine targets into Q07–Q09 holes, at the cost of hole minutes the mechanism items need. Proposed: adopt §14's L4, and DV5 re-plans Q16 H1 and H3 with Q16 H2 (§6 Q14), with full multiplication (Δ50) as the first candidate (§14.7 F13). **Resolved** (Δ158): §14's L4 is built as planned. DV5 re-plans Q16 H1 and H3 with Q16 H2 (Q14) and confirms F11–F16 (Q39).
28. **The name `big_add` (records; low).** Design §10.1 calls L4's Verus addition `add`; L4 names it `big_add` (§14.1 rule 8; the reason is in the author notes §1). Record the rename with Q26's entry. **Resolved** (Δ157).
29. **The solver swap and the brittleness flags (tools owner; blocks Q09's arming).** §17.5 needs `scripts/vcheck --solver <4.12.1|4.14.1>` and `scripts/vcheck --brittleness --solvers` for Dafny: the alternative solver is the other Z3 bundled with Dafny 4.11.0, pinned by name and hash in `scripts/dvl/pins.toml` beside the default (`VERSIONS.md` §1.1); both options are refused under `--gate`, which keeps the pin (Δ10); seeded runs keep `measure-complexity --random-seed S --mutations 1` (Δ17). Also: `--brittleness` flags (never gates) a symbol whose resource spread exceeds 20%; and `vcheck --ref --brittleness` covers the reference twins (DV0-REPORT §8 item 37). The guard needs no change: no `--solver-path` appears on the shell line. Proposed: built by the DV3b tools lane before Q09 is armed, with tests on throwaway roots. The `stable` form of Q43 runs on this option. **Assigned** (Δ159): the DV3b tools lane builds it as proposed before Q09 is armed, with the `stable` form of Q43, and records the options as built.
30. **Notes in `vcheck`'s text output (tools owner; low).** For Verus, `scripts/vcheck --json` carries some notes at severity `info` that the text output does not print; Q07's `recognise:recommends` card needs one of them. Until the text output prints them, the card and the chapter use `--json`. **Assigned** (Δ159) to the tools owner (low); until it is built, the card and the chapter use `--json`.
31. **What the Qwen replay needs from DV4, and when Q07 arms (orchestrator and DV4).** The replay (A2d, §15.4) is `scripts/contract_local.sh Q07 --file <agent file>` as `docs/design/DV4-PLAN.md` §6 plans it: a per-run agent container and the network-less verification container (`scripts/mksandbox-run`, DV4 §5), the agent container reaching the model only through DV4's HTTP-aware Ollama gate (endpoint and model allowlists, the run's options pinned, its calls, tokens and GPU time counted; DV4 §5.6.3), never through a plain TCP forward of `127.0.0.1:11434`; the allowed files staged from the commit that last changed the snapshot the CONTRACT names in its `specfreeze --against` line, never from the learner's tree after the `/contract` run (DV4 §6 step 3); `scripts/specfreeze` among the trusted tools and the snapshot read-only at its own path, so the agent can run its own acceptance; the acceptance lines in DV4 §6's checked forms, with the `git diff` line an audit line whose evidence is `patch.diff`; the patch and the run record under `research/runs/Q07/`, never applied (N2); the `fvh` ledger enforcing the registry's `budget` (Q07: `qwen_runs = 1`, `qwen_calls = 40`, `gpu_hours = 1`, §15.7). The route to the gate is Route B, the plan of record (DV4 §5.6.2, Δ146; subject to DV4's step 2c probe): the agent container has no network either, and the gate listens on a Unix socket in the run's state directory, which a relay inside the container forwards to; Route A, a per-run internal network whose gateway a filter closes, is used only if Route B fails its probe. Docker access is a report-only check (DV4 §5.1, Δ153): `scripts/mksandbox-run` calls plain `docker`, and when `docker version` is refused it prints a diagnosis read from `id` and `getent` and exits 3, never escalating; `contract_local.sh --available` reports through the same check; no course script calls `sudo` or `sg`. Re-adding the `docker` group is the user's host action (Δ60; DV4 host action H1, not done on 2026-10-06). **Q07 arms with A2d marked pending** (DV4 §13): its chapter says that the replay needs `scripts/contract_local.sh` (DV4 build step 3b) and how to ask `contract_local.sh --available` whether it can run; rung 7's `/contract` stands without it, so Q07 waits on no host action. **Decided** (Δ156; the route Δ146, the access check Δ153): as stated; A2d stays pending until DV4's steps 2–3b, which wait on host action H1. The DV3b records lane aligned this item's wording with DV4-PLAN §5.1 and §5.6.
32. **The cheat corpus and the forensics (records and README owner; before Q09 ships).** `tests/corpus/` is learner-readable, and its manifests record which instrument flags which construct; Q09's recorded gate fixtures will do the same. Both join the README's *Predictions first* list from Q09 on, until the forensic first tries are recorded (§17.6). **Assigned** (Δ159) to the owner of `README.md`'s *Predictions first* list, before Q09 ships (DV4-PLAN §14 Q10 asks the same).
33. **What the gate core builds on (DV4 and the Q09 lane; blocks Q09's arming).** **Assigned to DV4, build step 1a** (`docs/design/DV4-PLAN.md` §3.7, §11, §13, §14 P1), as frozen core (rule 2f) with core tests, without changing any §11.8 signature (Δ134): `fvh.verdicts` (`PASS`, `FAIL`, `UNKNOWN`, `GATE_WORDS`, `XCLASSES`, `GRADER_ONLY`), `fvh.corpus` (`load_half(lang, half)`; each fixture a `Delivery` and a `Label` kept apart), and `fvh.tools` (`INSTRUMENTS`, `ToolResult`, the `Runner` interface with `HostRunner`, `ContainerRunner` and `ReplayRunner`, `run`, `replay`, `ProvenanceError`, and the author-only recorder self-gated as `test_corpus.py --update` is, Δ103). `fvh.tools.run` shares its runner interface with DV4's executor, and an instrument on a target of `agent` provenance always runs in the verification container, so Q18's gate v2 and the lab gates, written on the same interface, never run an instrument on agent output on the host. The Q09 lane records the Q-half fixtures with the recorder and writes `test_q09_gate.py` (§17.7). The DELTAS entry that records the assignment is the records lane's (§6 Q46). Q09 arms only after step 1a and the Q09 lane's recording. **Resolved** (Δ145): DV4-PLAN §14 P1 adopted; Q09 arms after step 1a is built and the Q09 lane has recorded the fixtures.
34. **The gated permission items (Q08 lane).** §16.3: H5v and H6v ship with `admitted = false` and 0 minutes (§16.7). The module lane runs the admissibility gate on them (reference, TRIV, seeds 1–3, the pinned `vstd::cell` API), records the four results, and only then writes `admitted = true` with their minutes; on a failure it applies the fallbacks F-H5v and F-H6v and moves `tracked` to `recognise_v`. The plan's probes passed the reference and TRIV checks. **Assigned** (Δ159) to the Q08 lane, as stated.

DV5 checks that figure against `fvh.stats.mde_two_proportions` when it writes Q12's pre-registration template (the computation is in the author notes §4.1). **Deferred** to DV5 (Δ159).

37. **Interactive allowlist approvals (records; low).** As Q06's chapter says (Δ127, Δ142), Claude Code asks to approve each write of `.claude/state/contract-allowlist`; Q07–Q09's contract runs are interactive, so the chapters repeat that sentence. No headless `/contract` run is planned in Q07–Q09. **No decision needed** (Δ159): the Q07–Q09 chapter lanes repeat the sentence.
38. **Vocabulary for the banks (concepts owner; before the kits).** The Q07–Q09 banks need `concepts.md` tags for fuel and unfolding, the arithmetic library, bit-vectors, porting and fidelity, refinement, trait specifications, permissions, the verified wp, brittleness, trigger rewriting, forensics and gates. The kit lanes add the missing rows and retag, as Δ143's Open asked for error propagation. **Assigned** (Δ159) to the concepts owner, before the Q07–Q09 kits.
39. **The F-constraints of §14.7 (DV5, DV7).** F11–F16 bind the practice tiers and the challenges: confirm them when Q10–Q18 are planned (with §8.7's F5–F10). **Deferred** to DV5 and DV7 (Δ158, Δ159).
40. **The refinement holes and the statement holes through the tools (tools owner; before Q08 is armed).** **Form decided** (§14.0): a refined lemma that is a hole ships with the body `{ assume false; }`, the course's Dafny assume hole, not bodyless. The review found that a bodyless refined lemma breaks the tools as built (the learner-mode lint reports it as `bodyless-silent`, `firsttry`'s static hole finder sees no hole form, and `vcheck` reports the hole only at the given layer's line, so an exit block holding it would score as passed with no work done). The `assume false` body was probed on the pins with the tools as built, two refiners of `QG_L4_Spec` in one learner file at a learner exit path (the author notes §R.1): `vcheck` counts one `assume` hole per refined lemma at the learner's line, labelled with its item id, and none at the given layer; the learner-mode lint is clean; `vaudit` reports one `audit-assume` row per hole; `firsttry`'s static hole finder finds both; a dry exit run scores both blocks `unattempted` as shipped and `pass` with the references; `dvl.dafny.empty_body` fails both on maxauto and seeds 1–3. Nothing needs building. The tools lane confirms on the built files: `firsttry Q08 --lint-hollow`, `--check`, `--exit` (a dry run on the shipped exit and on the twins), `--report --strict`, `--empty-body` on H1d, H2d, H7d and X1d, and `vcheck --shipped Q08` (the per-file hole counts of `expected/Q08.counts`). The statement holes of §14.0 (T1v, H3v) stay skipped by `--empty-body`: their hole is the labelled assume in a given client, which `vcheck`, the strict report and `--exit` already count. **Adopted** (Δ154). The confirmation on the built files stays open until the lane that runs it records the results.
41. **The explain-back (skills owner; before Q09's viva).** `viva/SKILL.md` says nothing about opening a tier viva on learner prose; Q06's bank states the `AUDIT ROWS` opening (Δ143, Open). Q09's bank states the `EXPLAIN-BACK` opening the same way, and the skill gains one line for both. **Assigned** (Δ159) to the skills owner, before Q09's viva.
42. **Q09's second Dafny exercise file (orchestrator; low).** §17.1 gives T1d a file of its own, `dafny/Q/Q09_InternalsIII/std/Q09_Triggers.dfy`, whose module options differ from the main Dafny file's (§14.0), for a reason in the author notes. The registry's `exercises` and `[module.imports]` list the file (§17.11); `units` and the header counts are unchanged. Proposed: record it with Q26's entry. **Resolved** (Δ157).
43. **Per-item construct forms (tools owner; before Q07 is armed).** The review found checked uses whose construct a correct answer can avoid at the pin, so the tools could not tell a use from a non-use (the author notes §0.2 list each construct-free answer that verifies). Proposed: an optional `// form: <rule>[, <rule>]` marker line in an H, T or X block, with a closed vocabulary — `bv-exec` (Verus: no `*`, `/` or `%` in the item's exec code, outside `proof { }` blocks, `assert … by (…)` statements and spec clauses); `no-nl` (Verus: no `nonlinear_arith` in the block); `nl-steps:2` (Verus: at least two `assert … by (nonlinear_arith)` steps in the block, each with its own `requires`); `trigger` (an explicit trigger, `{:trigger …}`, `#[trigger]` or `#![trigger …]`, on a quantifier in the block); `stable` (the block's file verifies under `scripts/vcheck --brittleness`, Dafny with `--solvers`, §6 Q29). `firsttry` checks a block's forms at `--check` (a test-out's first try records as a fail when a form fails), at `--exit` (`judge_block` adds a reason) and at `--report --strict` (a hole stays unfinished), as Q21's resource-attribute check does. A twin block may carry `// cfree: { … }` lines (as `// triv:`), each a construct-free answer the author measured; `firsttry --empty-body` runs each and requires it to fail verification or the item's forms. The items that carry forms are named in §15.2, §16.2 and §17.2 and, for the exits, in the author notes. Built by the DV3b tools lane, with tests on throwaway roots, before Q07 is armed (`stable` before Q09 is armed, with Q29). **Assigned** (Δ159): the DV3b tools lane builds it as proposed before Q07 is armed (`stable` with Q29, before Q09 is armed); the entry that records it as built is Q46's item (d).
44. **Open statements in the freeze pin (tools owner; before Q07 and Q08 are armed).** A statement hole is completed by adding clauses: Q07.H7v ships with no `ensures`, Q08.T1v and H3v ship trait methods with no specification. `specfreeze --check` then reports `freeze-changed` on a correct answer (probed for an exec fn and for a trait method, the author notes §R.1), and so does rubric line A5 (`scripts/specfreeze --check <ID>` reports no finding), as §6 Q22 found for a frame hole. Proposed: a pin field `open_specs` (Dafny and Verus, exec fns and trait methods included): the signature is compared exactly, every shipped clause must still be there verbatim and in order, and added `requires` and `ensures` clauses are accepted; `--pin` takes the open specs from the twins (a declaration whose twin header differs from the shipped one by added clauses only); `--check` and `reference_lint` honour it. The Q07 and Q08 rubrics' A5 line names `port::from_u128` (Q07), `Toggle::flip`, `Odometer::advance` and `Odometer::current` (Q08) as open. **Assigned** (Δ159): the DV3b tools lane builds `open_specs` as proposed before Q07 and Q08 are armed; its as-built entry is Q46's item (d).
45. **The flagship's Dafny statement form (decided; Q08 lane).** `specfreeze --against` in hash mode counts a Dafny function's body as part of its statement, so a delivery that gives a bodyless function its body reported `freeze-changed` (probed, the author notes §R.1). Decided: the learner states each Dafny operation as a bodyless **method** whose `ensures` clause states its correctness, never as a bodyless function (§16.4); a method's body is not part of its statement. The Verus statements stay `external_body` stubs with `ensures`, so the two columns now state the same thing the same way. The flagship's acceptance lines were re-run on the twins in this form (`vcheck --gate` with zero holes, `specfreeze --against` the committed statements, `vaudit` with no finding, the witnesses unchanged): all held, once one proof of the Verus reference delivery had been repaired (the re-run found it failing; the author notes §R.1). No tools change. **Stands as decided** (Δ159): no tools change; the Q08 lane builds §16.4's form.
46. **Entries for the records lane (records; before the detailed tier is armed).** Proposed DELTAS entries, with Q26's: (a) the detailed tier's language shares and pairing — Q07 31/69, Q08 63/37 and Q09 20/18/62 hole minutes, and pairs only in Q07's T1 and Q09's T1, H1, X1 and X2 — as deviations from design §6.2 C1 and §6.4's module tilts, the tier rule (≥ 40 % per language) holding at 47/53 (§5.2; the dropped twins in the author notes §2.2); (b) the refinement-hole form of Q40; (c) the assignment of Q33; (d) the forms of Q43 and the open statements of Q44 once built; (e) the gate core's label-free classification (§17.7: the gate decides from instrument documents alone, and only the evaluator reads labels); (f) Q07 armed with A2d pending (Q31). **Resolved** (Δ159): (a) by Δ155, (b) by Δ154, (c) by Δ145, (f) by Δ156; (e) is built by the Q09 lane as §17.7 plans it, on the interface Δ145 records, and its as-built entry records it; (d) is recorded by the tools lane's as-built entry. Q26's entry is Δ157.

## 8. The BigNat spine: layer L3, and the moderate tier's conventions

*Written 2026-10-05 by the DV3a plan lane and revised the same day after review (this section and §9–§11; §5.1 holds their cross-module checks and §6 Q13–Q25 their open questions). The item tables are the contract for the DV3a module lanes. Like the rest of this plan, these sections name no answer and describe no tool behaviour that a Q04–Q06 prediction asks about; answers, gate results and the evidence behind every claim are in `Solutions/plans/Q04-Q06-author-notes.md`.*

> **Exit tables.** §9.2, §10.2 and §11.2 end with each module's exit table, which names each exit item by its id, form, strand, language, file and minutes only (as §2.2–§4.2 do). Exit items are unseen by design, so their statements, prompts, `need:` lines and shipped checks live in the author notes (§6 Q25, DELTAS Δ133).

### 8.0 Conventions the moderate tier adds to §0

§0 stands. The moderate tier adds these forms; the tool changes and decisions they raise are §6 Q13–Q25.

| what | form |
|---|---|
| **File order** (moderate tier) | Header; the **prediction block**; T items; W items; WRITE cards; RECOGNISE cards; H items; the final section. The prediction block comes before the T block, so every prediction of the module is committed (`scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --check`) before a test-out item is attempted; T items stay closed book and are still done before reading the chapter. Until `firsttry` enforces the order (§6 Q23), the file header and the T blocks say so |
| **Python items** (Q06, design §6.2 C8) | `# item: Q06.H6py S13` in `harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py`; `<lang>` is `py`. A Python hole is a function whose body (after its docstring) is the one statement `raise NotImplementedError("<item id>")`, counted from the syntax tree (never grep) by the `fvh` core's counter (§11.8). Python verdict items use `# SLOT: CHECK <item>` and the module's slot file, like the verifier files |
| **Dafny annotation hole over frames** (Q05.H2d) | §0's Dafny annotation hole with frame clauses as the missing annotations: the body is given, the `modifies` clause and the frame postcondition are missing, `assume false;` is the first statement. It needs §6 Q22 (the statement freeze must accept the learner's added frame clauses) before Q05 is armed |
| **Dafny annotation hole with the measure missing** (Q05.H4d) | §0's Dafny annotation hole whose missing annotations are the loop invariants **and** the loop's `decreases` |
| **Dafny annotation hole with the loop frame missing** (Q05.H1d) | the code is given; the invariants and the `modifies` clause of one loop are missing; `assume false;` is the first statement |
| **Dafny code hole with given annotations** (Q05.H5d) | the inverse of an annotation hole: the loop header, its invariants and the code after the loop are given; the code the item names (a case before the loop, the loop body) is missing; `assume false;` is the first statement |
| **Annotation hole in a `by method` body** (Q05.H3d) | the function and its body are given; inside `by method { … }` the loop code and its `decreases` are given, the invariants and the assertions and lemma calls they need are missing, `assume false;` is the first statement of the by-method body |
| **Exec hole in a `by method` body** (Q05.X1d) | the function is given; the by-method body is `{ assume false; }` |
| **Verus annotation hole with a group line** (Q04.H4v) | the code, the `decreases`, every proof call and the invariant clauses about indices and lengths are given; the learner writes the invariant's remaining clause and the one `broadcast use` line at the top of the body (two regions, each marked `@hole-begin`/`@hole-end` in the twin); one labelled `assume(false)` is the **first statement** of the body |
| **Verus annotation-hole shape** (authoring rule) | a Verus annotation hole ships only if its loop body, and every `return` inside it, verifies under the invariant clauses that ship (otherwise the shipped file is not green). Q05.H4v and Q05.H5v fail this rule and ship as exec holes; Q04.H4v's code is arranged so that it holds |
| **Property-test item** (Q06.H4d/H4v, X1d/X1v) | an exec hole: a bounded checker whose postcondition is **two-sided** (`ok` holds exactly when every sample has the property), so no constant body verifies. Below it, outside the verified code (Verus: a `#[cfg(test)]` module after `verus!`; Dafny: `@Test` methods), a commented suite under `Uncomment as you go:` runs the checker on a correct candidate (it must pass) and on a planted one (it must reject it). Done adds `scripts/vcheck --test` on the unit (§6 Q20) |
| **Budget item** (`uses: rlimit`, Q06) | a proof hole whose block quotes, commented, an attempt that does not verify at the pinned limit. The quoted attempt is not an answer: no answer is the attempt with lines deleted, and the author notes record its runs. The item asks for a proof that verifies at the pinned limit with **no resource attribute** (§6 Q21: the tools then check the attribute at the exit and in the strict report) |
| **Std tier** | Q04's `std/` unit, Q05's and Q06's Dafny units, and the Dafny exit, check and agent files of Q04–Q06 are std tier; all of them but Q05's exit file reach L3, which imports `Std`. Until the tools change (§6 Q13), each such file outside a unit imports at least one `Std` module itself |
| **Moderate rates** (spec §2.5; design §6.2 C1) | hole 6 min on average, itemized per item inside the holes line (§9.6, §10.5, §11.5); prediction 2.4–3 (a four-label obligation specimen 3); WRITE card 2.5; worked example 2.5 where the module's WRITE cards fill most of the worked line (Q04, Q06), 6 in Q05 (its two worked examples are the given class and module type that later items build on); exit item 5.5 (design §7.5's moderate exit lines: 22 min for 4 unseen items). The worked-example and exit rates differ from spec §2.5's ranges; the line totals are the design's |

### 8.1 Rules (design §10.1; spec §2.6; Δ22, Δ34, Δ35, Δ45, Δ48, Δ54, Δ94)

§1.1's six rules hold for L3. In addition:

1. **L3 re-supplies intro-tier targets.** It holds, proved, the Q02–Q03 targets that a later layer or module needs (§8.5). By the tier law (§1.1 rule 3) an answer to a tier-*k* item may appear in a layer of tier > *k*: L3 is moderate, Q02–Q03 are intro. L3 therefore holds whole answers of some Q02–Q03 holes and must never be opened before Q04: its header says so, and `CORE.md` already forbids an instructor to open or quote a layer above the record's `layer` because later layers re-supply earlier targets (Δ90). No layer holds the answer of any Q01–Q03 **exit or test-out** item (§8.6). **The orchestrator adopted this reading on 2026-10-05 (§6 Q15, DELTAS Δ130): §1.1 rule 3 now states it, with the matching exemption in the leak patterns' rule 2.**
2. **Nothing L3 gives is posed again.** No Q04–Q18 module poses as a hole, exit or test-out item anything §8.2–§8.3 list.
3. **Std tier (Δ34).** The Dafny layer imports `Std.Arithmetic.Power`, so its tier is `std` and only `std/` units reach it: Q04's `core/` unit reaches L1–L2 only. `Std.Arithmetic.LittleEndianNat` stays excluded from every BigNat file (Δ35); no item of Q04–Q06 uses it, also not as a search-then-name item (DV3a's decision under Δ35).
4. **Verus tier.** The Verus layer uses `vstd::prelude` only, so it adds no import tier to the files that reach it.
5. **Opacity (Δ48).** `weight` is `#[verifier::opaque]`. L3 gives its two defining equations as proved lemmas, so no module before Q06 needs `reveal`.
6. **No answer in the header.** As for L1–L2 (Δ110), the headers name no import tier and explain no measure, lever or broadcast mechanism; the Q04 chapter does.

### 8.2 L3, Dafny (tier: moderate; declared by Q04; imported by Q04–Q18)

`dafny/given/Q/QG_L3_BigNat.dfy`, `module QG_L3_BigNat`, `import opened QG_L1_BigNat`, `import opened QG_L2_BigNat`, `import Power = Std.Arithmetic.Power`; import tier `std`.

*Re-supplied intro-tier targets* (proved; each statement is the one its module posed):

| declaration | first posed |
|---|---|
| `lemma ToNatDropLastZero(s: seq<nat>) requires \|s\| > 0 && s[\|s\| - 1] == 0 ensures ToNat(s[..\|s\| - 1]) == ToNat(s)` | Q02.H1d |
| `lemma NormalizeToNat(s: seq<nat>) ensures ToNat(Normalize(s)) == ToNat(s)` (`decreases \|s\|`) | Q02.H3d |
| `lemma NormalizeCanonical(s: seq<nat>) requires Valid(s) ensures Canonical(Normalize(s))` (`decreases \|s\|`) | Q02.H4d |
| `lemma FromNatCorrect(n: nat) ensures Valid(FromNat(n)) && ToNat(FromNat(n)) == n` (`decreases n`) | Q03.H2d |
| `lemma AddLimbCorrect(s: seq<nat>, d: nat) requires Valid(s) && d < BASE ensures Valid(AddLimb(s, d)) && ToNat(AddLimb(s, d)) == ToNat(s) + d` (`decreases \|s\|`) | Q03.H1d (its value half) |

*Moderate-tier definition* (the targets of Q04–Q06 are lemmas and code **about** it; the definition is given):

| declaration |
|---|
| `function Cmp(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>): int` (`decreases \|a\| + \|b\|`) — three-way comparison by the most significant differing limb, a missing limb reading as 0 |

*Std-tier helper* (the bridge to the standard library):

| declaration |
|---|
| `lemma PowIsStdPow(n: nat) ensures Pow(n) == Power.Pow(BASE, n)` (`decreases n`) |

No lemma of L3 carries an induction attribute: every unit that reaches L3 sets manual lemma induction (`dafny/options/std.toml`, Δ45).

Crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l3` (dependencies `vstd` at the pin, `qg_bignat_l1`, `qg_bignat_l2`; `build = false`; `[package.metadata.verus] verify = true`), `src/lib.rs` with `pub use qg_bignat_l2::*;`, import tier `prelude`.

|---|---|

| `pub fn from_u64(x: u64) -> (r: BigNat) ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == x` | Q03.H2v |
| `pub fn add_limb(a: &BigNat, x: u64) -> (r: BigNat) requires a.wf(), ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == a.to_nat() + x` (it calls L2's `lemma_add_limb_lift`) | Q03.H1v |

*Moderate-tier definitions and helpers*:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|

| `pub proof fn lemma_weight_succ(n: nat) ensures weight(n + 1) == BASE * weight(n)` | proved by revealing `weight` |
| `pub broadcast proof fn lemma_weight_mono(m: nat, n: nat) requires m <= n, ensures #[trigger] weight(m) <= #[trigger] weight(n)` (`decreases n`) | group member |
| `pub broadcast proof fn lemma_seq_to_nat_upper(s: Seq<u64>) ensures #[trigger] seq_to_nat(s) < weight(s.len())` (`decreases s.len()`) | group member |
| `pub broadcast proof fn lemma_seq_to_nat_lower(s: Seq<u64>) requires seq_wf(s), s.len() > 0, ensures weight((s.len() - 1) as nat) <= #[trigger] seq_to_nat(s)` (`decreases s.len()`) | group member |

| `pub proof fn lemma_lt_at(a: Seq<u64>, b: Seq<u64>, i: int)` — same length, equal above `i`, `a[i] < b[i]`: `seq_to_nat(a) < seq_to_nat(b)` (`decreases i`) | given for Q04.H4v, as L2's lifting lemma is given for Q03.H1v (Δ52) |

No L3 crate or file holds an `external_body`, `assume`, `admit`, `{:axiom}`, abstract module or test, so `expected/verus_trust.toml` stays absent (Δ95) and `vaudit --mode given` reports 0 (Δ97).

### 8.5 What L3 re-supplies, and the decision on §1.7 F3

- **Kept from §1.5's forward contract:** Dafny `ToNatDropLastZero`, `NormalizeToNat`, `NormalizeCanonical`, `FromNatCorrect`, `AddLimbCorrect` (the value half Q03.H1d posed plus the validity half); Verus `lemma_seq_to_nat_drop_last_zero`, `from_u64`, `add_limb`. `FromNatCorrect` and `from_u64` are needed by L4 (its abstract specification and the `NatLike` implementation, design §10.1); the normalization and carry facts are needed by later normalization and addition proofs.
- **`FromNatCanonical`** (in §1.5's list) is not in L3: no Q02–Q03 item posed it (it was a DV0 candidate; Q03 posed `FromNatCorrect` only), so there is nothing to re-supply. L4 or a later layer states and proves it if one of its targets needs it.
- **Not in L3, by the F3 decision:** Dafny `NormalizeLoop` (a method no layer calls) and Verus `normalize`. Both stay module-local twins in `Solutions/`. DV3b and DV5 keep them out of L4–L5 as well, so Q10 may pose the normalize invariant pair as DV0 planned (re-supplied from L6 on if Q13's `sub` needs `normalize`).
- **Consequence for Q10 (DV5):** `FromNatCorrect` is now given from Q04 on, so Q10's round-trip pair cannot re-pose it: its Dafny half needs a fresh twin (for example a mirror of the extras' `to_u64`), never `FromNatCorrect`. §6 Q14 records the decision for DV5 to confirm.

### 8.6 Proof that L3 answers no item of a module that imports it

1. **The layer is listed exhaustively** in §8.2–§8.3.
2. **No L3 declaration has the statement of a Q04–Q06 item.** For every T, H and X item of §9–§11, no L3 declaration has the same or a stronger `ensures` (table below).
3. **No L3 proof is an item's whole answer.** The re-supplied proofs answer Q02–Q03 holes (tier law: allowed, §8.1 rule 1 and §6 Q15), never a Q04–Q06 item. The weight equations are single unfoldings; Q04.H1v asks for the power of a sum.
4. **No L3 code answers an annotation or exec hole of Q04–Q06.** L3's exec code is `from_u64` and `add_limb`; no Q04–Q06 item asks for either. `lemma_lt_at` is a given helper of Q04.H4v, whose hole is one invariant clause and the scoping of the bounds group.
5. **No layer holds the answer of a Q01–Q03 exit or test-out item.** Checked against §2.2–§4.2's T rows and the exit statements in the author notes (Δ133): L3 states no exit or T target of Q01–Q03.

| item(s) | kind of target | same statement in L3 | L3 code answering it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q04.T1d / T1v | recursive lemma, zero value ⇒ zero limbs | no | none |
| Q04.H1d / H1v | the power of a sum | no (L3 states `weight`'s one-step equation) | none |
| Q04.H2d | quantified prefix bounds | no (L2's `ToNatBound` is the bound for one sequence; H2d asks for every prefix) | none |
| Q04.H2v | the first-difference order, quantified | no (`lemma_lt_at` is the fact for one index; H2v asks for its universal form) | none |
| Q04.H3d / H3v | a shifted value stays below a power | no | none |
| Q04.H4d | `Cmp` agrees with the value order | no (`Cmp` is a definition) | none |
| Q04.H4v | `cmp`'s invariant clause and group line | no (no exec comparison in L3) | none |
| Q04.H5v | a product bound | no | none |
| Q04.X1d, X2d, X1v, X2v | exit items (statements in the author notes) | no | none |
| Q05 items | frames, `by method`, measures, parsers, results, VCs | no | none (L3 holds no class, no loop of Q05's, no parser) |
| Q06 items | triggers, opacity, budgets, property checkers, statistics | no | none (L3's only opaque definition is `weight`, whose two equations are given lemmas; no Q06 item asks for them) |

### 8.7 Forward constraints (for DV3b–DV7)

- **F5.** No module Q04–Q18 poses anything §8.2–§8.3 list (rule 2).
- **F6.** L4 must re-supply the Q04–Q06 targets that L4 and later modules call: at least Dafny `CmpCorrect` (L4's abstract specification proves `Spec.CmpCorrect` with it) and Verus `cmp` (the `NatLike` implementation calls it); likely Dafny `PowAdd` and Verus `lemma_weight_add`, `lemma_seq_to_nat_append` (addition and multiplication proofs). Whatever L4 re-supplies, no Q07–Q18 module poses again. In particular design §7.5's Q16 H2 ("`cmp` with invented invariant") cannot pose `cmp` once L4 gives it: DV5 poses DV0's non-canonical `cmp_any` variant instead (§6 Q14).
- **F7.** Q04, Q05 and Q06 are one tier: no module's files hold another's answers, and no Q04–Q06 given code (agent files included) answers a Q04–Q06 item. Shared techniques are intended (Q04's shifted-bound proof and Q06's budget items; Q04's `cmp` scan and Q05's `CmpFast` scan in the other language). One statement is shared across languages on purpose, by a Q04 exit item and a Q06 budget hole (the author notes name them): no Q06 file holds the exit item's proof, and the Q06 hole is about the budget, not the induction.
- **F8.** F3 as decided in §8.5: `NormalizeLoop` and Verus `normalize` stay out of L4–L5; Q10's round-trip pair is a fresh twin.
- **F9.** The Dafny exit, check and agent files of Q07–Q18 that reach L3 or L4 need a std tier; §6 Q13's tool change, or the import workaround of §8.0, applies to them too.
- **F10.** `CanonicalUnique` (DV0's home: "Q06 or Q13") is the top lemma of Q06's Dafny agent file (§11.4). Q13 does not pose it; Δ53's Q13 bundle ("normalize gives the unique canonical numeral") needs another second half, or a layer from L5 on re-supplies `CanonicalUnique` for it (§6 Q14).
