### 9.3 Constructs (registry)

- `write_d = ["calc", "forall", "lemma-recursion", "library-call"]`, `write_v = ["calc", "forall", "lemma-recursion", "library-call", "broadcast-use", "nonlinear-arith"]`: 6 units (4 paired, 2 Verus), 10 slugs (caps 6 / 10).
- `recognise_d = ["induction-auto"]`, `recognise_v = []`. Design §6.3's `d:induction-auto` comes after `{:induction false}` work: Q01.H3d, Q02.H4d and the Q03-on manual units.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): calc d H1d, X1d · v H1v, X1v; forall d T1d, H2d, X2d · v T1v, H2v; lemma-recursion d T1d, H1d, H4d, X1d · v T1v, H1v; library-call d H3d, X2d · v H1v, H3v; broadcast-use v H4v, X2v; nonlinear-arith v H5v, X1v.
- **Usage by strand** (design §7.3: earlier strands at this depth). Every item's strand is at least the strand of every construct it uses: H1d (S4) uses `calc` (S3) and lemma recursion (S4); every Verus item that uses the library, a group or `nonlinear_arith` is S5 and sits in `library.rs` or the exit file.
- **Hand before automation** (design §6.3), by file order: H1d (the power of a sum, by hand) precedes H3d's standard-library calls; H2v calls L3's `lemma_lt_at` by name before H4v brings the group in with `broadcast use`; inside `library.rs`, the C5v card shows the named calls before the group and the C6v card shows a hand proof with a library lemma before `nonlinear_arith`, and H3v's library calls precede H5v's `nonlinear_arith`.
- **Library staging.** Dafny: only the library file, the check unit, the exit file and the agent file reach `Std`; the core file stays core. Verus: the crate root `lib.rs` (S1–S4) and the exit file import no `vstd::arithmetic` (tier `prelude`); `library.rs` (S5) and the agent file do (tier `arithmetic`). These are §9.7's tiers, and `check_course_map.py`'s import-tier group checks them: §6 Q16 admits `vstd::calc` in the `prelude` tier and every wider one (built, §13.1).

### 9.4 Agentic file (rung 4: retriever race)

- **Shape:** green-with-holes bank (spec §3.11.1). Three goals per file, each a statement whose proof is a short chain of standard-library calls; no goal states a Q04–Q06 item, and no goal's proof is the whole answer of one. The Dafny module carries `{:disableNonlinearArithmetic}`, so no goal falls to arithmetic automation (Δ46). Dafny goals: a strict product inequality; the quotient of a shifted value; a product of remainders modulo `m`. Verus goals over `vstd`'s `pow`: a power of a power; the low limb of a power; a positive power of `BASE` modulo `BASE`. Every goal fails every empty-body mode, the light arithmetic groups included.
- **CONTRACT** (line 1, three keys): `goal:` close every goal with standard-library lemma calls · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line · zero holes · `git diff` shows only the three bodies · each body holds only library calls (and their arguments), each call on its own line followed by `// found-by: <rung>`, where `<rung>` is one of `ide`, `grep`, `docs`, `llm`, `memory` · no lemma of your own · re-verify after every edit · `scripts/vaudit --compare expected/Q04_AgentRetriever.audit <file>` (Verus: `expected/q04_proof_craft_agent.audit`) reports no difference.
- **Learner follow-up (15 min):** before the run, take each goal through your own search ladder (IDE hover and go-to-definition, a scoped `grep` of the pinned library sources, the library's documentation, a model, memory), timing it, and fill the `SEARCH LOG` (goal, lemma, rung, minutes). Then run `/contract Q04` in a fresh session: its first live test of contract v0. Afterwards fill `COMPARISON`: did each `found-by:` rung match where the lemma lives? Did the transcript show a lemma name that does not exist (a resolution error), a non-library step, or a statement edit? Which of your v0 rules did the run test?

### 9.5 The library and the toolchain-mismatch lab (20 min)

Done by hand in the learner's own terminal, in a scratch directory under `/tmp`, never in the course tree and never in a Claude session (the guard refuses some of these commands there, rule 2g). The learner records each command, its exit code and the first line it printed in the `TOOLCHAIN LAB` block of `Q04_Library.dfy`, then reads `VERSIONS.md` §1 (the pinned toolchains): only that section, since later sections record behaviour that later predictions ask about.

1. **Dafny and Z3.** Run the pinned `dafny verify` on a one-lemma file three ways: as `scripts/vcheck` runs it (the bundled default solver), with `--solver-path` naming the other Z3 bundled in the Dafny tree, and with `--solver-path` naming a path that does not exist.
2. **Verus and Z3.** Run the pinned `verus` on a one-function file, then again with `VERUS_Z3_PATH` naming that other Z3.
3. **Verus and rustc.** Compare the `Toolchain:` line of `verus --version` with `rustc --version` and `rustup toolchain list` run outside the course tree; then run `verus` once with `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` naming your default toolchain.
4. **The two library tiers.** Read `dafny/options/core.toml` and `std.toml`, and the import lines of `Q04_ProofCraft.dfy`, `Q04_Library.dfy`, `lib.rs` and `library.rs`; note which standard-library module no BigNat file may import (Δ35).

### 10.3 Constructs (registry)

- `write_d = ["dynamic-frames", "by-method", "error-propagation", "lexicographic-decreases"]`, `write_v = ["mut-ref", "result", "error-propagation", "lexicographic-decreases"]`: 6 units (2 paired, 2 per language alone), 8 slugs.
- `recognise_d = recognise_v = []`.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): dynamic-frames d H1d, H2d; by-method d H3d, X1d; error-propagation d H5d, X2d · v H5v, X2v; lexicographic-decreases d T1d, H4d · v T1v, H4v; mut-ref v H1v, H2v, X1v; result v H2v, H5v, X1v.
- **Given code** (no item): Dafny `LimbStack` (fields, `Valid`, the constructor, `Size`), `BitCount`, `Ones`, `AllDigits`, `DecValue`, `Digit`; Verus `LimbStack` (`inv`, `new`), `Full`, `bit_count`, `ones`, `ParseError`, `is_digit`, `all_digits`, `dec_value`, `digit`, `mul10_add`. Δ54: every `&mut self` method is on a type the module owns; `shift_up` is a free function on L1's `BigNat`. Each given definition sits above the item block that uses it, never under its marker (Q03's lesson, §7.4).
- **wp→VC lab** (20 min, on paper, written with the chapter): the verification conditions of a call against a callee's contract and frame, and of a loop whose body makes the call. P6d and P9dv are its checked items.

### 10.4 Agentic file (rung 5: infra debugger, wiring)

- **Shape:** planted failure, infrastructure class (design §12, "Q05 (wiring)"). Each file is RED with exactly **2** planted wiring failures, diagnosed from receipts; the rest of the file is given code that verifies once the wiring is right (no hole). A wiring file is never the fix: `dfyconfig.toml`, `Cargo.toml`, `MODULES.toml` and `scripts/` are guard class 2g, refused under every allowlist.
- **Receipts first.** Before the run the learner fills the file's `RECEIPTS` block: for `scripts/vcheck --json <agent file>` and `scripts/vaudit <agent file>`, the command, its exit code and every diagnostic or finding line, verbatim; and the module's layer chain as `MODULES.toml` states it.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` diagnose the file's wiring failures from the `RECEIPTS` block, then repair them · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` a `DIAGNOSIS` block written before any edit, one line per failure: the instrument that reports it, the cause, and the receipt line it rests on · `git diff` changes no line of a lemma, function, method or fn (signature, contract or body): only lines outside every declaration change, and the `DIAGNOSIS` block · `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line · zero holes · re-run both commands after every edit · `scripts/vaudit --compare expected/Q05_AgentInfra.audit <file>` (Verus: `expected/q05_frames_agent.audit`) reports no difference.
- **Learner follow-up (15 min):** collect the receipts; run `/contract Q05` in a fresh session; then audit: does each diagnosis line rest on a receipt, or on a guess? Did the transcript show an attempt on a wiring file (a guard refusal) or on a declaration? Did the contractor stop at a green gate, before the audit was clean?

## 11. Q06 — The machine and the verifier loop

*Tier: moderate, family C (S9–S13), layer L3, 281 min + the tier viva (30 min, `viva_mode = "tier"`, pool Q04–Q06). Exit: 2 D + 2 V unseen items, ≥ 3/4 and ≥ 1/2 in each language. Agent rung 6: audited prover, in Dafny. Card 02.4. Python enters as the tool language (design §6.2 C8): its statistics are done by hand and by composing the frozen `fvh.stats` (§11.8).*

Then uncomment the suite: the correct candidate passes, the planted one is rejected | exec hole `{ assume false; }` + commented `@Test` suite · none |

### 11.3 Constructs (registry)

- `write_d = write_v = ["trigger", "opaque-reveal", "rlimit", "property-test"]`: 4 paired units, 8 slugs.
- `recognise_d = recognise_v = []`.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): trigger T1, H1; opaque-reveal H2, X2; rlimit H3, X2; property-test H4, X1 (each in both languages).
- **The escalation ladder** (design §11.1 row 14): concrete checks (Q02–Q03), then bounded property tests (this module: Dafny bounded `expect` loops in `@Test` methods; Rust `#[test]`s outside `verus!`), then proof. Each checker's postcondition is two-sided (§8.0), so the verifier itself rejects a checker that always answers the same; the suites exercise it on candidates. No crates.io dependency enters a course crate: course tools build tests with `verus --compile --test`, which links `vstd` and the layer crates only (Δ20, Δ42), so a sampler is hand-written.
- **Done**, beyond §0: `scripts/vcheck --test` on each unit passes every test, and the Python file has zero holes with `harness/tests/q/test_q06_stats.py` green (§11.8). The budget items' proofs carry no resource attribute (§6 Q21).
- **Statistics by hand** (the paper half of the 30-minute line, written with the chapter): a Wilson interval and its width at n = 20, pass@k from its definition on a small table, why only the first attempt per task enters a pass rate, and a paired comparison read from its discordant pairs. Neither the paper problems nor the frozen tests use P7py's or P9py's inputs (§11.8).
- **`EVAL.md` v0** (15 min): the learner's first evaluation report in `research/EVAL.md`, section `Q-v0`: claim, metric and estimator, n, the interval, exclusions, cost, and a disclosure line, over the learner's own first-try record (`instructor/calib/*.tsv`, read through `fvh.records`) and H6py's function.
- **`TRUST.md` v0** (10 min): the learner's trusted-base inventory for Q04–Q06 in `research/TRUST.md`, section `Q-v0`: per language the front end, the solver and its version, the standard library's trusted parts, the course wrappers and the given layers, and for each entry what would make a green run wrong. It is S10's artifact; D4 keys it under S12 because only S12 and S13 keys may rest on prose (§11.6).

### 11.4 Agentic file (rung 6: audited prover, Dafny)

- **One language.** The rung runs on the Dafny file only: the Verus statement of the same top lemma has no decomposition whose leaves are all admissible holes (the author notes give the evidence), and the 15-minute line holds one skeleton.
- **Shape:** green-with-holes bank whose statements the learner writes (spec §5.1: "the learner's green hole-skeleton is the contract; the contractor fills ≤5 leaves"). The file ships one frozen top lemma, canonical numerals are unique (`lemma CanonicalUnique(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) requires Canonical(a) && Canonical(b) && ToNat(a) == ToNat(b) ensures a == b`, `decreases |a|`), as a hole, above an empty `SKELETON` section whose comment names the shape of a decomposition: a lower bound on a canonical numeral's value, an order between the lengths of canonical numerals, and a monotonicity fact about `Pow`.
- **Before the run (the learner):** write at most 5 leaf lemmas as holes in `SKELETON` and replace the top lemma's hole by a proof from them, until the file is green with only the leaf holes; copy the file to `research/q06_skeleton.dfy` (the statement snapshot); **commit the skeleton** (`git commit`), so that the acceptance's `git diff` compares the run with it.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` prove every leaf lemma of the skeleton · `allowed-files:` the agent file · `acceptance:` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line · zero holes · `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q06_skeleton.dfy <file>` reports no change · `git diff` against the committed skeleton shows only the leaves' bodies · no new lemma · at most 5 leaves · re-verify after every edit · `scripts/vaudit --compare expected/Q06_AgentProver.audit <file>` reports no difference.
- **Learner follow-up (15 min, with the skeleton):** write and commit the skeleton (the main work); run `/contract Q06` in a fresh session; audit the statements (the freeze check), the assumptions (the audit) and each leaf's usefulness (call it once on concrete arguments, or say why its `requires` can hold); record in `AUDIT` which leaf took the contractor longest and why.

### 11.7 The moderate-tier gate and viva (30 min, outside the 281)

- **Who sits it.** `viva_mode = "tier"` on Q06 only: `/viva Q06` examines Q04, Q05 and Q06; `/viva Q04` and `/viva Q05` refuse and name Q06, as `/viva Q02` names Q03.
- **The pool.** Every `viva: yes` question of the three moderate-tier banks (`.claude/skills/instructor/banks/Q04.md`, `Q05.md`, `Q06.md`, written with the chapters). Q06's bank tables the draws under *Tier viva draws*, as Q03's does: pairwise disjoint draws, each with at least one question from each module, at least one verifier-refereed predict and at least one backward edge, no strand twice; the first viva sits the first draw disjoint from every earlier tier viva record.
- **Predicted audit rows** (spec §2.3.3, the moderate row; 3 of the 30 minutes). Before the viva, once `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --report --strict` passes for Q04, Q05 and Q06, the learner writes in the `AUDIT ROWS` block of `Q06_Machine.dfy` one line per Q04–Q06 exercise and exit file: the number of `scripts/vaudit` findings expected and their kinds. Then runs `scripts/vaudit` on each file and records whether each line held. The viva opens on any line that did not. Spec §2.3.3 makes the confirmed rows an exit check; this plan keeps them advisory (§6 Q24).
- **The exit** of each module stays its own (§9.2, §10.2, §11.2): `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --exit`, 4 unseen items, at least 3 of 4 and at least 1 of 2 in each language (`firsttry`'s `EXIT_TIERS`, moderate).

### 11.8 The `fvh` core Q06 needs (built in DV3a, before Q06 was armed)

Design §10.3 and §14.1 place a frozen core and learner packages in one `uv` project; guard rules 2 (learner-owned `harness/q/`, `harness/r/`), 2f (frozen `harness/fvh/**`, `harness/tests/**`) and 2g (`pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `conftest.py`, `pytest.ini`, `*.pth` at any depth) already cover its paths, and `check_course_map.py` already knows the `py-std` and `py-fvh` import tiers and the `# item:` grammar. When this plan was written nothing below existed (`VERSIONS.md`: no `pyproject.toml`, no `uv.lock`). DV3a built it, not DV4 (§6 Q19, DELTAS Δ134), with the deviations DELTAS Δ136 lists (§13.1), and `VERSIONS.md` §1.4 now pins the lock. Q06 needs exactly this much of it; the rest of the core (protocol, backends, budgets, sandbox client, cheat corpus) can follow before Q07 and Q12.

**Layout.**

| path | class | content |
|---|---|---|
| `harness/pyproject.toml`, `harness/uv.lock` | 2g | project `fvh`, `requires-python >= 3.12` (the host's `python3`), **no runtime dependency** (the standard library only, so `fvh.stats` is auditable); a pinned `pytest` in a dev group; `[tool.pytest.ini_options] testpaths = ["tests"]`, `pythonpath = [".", "q", "r"]`, `addopts = "-p no:cacheprovider"`, so one process imports `fvh`, `fvh_q` and `fvh_r` (design §14.2 rule 5) and no `.pytest_cache` is written. `uv` 0.12.4 is the pinned host tool (`VERSIONS.md`) |
| `.gitignore` entries | 2g | `harness/.venv/` and `__pycache__/` at any depth; the course tools run pytest with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, so the frozen `harness/tests/` gains no bytecode files |
| `harness/fvh/__init__.py`, `stats.py`, `records.py`, `holes.py` | 2f, frozen | the core modules below |
| `harness/q/fvh_q/__init__.py` | learner package scaffolding | empty (the checker declares `__init__.py` files itself) |
| `harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py` | 2, learner-owned; tier `py-fvh` | Q06's Python file (§11.2): imports only the standard library and `fvh` |
| `harness/r/fvh_r/__init__.py` | learner package scaffolding | empty (so the coexistence import holds before R05) |
| `harness/tests/core/test_stats.py`, `test_records.py`, `test_holes.py` | 2f | the core's own tests (known values, below) |
| `harness/tests/q/test_q06_stats.py` | 2f | Q06's acceptance tests: they fail while any of H5py–H7py is a hole and pass on the reference; they also check composition: the H6py and H7py tests replace `fvh.stats.wilson_interval` and `fvh.stats.pass_at_k`, and any name the learner module bound to them, with recording wrappers and require the call, since statistics code lives only in the core (design §9.2 rule 4). They check outputs against constants and against `fvh.stats` itself, never against a re-implementation of H5py–H7py, and none uses P7py's or P9py's inputs |
| `Solutions/harness/pyproject.toml` | reference, its own `uv` project, never a member | project `solutions-harness` (reference identifiers start with `solutions`, §14.2 rule 2) |
| `Solutions/harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py` | reference twin | the filled file; each H item declares `# triv: <body>` (the Python TRIV bodies, below) |

**`fvh.stats`** (pure functions, standard library only, deterministic given `seed`; every interval is a closed `(lo, hi)` tuple of floats in `[0, 1]` for proportions). Q06 composes the first two; the rest are frozen at the same time so that Q09 and Q12 compose, never write, statistics code.

| signature | contract |
|---|---|
| `pass_at_k(n: int, c: int, k: int) -> float` | the unbiased estimator `1 - C(n - c, k) / C(n, k)` (exact integer arithmetic; `1.0` when `n - c < k`); `ValueError` unless `0 <= c <= n` and `1 <= k <= n` |
| `wilson_interval(successes: int, n: int, confidence: float = 0.95) -> tuple[float, float]` | the Wilson score interval; `ValueError` unless `0 <= successes <= n`, `n >= 1` and `0 < confidence < 1`; the normal quantile from `statistics.NormalDist` |
| `bootstrap_ci(values: Sequence[float], statistic: Callable[[Sequence[float]], float] = statistics.fmean, *, n_resamples: int = 10_000, confidence: float = 0.95, seed: int) -> tuple[float, float]` | percentile bootstrap with `random.Random(seed)`; `ValueError` for an empty `values` |
| `mcnemar(b: int, c: int, *, exact: bool = True) -> float` | two-sided p-value from the discordant counts (exact binomial; `exact=False`: the continuity-corrected chi-square); `1.0` when `b + c == 0` |
| `permutation_test(x: Sequence[float], y: Sequence[float], *, paired: bool, n_permutations: int = 10_000, seed: int) -> float` | two-sided p-value of the mean difference (sign flips when `paired`); `ValueError` for an empty sample |
| `holm(pvalues: Sequence[float], alpha: float = 0.05) -> list[bool]` | Holm–Bonferroni rejections, in input order |
| `mde_two_proportions(n: int, p0: float, *, alpha: float = 0.05, power: float = 0.8, paired: bool = False) -> float` | the minimum detectable difference at `n` tasks per arm |

**`fvh.records`.** `@dataclass(frozen=True) class RunRecord: task_id: str; arm: str; attempt: int; lang: str; verdict: str; holes: int; errors: int; verified_count: int` with the property `success` (`verdict == "verified" and holes == 0`), the course's definition of done; `load_jsonl(path) -> list[RunRecord]` and `dump_jsonl(records, path) -> None`; `first_attempts(records) -> list[RunRecord]` (attempt 1 per `(task_id, arm)`); `@dataclass(frozen=True) class CalibRow: item: str; kind: str; verdict: str; date: str; slot_hash: str` and `load_calib(path) -> list[CalibRow]`, reading `instructor/calib/<ID>.tsv` exactly as `scripts/firsttry.py` writes it (columns `item kind verdict date slot-hash`; the core takes the verdict and kind vocabularies from that writer); `calib_records(rows, arm) -> tuple[list[RunRecord], int]`, the bridge `EVAL.md` v0 uses: one record per row of kind `predict` (`task_id` the item id, `attempt` 1, `lang` the item id's language suffix, `verdict` `"verified"` for the writer's pass word and `"failed"` otherwise, `holes` 0, `errors` 0 or 1, `verified_count` 0) and the number of rows of any other kind, which `EVAL.md` reports as exclusions. H5py builds a `RunRecord` from one `scripts/vcheck --json` document (its keys as `vcheck` prints them: `verdict`, `holes`, `diagnostics` with a `severity` per entry, `verified_count`, `lang`, `stage`, `exit_code`); H5py's docstring states the mapping. H6py and H7py raise `ValueError` for an arm with no records (the Wilson interval is undefined at n = 0).

**`fvh.holes` (the Python hole form and counter).** A Python hole is a function (or method) whose body, after an optional docstring, is exactly one statement `raise NotImplementedError("<item id>")`, the id that of the `# item:` marker above it. `count_holes(source: str) -> list[Hole]` (`Hole(item, name, line)`) works on the syntax tree (`ast`), never on text, so a hole in a comment or a string does not count and a renamed exception is not a hole; `lint(source) -> list[Finding]` reports hole-like bodies that are not holes (a bare `raise NotImplementedError`, an id that does not match the marker, a body of only `...` or `pass` under an H marker). CLI: `python -m fvh.holes [--json] FILE...`, one line per file (`<file>: <n> hole(s)`), exit 0.

**Course tools (DV4, `scripts/`, 2g).**

1. `scripts/dvl/python.py`: `holes(file)` through `fvh.holes`; `test(target, names=None)` through `uv run --directory harness pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider` with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, on the acceptance tests that a file's registry module names (Q06: `tests/q/test_q06_stats.py`), its pass and fail lines parsed from pytest's JUnit XML report written to the run's `raw_dir`; the run's environment is the dvl environment (no network: `uv run --offline --frozen`).
2. `scripts/vcheck` on a `.py` file or on `Q06 py`: the summary line `vcheck: <file> [python, learner loop]: <verdict> — <passed> passed, <failed> failed, <n> holes`; `--gate` passes only with zero holes and every test passing; `--json` as for the verifiers; `--ref` lays `Solutions/harness/q/fvh_q/q06_stats.py` over a scratch copy of `harness/` and runs the same tests; `--shipped Q06` and `--pin-counts Q06` include the Python file in `expected/Q06.counts` (role `harness`; its shipped verdict is `failed`, its hole count 3).
3. `scripts/firsttry.py`: `--report --strict` counts the Python holes (AST) beside the verifier holes; `--lint-hollow` gains the Python branch (every H marker in a `.py` file sits above a counted hole, as shipped); `--empty-body` gains the Python TRIV mode: each H item's body replaced by its twin's `# triv:` body, on top of the reference, must make the acceptance tests fail (a hole whose tests pass on a trivial body is not admitted). The verdict items P7py and P9py use the existing slot and pin machinery unchanged.
4. `scripts/ci.sh --job harness`: `uv run --directory harness --frozen pytest tests/core -p no:cacheprovider`; in author runs also the reference overlay and the Python TRIV mode; `check_course_map.py`'s import-tier group on every `.py` file. The guard's existing rows already allow `uv run --directory harness pytest` and refuse pytest from the checkout root.

**What Q06 needs and nothing more.** Before Q06 is armed: the layout rows above, `fvh.stats` (all seven), `fvh.records`, `fvh.holes`, the three tool changes and the CI job, each with its tests. Not needed for Q06: the `propose` protocol, backends, budgets, the sandbox client, the cheat corpus and `fvh report` (Q07–Q12).

## 13. As built (DV3a)

*Recorded by the DV3a integration lane, 2026-10-05, from the tools, fvh, records, spine and module lanes' reports and the files as built. Where a row of §8–§11 and this section differ, the files and this section describe the course as built. Like the rest of this plan, this section names no answer, no exit statement and no tool behaviour that a prediction asks about; the author-only detail is in `Solutions/plans/Q04-Q06-author-notes.md` §2.9, §3.6, §4.8 and §8 and in `Solutions/plans/Q04-code-notes.md`, `Q05-code-notes.md` and `Q06-code-notes.md`. DELTAS Δ136–Δ140 record the same for later phases.*

### 13.1 Spine, registry and tools (§8, §6, §11.8)

- **L3** holds exactly §8.2–§8.3's contents (DELTAS Δ138). Its headers follow spec §3.12; `Cmp`'s comment says only what it returns, shorter than §8.2's gloss. No layer has a count pin and none needs a trust entry.
- **The registry** holds §9.7, §10.6 and §11.6 verbatim: Q04, Q05 and Q06 are `authoring`. `gen_units.py` writes 13 units. The import tiers assume §6 Q16, which is built.
- **§6's tool items are built:** Q13, Q16, Q18 (with Q6), Q20, Q21, Q22 and Q23 (DELTAS Δ137, with the integration lane's fixes in Δ140), Q15's verifier exemption and the calibration harness's tier filter (Δ137; the skill simulations' filter is built in the kits phase, Δ141), and Q19, the `fvh` core (Δ136). §8.0's workaround for Dafny exit, check and agent files (importing a `Std` module themselves) is no longer needed: a file's declared tier decides.
- **§11.8** is built in DV3a, not DV4 (Δ134), with the deviations Δ136 lists; the Python hole form is §8.0's `raise NotImplementedError("<item id>")`. `expected/Q06.counts` pins the learner Python file's shipped state (role `harness`).

### 13.2 Q04 (§9)

- **§9.1:** the check unit imports L1–L3 and no `Std` module (§6 Q13 is built).
- **Exit files:** both headers allow the pinned standard library's own source to be open beside the given spine.
- **Cards and demos** are the lane's design within the author notes' constraints; the C1, C5v and C9dv demos depart from the plan's sketch, and two demos carry an `after:` line that §9.2 does not list (conservative).
- **Agent files** keep the imports the author notes verified.

### 13.3 Q05 (§10)

- **Dafny file order:** the WRITE cards come before W1d, because W1d is the given `LimbStack` class and H1d and H2d are methods of it. Every card still precedes the holes that use it; the Verus file keeps §8.0's order.
- **Obligation specimens:** the learner specimens of P4d and P5v stay contract-only; their twins carry reference bodies (the reference lint rejects a bodyless or `external_body` twin), which does not change the outcome.
- **Checks:** every hole except H3d gets a commented check (H3d's is its by-method postcondition); T1v's check carries a `// did:` receipt, as in Q02.
- **Agent files:** the CONTRACT block is on line 1, the header states the shipped state, and the receipt and diagnosis blocks are at the end of each file.
- **H2d** is a frame annotation hole in the method's header; its freeze pin uses §6 Q22's open frame.

### 13.4 Q06 (§11)

- **Given definitions** sit directly above the marker of the hole that uses them, not after it (the empty-body gate empties the first function of an item's block).
- **X1d and X1v** ship a commented property suite inside their block (Dafny) or after `verus!` (Verus), which `--report --strict` runs (§6 Q20).
- **Cards:** the C3 demos differ per language, C4's failure demo is a caller's `assert` against a one-sided checker, and the C6dv demos pass the learner lint that `firsttry --lint-hollow` applies to every shipped specimen; the chapter shows the attributes P6dv asks about through scratch-probe pins after its box.
- **Agent file:** the twin keeps the shipped order (the frozen top lemma, then an empty SKELETON) and holds its reference leaves after the "clearly marked" heading.
- **Python:** hint lines follow the three Python holes; `research/EVAL.md` and `research/TRUST.md` gain one pointer comment under their Q-v0 headings (§11.3), and stay unwritten.

### 13.5 Integration

- **Mechanical fixes** (DELTAS Δ140): the Verus `seq_lib` and `arithmetic` tiers inherit `calc!` from `prelude` (§6 Q16 says every wider tier); the fvh lane's wiring of the Python branches into `scripts/vcheck`, `scripts/vaudit` and `scripts/firsttry.py` applied; `reference_lint.py` honours open frames as `specfreeze` does; Q06's count pin re-written with the Python file; the suites' fixtures adapted to Q04–Q06 being armed, as in DV2a.
- **CI** (DELTAS Δ140): the author run passes every job except `static`, which fails only at `chapter_lint.py --all`, and `paper` and `rubric`, which fail only on Q04–Q06's missing chapters, written pairs and rubrics (the DV3a kits phase); the study run likewise (`static` and `paper`). The empty-body job passes for all six modules, with the region gates and the Python TRIV gates.
- **The leak scan** (DELTAS Δ140; the author-only detail in the author notes §8): no answer of a Q04–Q06 hole, exit, test-out, agent or prediction item appears in a learner-visible file, and no exit statement appears in this plan.

### 13.6 The DV3a kits phase (integration lane, 2026-10-06)

*This section only points to DELTAS, which holds the phase's deviations for later phases; like the rest of this plan, it names no answer.*

- **The chapters, written pairs, rubrics, banks, calibration fixtures and leak patterns of Q04–Q06:** Δ142 (the three chapters as built, with §6 Q17's recorded runs), Δ143 (the written pairs, rubrics, banks with the moderate tier's viva draws, fixtures and leak patterns; Q04's and Q05's pairs hold a blueprint problem and Q06's the statistics of §11.3) and Δ144 (the integration: alignment, rubric pins, CI, the leak scan).
- **The follow-ups of DV3a's tool and records items:** Δ141 (Dafny opacity and Python statements frozen, the Python twin linted, the CI holes step, the region gate's dangling keyword, the skill simulations' tier filter, and the records lane's corrections to §5.1, §9.1, §9.3, §9.7 and §11.8).
- **Status.** Q04–Q06 stay `authoring`: their skill simulations and the author's grader calibration are still to come (Δ144, Open).

## 14. The BigNat spine: layer L4, and the detailed tier's conventions

*Written 2026-10-06 by the DV3b plan lane (this section and §15–§17; §5.2 holds their cross-module checks and §6 Q26–Q42 their open questions). The item tables are the contract for the DV3b module lanes. Like the rest of this plan, these sections name no answer, no exit statement and no tool behaviour that a Q07–Q09 prediction asks about; answers, gate results, candidate files and the evidence behind every claim are in `Solutions/plans/Q07-Q09-author-notes.md`.*

> **Exit tables.** §15.2, §16.2 and §17.2 end with each module's exit table, which names each exit item by its id, form, strand, language, file and minutes only (Δ133). The statements, `need:` lines and shipped checks are in the author notes from the first draft.

### 14.0 Conventions the detailed tier adds to §0 and §8.0

§0 and §8.0 stand (the moderate file order, the prediction block before the T block, the property-test and budget item forms, the std tier). The detailed tier adds these:

| what | form |
|---|---|
| **Rates** (spec §2.5; design §6.2 C1) | hole 8 min on average, itemized per item inside its line (§15.6, §16.6, §17.6); prediction 2.5–4 (a scenario with two or four cases, or a hand calculation, takes the upper end); WRITE card 2.5; worked example 5–8; exit item 5.5; explain-back 6 (Q07, Q08) or 3 (Q09). The line totals are design §7.5's |
| **Exit + explain-back** (design §7.1, "as moderate + explain-back") | the exit is the moderate one: 4 unseen items, 2 Dafny + 2 Verus, `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --exit` passes at ≥ 3/4 with ≥ 1/2 in each language (`firsttry`'s `EXIT_TIERS`, detailed = moderate, Δ102). After the exit is scored, the learner fills the `EXPLAIN-BACK` block at the end of the module's Dafny exit file: for one exit item that passed (Q07: a Verus one; Q08: a Dafny one; Q09: the learner's choice), at most 120 words on what the verifier needed and why each step is there. It is advisory: no tool reads it, and the Q09 tier viva opens on the three blocks (§17.7), as Q06's opened on the `AUDIT ROWS` |
| **Refinement hole** (Q08, Dafny) | a refining module (`module Q08_… refines QG_L4_Spec`) ships its definitions; a refined lemma that is a hole ships with the body `{ assume false; }` (the course's Dafny assume hole) and inherits its frozen statement from the abstract module, which the block's comment quotes. The hole is counted at the learner's line, once per refining module (§6 Q40). The learner replaces the body and may add helper lemmas to the refining module; the refined statement itself cannot be changed. Abstract modules live only in a given layer (L4's `QG_L4_Spec`): the learner-mode lint still flags an abstract module in a learner file (Δ3), so no Q07–Q09 learner file or specimen declares one |
| **Statement hole with a labelled assume** (Q08 trait specs) | a trait whose methods ship without `requires`/`ensures` (or with only the ones the item gives), a given implementation, and a given generic client whose body ends in one labelled `proof { #[verifier::proof_note("<item id>")] assume(false); }`. Done: write the trait's specs so that the implementation verifies against them and the client verifies **without** the assume, then delete the assume. Too strong a spec fails the implementation, too weak a spec fails the client; the assume is the tool-counted hole. The methods are `open_specs` in `expected/Q08.freeze` (§6 Q44), so added clauses are no freeze change; so is Q07.H7v's port, which ships with no `ensures` |
| **Construct forms** (§6 Q43) | a block whose construct a correct answer could otherwise avoid at the pin carries a `// form:` line; its prompt states the form in words, and `firsttry` checks it at `--check`, `--exit` and `--report --strict`. The items: Q07.H5v (`no-nl`), H6v and H7v (`bv-exec`); Q09.T1d and T1v (`trigger`, `stable`), H1d (`stable`), H1v (`nl-steps:2`, `stable`); the exits' forms are in the author notes |
| **Spec-definition hole** (Q07 port) | `pub uninterp spec fn <name>(…) -> …;` ships; `--no-cheating` counts it (Δ8); the learner replaces it with an `open spec fn` and its body. `specfreeze` compares it without `uninterp`/`open`/`closed` (its `open_bodies` rule, §6 Q7) |
| **Gated item** (Q08 tracked permissions) | an H item whose admission waits on the admissibility gate (design §7.5: complete reference, empty-body gate failing, 3-seed stable, the API at the pin). Its `[module.admissible]` entry carries `admitted`, its minutes (0 while not admitted) and a named fallback item that takes the minutes if the gate fails (§16.3) |
| **Repair item** (Q09 repair pair) | a proof hole whose block quotes, commented, an attempt and a `BRITTLENESS` table (rows: seed 1–3 × the bundled solvers for Dafny, seed 1–3 for Verus; columns: verdict, resource count). Done: the table filled from `scripts/vcheck --brittleness` runs (§17.5), then a proof that verifies under every row with no resource attribute. The table is reviewed; the proof and its forms (§6 Q43) are gated |
| **Forensic delivery** (Q09, S12) | as Q03's fake-green delivery specimens (Δ112, Δ118): a self-contained specimen holding one green delivery and one verdict slot for its species (`hole`, `trusted`, `unchecked`, `vacuous`, `weak` or `healthy`, spec §3.14), its planted line marked `// planted: <item id>`. The learner records the species (the first try), then catches the delivery with an instrument and fills that row of the fixed-width `DIAGNOSIS` form in `research/q09_forensics.md`: statement true? proof valid? theorem useful? instrument? root cause? N catches use N different instruments (spec §3.14 rule 3) |
| **Solo Python** (Q09 gate core) | guard rule 2s: `harness/q/fvh_q/gate/` is solo work, never on an allowlist; `/tutor` keeps its normal rungs (Q09 is not a challenge module). Holes are §8.0's Python holes; the acceptance tests are frozen (rule 2f) |
| **Std tier and Verus tiers** | every Dafny file of Q07–Q09 that reaches L4 is std tier (from its `[module.imports]` entry, §6 Q13, built). L4's Verus crate uses `vstd::arithmetic`, so every Verus file that reaches it is at least `arithmetic` tier (the check is transitive, Δ77); a file that uses `vstd::cell` is `all` |
| **Nonlinear arithmetic** | Q07's and Q08's Dafny modules carry `{:disableNonlinearArithmetic}` (the arithmetic-library lever, Δ46; each module states it, Δ31). In Q09, the trigger file `Q09_Triggers.dfy` and the agent module carry it, and the main Dafny exercise module and the exit module do not, each for a reason in the author notes |

### 14.1 Rules (design §10.1; spec §2.6; Δ22, Δ31, Δ34, Δ35, Δ44, Δ50, Δ54, Δ55, Δ94, Δ97, Δ130)

§1.1's six rules and §8.1's hold for L4. In addition:

1. **L4 re-supplies moderate-tier targets.** It holds, proved, the Q04–Q06 targets that L4 itself and later modules call (§14.5). By the tier law (§1.1 rule 3, Δ130) a detailed layer may hold the whole answers of moderate-tier holes. So L4 must never be opened before Q07: its header says so, and `CORE.md` already forbids an instructor to open or quote a layer above the record's `layer` (Δ90). No layer holds the answer of a Q01–Q03 exit or test-out item, and L4 holds the answer of no Q04–Q06 exit item either (§14.6), so that this plan never has to name an exit declaration (Δ133).
2. **Nothing L4 gives is posed again.** No Q07–Q18 module poses as a hole, exit or test-out item anything §14.2–§14.3 list. The detailed tier's holes are mechanism items (unfolding, nonlinear facts, bit-vectors, porting, refinement, trait specifications, permissions, a verified wp, brittleness, forensics, gates) that *use* the L4 objects; they never re-prove them.
3. **Std tier (Δ34).** The Dafny layer imports `Std.Arithmetic.Mul`, so its tier is `std` and only `std/` units reach it. `Std.Arithmetic.LittleEndianNat` stays excluded (Δ35): it holds near-answers for addition.
4. **Verus tier.** The Verus layer uses `vstd::arithmetic::mul`, so its import tier is `arithmetic` (not `prelude` like L1–L3), and every Verus file of Q07–Q18 that reaches it is `arithmetic` or wider (§14.0).
5. **Nonlinear arithmetic is off in every L4 module**, each module attributed explicitly (Δ31: the module attribute leaks into later modules of the same file only). The nonlinear facts its proofs need come from `Std.Arithmetic.Mul` / `vstd::arithmetic::mul`.
6. **The given role and the frozen statements (Δ97).** L4's abstract module `QG_L4_Spec` is refined, in the same file, by the concrete `QG_L4_Limbs`, which gives every member a body; so `vaudit --mode given` passes. The abstract module is the course's frozen statement layer: a refinement in a later module inherits its statements and cannot weaken them (Δ44).
7. **No answer in the header**, as for L1–L3 (Δ110): the headers name no import tier and explain no measure, lever, fuel, opacity or broadcast mechanism; the Q07 chapter does.
8. **Names.** L4's Verus addition is the free function `big_add` (Δ54: no inherent methods on `BigNat`); the reason for not naming it `add` is in the author notes.

### 14.2 L4, Dafny (tier: detailed; declared by Q07; imported by Q07–Q18)

`dafny/given/Q/QG_L4_BigNat.dfy`, three modules, each `{:disableNonlinearArithmetic}`; import tier `std`.

*Module `QG_L4_BigNat`* (`import opened QG_L1_BigNat`, `QG_L2_BigNat`, `QG_L3_BigNat`, `Std.Arithmetic.Mul`):

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `lemma PowAdd(m: nat, n: nat) ensures Pow(m + n) == Pow(m) * Pow(n)` (`decreases m`) | re-supplied, first posed as Q04.H1d |
| `lemma CmpCorrect(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) ensures -1 <= Cmp(a, b) <= 1 && (Cmp(a, b) < 0 <==> ToNat(a) < ToNat(b)) && (Cmp(a, b) == 0 <==> ToNat(a) == ToNat(b))` (`decreases \|a\| + \|b\|`) | re-supplied, first posed as Q04.H4d |
| `MulNonneg`, `MulZero`, `MulComm`, `MulLimbDigit(x, d, c)` (a carry out of one limb product is a limb), `DistribHorner(h, t, x)` (the Horner step distributes over a multiplier) | arithmetic facts by library calls |
| `opaque function AddC(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>, c: nat): seq<nat>` (`decreases \|a\| + \|b\|`); `function Add(a, b) = AddC(a, b, 0)` | schoolbook addition with a carry |
| `lemma {:induction false} AddCCorrect(a, b, c) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) && c <= 1 ensures Valid(AddC(a, b, c)) && ToNat(AddC(a, b, c)) == ToNat(a) + ToNat(b) + c`; `lemma AddCorrect(a, b)` (the same at carry 0) | addition's proof |
| `lemma AddCDropStep(a, b, i, c)` | one unfolding of `AddC` at position `i` |
| `method AddLoop(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) returns (r: seq<nat>) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) ensures r == Add(a, b) && Valid(r) && ToNat(r) == ToNat(a) + ToNat(b)` | the compiled loop, verified |
| `function MulLimbC(s: seq<nat>, d: nat, c: nat): seq<nat>` (`decreases \|s\|`); `function MulLimb(s, d) = MulLimbC(s, d, 0)` | multiplication by one limb |
| `lemma {:induction false} MulLimbCCorrect(s, d, c) requires Valid(s) && d < BASE && c < BASE ensures Valid(MulLimbC(s, d, c)) && ToNat(MulLimbC(s, d, c)) == ToNat(s) * d + c`; `lemma MulLimbCorrect(s, d)` | its proof |
| `method MulLimbLoop(s: seq<nat>, d: nat) returns (r: seq<nat>) requires Valid(s) && d < BASE ensures r == MulLimb(s, d) && Valid(r) && ToNat(r) == ToNat(s) * d` | the compiled loop, verified |

*Abstract module `QG_L4_Spec`* (the frozen statements any big-number implementation must provide): `type BigNat`; `ghost function Val(x: BigNat): nat`; `ghost predicate Wf(x: BigNat)`; `function Zero(): BigNat`, `FromNat(n: nat): BigNat`, `Add(a, b): BigNat`, `Cmp(a, b): int`; bodyless lemmas `ZeroCorrect() ensures Wf(Zero()) && Val(Zero()) == 0`, `FromNatCorrect(n) ensures Wf(FromNat(n)) && Val(FromNat(n)) == n`, `AddCorrect(a, b) requires Wf(a) && Wf(b) ensures Wf(Add(a, b)) && Val(Add(a, b)) == Val(a) + Val(b)`, `CmpCorrect(a, b) requires Wf(a) && Wf(b) ensures (Cmp(a, b) < 0 <==> Val(a) < Val(b)) && (Cmp(a, b) == 0 <==> Val(a) == Val(b))`.

*Module `QG_L4_Limbs refines QG_L4_Spec`* (the limb implementation): `BigNat = seq<nat>`, `Val = ToNat`, `Wf = Valid`, `Zero = []`, `FromNat` = L2's, `Add` = L4's, `Cmp` = L3's; the four lemmas proved by one call each.

No lemma of L4 carries an induction attribute beyond the two `{:induction false}` above; the two loops carry `{:isolate_assertions}`. The file holds no test and no live check whose value a later prediction asks about (§1.1 rule 5).

### 14.3 L4, Verus (tier: detailed; declared by Q07; imported by Q07–Q18)

Crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l4` (dependencies `vstd` at the pin, `qg_bignat_l1`, `qg_bignat_l2`, `qg_bignat_l3`; `build = false`; `[package.metadata.verus] verify = true`), `src/lib.rs` with `pub use qg_bignat_l3::*;` and `use vstd::arithmetic::mul::*;`, import tier `arithmetic`:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `pub proof fn lemma_weight_add(m: nat, n: nat) ensures weight(m + n) == weight(m) * weight(n)` (`decreases m`) | re-supplied, first posed as Q04.H1v |
| `pub proof fn lemma_seq_to_nat_append(a: Seq<u64>, b: Seq<u64>) ensures seq_to_nat(a + b) == seq_to_nat(a) + weight(a.len()) * seq_to_nat(b)` (`decreases a.len()`) | re-supplied, first posed as Q06.H3v |
| `pub fn cmp(a: &BigNat, b: &BigNat) -> (r: Ordering) requires a.wf(), b.wf(), ensures` the three results agree with the order of `to_nat()` | re-supplied, first posed as Q04.H4v |
| `pub proof fn lemma_add_lift(a, b, r: Seq<u64>, c: Seq<nat>)` — per-limb relations `limb_at(a, j) + limb_at(b, j) + c[j] == r[j] + BASE * c[j + 1]` give `seq_to_nat(r) == seq_to_nat(a) + seq_to_nat(b) + c[0]` (`decreases r.len()`) | the carry lifting of addition |
| `pub fn big_add(a: &BigNat, b: &BigNat) -> (r: BigNat) requires a.wf(), b.wf(), ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == a.to_nat() + b.to_nat()` | addition, verified |
| `pub proof fn lemma_mul_limb_lift(a: Seq<u64>, x: nat, r: Seq<u64>, c: Seq<nat>)` — per-limb relations `limb_at(a, j) * x + c[j] == r[j] + BASE * c[j + 1]` give `seq_to_nat(r) == seq_to_nat(a) * x + c[0]` | the carry lifting of `mul_limb` |
| `pub fn mul_limb(a: &BigNat, x: u64) -> (r: BigNat) requires a.wf(), ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == a.to_nat() * x` | multiplication by one word, verified |
| `pub trait NatLike: Sized` with `spec fn valid(&self) -> bool`, `spec fn value(&self) -> nat` and the exec methods `zero`, `from_word(x: u64)`, `plus(&self, other: &Self)`, `times_word(&self, x: u64)`, `compare(&self, other: &Self) -> Ordering`, each with its `requires`/`ensures` | the interface a natural-number implementation offers |
| `impl NatLike for BigNat` (`valid` = `wf`, `value` = `to_nat`, the bodies `from_u64`, `big_add`, `mul_limb`, `cmp`) | its implementation for the spine |

No L4 crate or file holds an `external_body`, `assume`, `admit` or `{:axiom}`, so `expected/verus_trust.toml` stays absent (Δ95). Full multiplication is not in L4 (Δ50: it stays a candidate for L5–L7 or a capstone; §14.7 F13).

### 14.4 Who imports what

| layer | tier | declared by (`[module.imports]`) | imported by | Dafny unit tier | Verus tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | survey | Q01 | Q01–Q18 | core | prelude |
| L2 | intro | Q02 | Q02–Q18 | core | prelude |

Generated units (`gen_units.py`, Δ78): `dafny/Q/Q07_InternalsI/std/dfyconfig.toml`, `dafny/Q/Q08_InternalsII/std/dfyconfig.toml`, `dafny/Q/Q09_InternalsIII/std/dfyconfig.toml` (L1–L4; std options: `standard-libraries`, manual lemma induction); `verus/q/q07_internals_i`, `q08_internals_ii`, `q09_internals_iii` each depending on `vstd` and `qg_bignat_l1`–`l4`. `verus/Cargo.toml` gains `given/qg_bignat_l4` and the three module crates.

### 14.5 What L4 re-supplies, and §8.7 F6

- **F6 kept:** Dafny `CmpCorrect` (`QG_L4_Limbs.CmpCorrect` calls it) and Verus `cmp` (`NatLike::compare` calls it); Dafny `PowAdd` and Verus `lemma_weight_add` and `lemma_seq_to_nat_append` (the Q07–Q09 references and Q08's flagship delivery call them).
- **Not re-supplied:** Dafny `ShiftBound`, `PrefixBounds`, `TwoShifts`; Verus `lemma_shift_bound`, `lemma_value_times_limb`, `lemma_first_difference`; the Q05 targets; every Q04–Q06 exit target (rule 1). No later item needs them; a later layer may still re-supply them (§1.1 rule 3).
- **Kept out of L4–L5, as decided for L3 (§8.5, F8):** Dafny `NormalizeLoop` and Verus `normalize`, so that Q10 can pose them. No L4 code normalizes (Verus `big_add` and `mul_limb` produce canonical results by construction).
- **New in L4 (never posed):** addition and multiplication by one limb with their proofs and loops, the arithmetic facts, the frozen abstract module and its limb refinement, the trait and its implementation. Design §10.1 lists them as the detailed tier's spine; F11–F13 (§14.7) record what that costs later modules.

### 14.6 Proof that L4 answers no item of a module that imports it

1. **The layer is listed exhaustively** in §14.2–§14.3.
2. **No L4 declaration has the statement of a Q07–Q09 item.** For every T, H and X item of §15–§17, no L4 declaration has the same or a stronger `ensures` (table below).
3. **No L4 proof is an item's whole answer.** The re-supplied proofs answer Q04 and Q06 holes (tier law: allowed, Δ130), never a Q07–Q09 item, and never an exit item of Q01–Q06 (checked against the exit statements in both author notes, Δ133).
4. **No L4 code answers an exec, annotation or statement hole of Q07–Q09.** L4's exec code is `cmp`, `big_add`, `mul_limb`, `AddLoop`, `MulLimbLoop` and the `NatLike` implementation; no Q07–Q09 item asks for any of them. Where an item builds on an L4 object (Q08's refinements and generic clients, Q08's flagship), the item's target is a new statement about it.
5. **Techniques shared on purpose** (named in the author notes with the items): L4's carry splits use `%` and `/` on a double word, Q07's bit-vector items use shifts and masks; L4's limb-product bound and Q07's division-by-a-limb items call different library lemmas on different statements; L4's limb-digit fact and Q09's proof-split items bound products on different statements, with a constant bound in L4 and a symbolic one in Q09.

| item(s) | kind of target | same statement in L4 | L4 code answering it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q07.T1d / T1v, H1d, H4v | the value of a short limb literal; an addition on literals | no (L4 states general facts) | none |
| Q07.H2d, H3d, H5v | division by one limb; division by the next weight | no | none |
| Q07.H6v, H7v–H9v | a double-word split; the port of `FromNat`, its mirror | no (no conversion from a double word in L1–L4) | none |
| Q07.X1d, X2d, X1v, X2v | exit items (statements in the author notes) | no | none |
| Q08.T1d, T1v, H1d, H2d, H7d | a carry by subset types; refinements of `QG_L4_Spec` with another well-formedness; a trait spec | no (`QG_L4_Limbs` refines with `Valid`; the items refine with another representation invariant) | none (`QG_L4_Limbs`'s one-call proofs do not prove the items' refined lemmas) |
| Q08.H3v, H4v, H5v, H6v | a trait spec; a generic client; permissioned cells | no | none |
| Q08.H8d–H10d | the verified wp | no (L1–L4 hold no language) | none |
| Q08.X1d, X2d, X1v, X2v | exit items | no | none |
| Q09.T1d / T1v, H1d / H1v | monotonicity under a quantified step; a product bound | no | none |
| Q09.P6d–P15v | forensic deliveries (verdicts) | — | none |
| Q09.H2py–H5py | the gate core (Python) | — | none |
| Q09.X1d, X2d, X1v, X2v | exit items | no | none |

### 14.7 Forward constraints (for DV4–DV7)

- **F11.** No module Q07–Q18 poses anything §14.2–§14.3 list (rule 2).
- **F12.** L4 proves addition (both languages) and multiplication by one limb (both languages), so design §7.5's Q16 H1 ("generalize before induction via the add carry", Dafny) and Q16 H3 (`mul_limb`, Verus, with a Dafny mirror) cannot be posed as written. DV5 re-plans Q16 H1 and H3 when it plans Q16 (candidates in the author notes: full multiplication `mul`/`MulCorrect`, which Δ50 measured and which no layer gives; division by one limb; a borrow generalization), with Q16 H2's switch to `cmp_any` (§8.7 F6, §6 Q14).
- **F13.** Full multiplication is in no layer through L4. If DV5 poses it in Q16, no layer from L5 on may give it to Q10–Q16; if a later layer re-supplies it, only modules after that layer's first module may use it.
- **F14.** Q08's flagship puts the learner's own limb shifts and splits (§16.4) into the learner's agent file. No Q10–Q18 item poses the same statements; a later layer that needs them re-supplies its own proofs.
- **F15.** Q07, Q08 and Q09 are one tier (§8.7 F7 for the moderate tier): no Q07–Q09 file holds another Q07–Q09 item's answer, and no given code of the tier (agent files and their twins, exit scaffolding and card demos included) answers a Q07–Q09 item or shows an exit's proof pattern. Every module lane re-runs the check of the author notes §2.7 (each T, H and X statement and each reference line against every agent file, twin, exit file and specimen of the tier) on its files as built. The shared techniques are named in the author notes.
- **F16.** Every Dafny file of Q10–Q18 that reaches L4 is std tier, and every Verus file is `arithmetic` or wider (rule 4).

Agent rung 7: refactorer under harness, with a Qwen replay in the sandbox. Card 02.8. The D→V port with its fidelity audit (design §6.5) has its own 35-minute line.*

### 15.3 Constructs (registry)

- `write_d = ["fuel", "arithmetic-library"]`, `write_v = ["fuel", "arithmetic-library", "bit-vector"]`: 3 units (2 paired, 1 Verus), 5 slugs.
- `recognise_d = ["isolate", "bv-types"]`, `recognise_v = ["recommends"]`, disjoint from WRITE. **This departs from design §7.4 and the DV1a record**, which list Dafny `isolate` and Verus `recommends` as WRITE: on the pinned toolchain neither construct yields the two checked hole uses a WRITE construct needs (the probes and the reasons are in the author notes; §6 Q26 asks the orchestrator to adopt the change). Each keeps a card and a prediction.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): fuel d T1d, H1d, X2d · v T1v, H4v, H9v; arithmetic-library d H2d, H3d, X1d · v H5v, X2v; bit-vector v H6v, H7v, X1v. The Verus arithmetic-library and bit-vector uses carry `form:` lines (`no-nl`, `bv-exec`; §6 Q43), because an answer that avoids the construct can verify at the pin (the author notes §0.2 name each); the other uses need their construct at the pin (nonlinear arithmetic off, the opacity of `AddC` and `weight`, unfolding).
- **Usage by strand.** Every item's strand is at least the strand of every construct it uses: fuel (S4) is used by S4 items, the arithmetic library (S5) by S5 items, bit-vectors (S1) by S1 items; H7v also uses Q06's `property-test` (an earlier tier).
- **Hand before automation** (design §6.3), by file order: C1d and C1v unfold by hand before the fuel attribute; C2d and C2v name each library fact before any automation is mentioned (none is used in Q07's holes); C3v shows a hand route before `bit_vector`; T1d/T1v (closed book) come before C1.
- **Given code** (no item): the two loops, the arithmetic facts and the frozen spec stay in L4; Q07's files add only W1d, W1v, the check unit's two tests and the port's mutants.

### 15.4 Agentic file (rung 7: refactorer under harness, with a Qwen replay)

- **Shape:** green, no hole (a refactoring task; spec §5.1: "the learner reviews the diff for silently weakened statements"). Each file holds verified but monolithic proofs (the same reasoning inlined in several lemmas or exec fns) about compositions of L4's operations, in two sections: `FROZEN` (statements the contract pins: the snapshot holds exactly this section) and `API` (statements that callers rely on but that no acceptance line pins). A weakened `API` statement can pass every machine-checked acceptance line, so only a reader is sure to catch it: that is what `REVIEW` is for. An empty `HELPERS` section takes the new lemmas.
- **Before the runs (the learner):** copy the `FROZEN` section (the module header and its statements) to `research/q07_frozen.dfy` (and `research/q07_frozen.rs`) and commit them, in a commit that leaves the agent files as shipped. That commit is the starting state of both runs: the `/contract` run starts from it, and `contract_local.sh` stages the allowed file from it (DV4 §6 step 3), never from the tree after the `/contract` run.
- **CONTRACT** (line 1, three keys; each machine-checkable line in a form `contract_local.sh` checks, DV4 §6): `goal:` refactor the proofs so that the shared reasoning lives in helper lemmas under `HELPERS` and no proof body is longer than the `REFACTOR` comment's bound; change no statement · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line `` · zero holes · `` `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q07_frozen.dfy <file>` reports no change `` (Verus: `.rs`) · `` `scripts/vaudit <file>` reports no finding `` · `git diff` from the snapshot's commit changes no line of a `FROZEN` statement, and every new lemma sits under `HELPERS` (an audit line: its evidence is the diff, `patch.diff` for the replay) · no `{:fuel}` attribute is added to any lemma (an audit line; resource attributes are `vaudit` findings) · re-verify after every edit.
- **The Qwen replay** (A2d, pending until DV4 step 3b, §6 Q31): after the `/contract` run, the learner runs `scripts/contract_local.sh Q07 --file dafny/Q/Q07_InternalsI/agent/Q07_AgentRefactor.dfy` in their own terminal. It replays the same CONTRACT under opencode and the local model in a per-run container, from the snapshot's commit, with the snapshot read-only at its own path and `scripts/specfreeze` among the trusted tools, so the agent can run its own acceptance; it re-checks the machine-checkable lines in the network-less verification container and writes the patch and the run record to `research/runs/Q07/<run>/`. The patch is data: it is never applied to the tree (N2). The module's budget allows one Qwen run (`qwen_runs = 1`, `qwen_calls = 40`, `gpu_hours = 1`). The chapter quotes no replay output until a recorded run exists (Δ121).
- **Learner follow-up (15 min):** snapshot and commit (2); review the Claude run's diff and fill `REVIEW` (5): every changed line, every `API` statement (weaker than before? a conjunct dropped? an attribute added?), every new helper's statement (weaker than what its callers need? a `requires` no caller can establish?); audit the Qwen patch the same way from `patch.diff` and its run record (5) and fill `COMPARISON` (3): which acceptance lines each run met, which statements each changed, what only a reader could catch.

### 15.5 The D→V port and its fidelity audit (35 min)

Design §6.5's two instruments, on one small object: L2's `FromNat` (Dafny, with L3's `FromNatCorrect`) ported to Verus for inputs below 2^128 (`port.rs`).

1. **The port (H7v, 13 min).** Transcribe the contract, write the body. Done: the gate and the commented use-site check (concrete calls whose results the contract must determine).
2. **Differential execution (4 min).** The Dafny check unit runs `FromNat` on a fixed sample of six inputs and `expect`s the limbs (given; `scripts/vcheck --test dafny/checks/Q07_checks.dfy`). The learner copies the same rows into the commented `#[cfg(test)]` suite below `port.rs`'s `verus!` block and runs `scripts/vcheck --test verus/q/q07_internals_i` (Dafny compiled to Python against Verus compiled by `verus --compile`, design §6.5). H7v's `uses:` names `property-test`, so `firsttry --report --strict` runs the suite (§6 Q20, built).
3. **Mutant-kill parity (6 min).** `port.rs` ships three mutant bodies of `from_u128`, commented under `MUTANTS`. For each, the learner swaps it in (a scratch copy), records whether the Verus contract rejects it (`scripts/vcheck`), and whether the Dafny contract rejects the matching Dafny output (the check unit's second given test prints `Valid` and `ToNat` of each mutant's outputs; it `expect`s nothing, so the learner reads the printed lines and decides). The `FIDELITY` block records the table: a mutant one contract kills and the other does not is a fidelity finding.
4. **The mirror (H8v, H9v; 12 min).** An independently transcribed mirror of `FromNat`'s *definition* (not of H7v's code) and the implication lemma: the mirror's output meets the port's contract. The `FIDELITY` block states the result "relative to the mirror".

The `FIDELITY` block's last line answers: what does the port promise that the source does not, or the reverse? It is advisory (rubric and viva). Neither the sample nor any learner-visible file of Q07 contains an input whose `FromNat` value a Q01–Q06 prediction asks about (§1.1 rule 4).

`budget` changes `qwen_calls` from DV1a's 0 to 40: an agent run is a sequence of model calls, DV4's Ollama gate counts every request of a run (turns, titles and compaction included), and the turn cap is set no higher than this (DV4 §6). Q07 arms with these fields while A2d is pending (§6 Q31): `engines` names `agent` from the start, and `scripts/contract_local.sh Q07 --available` tells the learner whether the replay can run yet. Every other field keeps its DV1a value (`review_bar`, `persona`, `viva_mode = "none"`, `card = ["02.8"]`, `admissible = {}`). The open statement of H7v lives in `expected/Q07.freeze` (`open_specs`, §6 Q44), not in the registry.

## 16. Q08 — Internals II: refinement, permissions, verified wp

*Tier: detailed, family B (S6–S8), layer L4, 270 min. Exit: 2 D + 2 V unseen items, ≥ 3/4 and ≥ 1/2 in each language, plus the explain-back. Agent rung 8: the **spec-to-proof flagship** (the learner writes the statements; the contractor delivers implementation and proofs). Card 02.1. The verified wp for assignment, sequence and `if` (Dafny; loops stay on paper) has its own 30-minute line. The permission items are gated (§16.3).*

### 16.3 Constructs (registry) and the gated items

- `write_d = ["refines"]`, `write_v = ["trait-spec", "tracked"]`: 3 units (single-language), 3 slugs. `recognise_d = recognise_v = []`.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): refines d H1d, H2d, H7d, X1d; trait-spec v T1v, H3v, H4v, X1v; tracked v H5v, H6v.
- **The gated items** (design §7.5's admissibility gate; design §5's permissions row "gated"). H5v and H6v enter with the module only when the module lane's run shows, on the shipped forms and the twins: the reference verifies, the empty-body gate (TRIV; INV where a loop exists) fails, the twins are stable over seeds 1–3, and the `vstd::cell` API they use is the pinned one. The plan's probes passed the first two (author notes §3.2). The record starts with `admitted = false` and 0 minutes for both (§16.7: a gated item carries no minutes until admitted). If the run passes, the module lane records the four results and only then writes `admitted = true` with their minutes; if not, the entries stay as they are and the fallbacks take the 14 minutes: **F-H5v** a `Ghost`-history counter (`struct Tally { n: u64, log: Ghost<Seq<u64>> }`, `bump` keeping `n == log.len()`), **F-H6v** a two-field transfer on a struct the module owns (`&mut self`, no permission). With the fallbacks, `tracked` moves from `write_v` to `recognise_v` (its card and P2v stay), and §5.2's slug count drops by one.
- **Usage by strand.** refines (S6) by S6 items; trait-spec (S6) by S6 items; tracked (S8) by S8 items. The verified-wp holes use no WRITE slug (their construct is the program-logic strand itself).
- **Hand before automation.** The refinement holes come after W1d shows a client of the frozen statements; the wp line works by hand (P5dv, the paper lab) before H8d–H10d mechanize it.
- **Loops stay on paper.** The written pair's B-problems state the loop rule and its side conditions by hand; no Q08 file models a loop in the wp language.

### 16.4 Agentic file (rung 8: the spec-to-proof flagship)

- **Shape:** spec-first in reverse (spec §5.1, "the learner writes the signatures and statements and reviews them for non-vacuity; the agent delivers implementation and proofs"). The agent files ship green with no hole: a header, the CONTRACT block, and three empty sections. `STATEMENTS` names the operations to specify, limb shifts and splits (Dafny: shift left by `k` limbs, and the split at `k` limbs into the low part and the part above it; Verus: shift left by `k` limbs and the limbs above the low `k`, each on canonical `BigNat`s). `WITNESSES` holds the learner's non-vacuity checks. `HELPERS` is for the contractor.
- **Before the run (the learner, 14 min):** write each operation's signature and correctness statement in `STATEMENTS`: in Dafny a bodyless **method** whose `ensures` clause states its correctness (one clause, joined by `&&`, as every Dafny hole carries, Δ5), never a bodyless function, whose body would count as part of its statement (§6 Q45); in Verus an `external_body` stub with its `requires` and `ensures`. Write `WITNESSES` (concrete calls showing every `requires` satisfiable, and every statement meaningful on a non-trivial input); copy the file to `research/q08_statements.dfy` (`.rs`) and **commit** both.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` implement and prove every statement in `STATEMENTS`; change no statement · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 and prints only the summary line `` · zero holes · `` `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q08_statements.dfy <file>` reports no change `` (Verus: `.rs`) · `` `scripts/vaudit <file>` reports no finding `` · `git diff` against the committed statements changes only bodies and the `HELPERS` section · every `WITNESSES` check verifies unchanged · new helper functions and lemmas only under `HELPERS` · re-verify after every edit. The plan re-ran these lines on the agent twins in this form (the author notes §R.1).
- **Learner follow-up (6 min):** audit the delivery: the freeze check, the diff's scope, each helper's statement (does any helper assume what it should prove?), the witnesses, and the transcript (did the contractor ask to change a statement? did it stop and report a statement it could not prove?). Record in `AUDIT` which statement cost the contractor most and why.
- **What the learner gains:** the agent file afterwards holds the learner's own verified shifts and splits (§14.7 F14).

### 16.5 The verified wp (30 min)

`Q08_Wp.dfy` (Dafny; design §7.4: "a verified wp for assign, sequence and if; loops stay on paper"). W2d gives the language and its semantics; H8d proves the substitution lemma, H9d the soundness of `Wp`, H10d uses soundness to verify a three-assignment program by computing its precondition through `Wp`. The paper lab (the pair's B1) does the same calculation by hand first and states the loop rule; P5dv is its checked item.

A lane that copies this record copies no admission. The Q08 lane changes an entry to `admitted = true` with its minutes (H5v 6, H6v 8) only after it has recorded the four admissibility results for that item (§16.3, §6 Q34); if a result fails, the entry stays and the fallback item takes the minutes. `budget` and every other field keep their DV1a values (`card = ["02.1"]`, `viva_mode = "none"`). The open statements of T1v and H3v live in `expected/Q08.freeze` (`open_specs`, §6 Q44).

## 17. Q09 — Internals III: brittleness, forensics, gate core

*Tier: detailed, family C (S9–S13), layer L4, 295 min + the detailed-tier viva (30 min, `viva_mode = "tier"`, pool Q07–Q09). Exit: 2 D + 2 V unseen items, ≥ 3/4 and ≥ 1/2 in each language, plus the explain-back. Agent rung 9: the red-teamed contractor, then contract v1. Card 07.2. Python returns as the tool language: the learner's gate core is solo work (guard rule 2s). The module has no "holes" line: its proof holes are the test-out items, the repair pair and the exit; its Python holes are the gate core.*

### 17.3 Constructs (registry)

- `write_d = write_v = ["proof-split", "trigger-rewrite"]`: 2 paired units, 4 slugs. `recognise_d = recognise_v = []`.
- Checked uses (≥ 2 per language): proof-split d H1d, X1d · v H1v, X1v; trigger-rewrite d T1d, X2d · v T1v, X2v. Every one carries a `form:` line (§6 Q43): `stable` on all of them; `nl-steps:2` on the Verus proof-split uses and `trigger` on the trigger-rewrite uses, because on those an answer without the construct can verify at the pin (the author notes §0.2 name each). Where a form checks only that the construct is present, the citation is marked `# nominal:` in §17.11.
- **Usage by strand.** Both constructs are S11's; every item that uses them is S11.
- **Hand before automation.** C1 and C2 show the hand measurement before the repair; the repair pair's tables are filled before any proof is written.
- **The forensic instruments** (spec §11.4's list): the audit, the lint, the spec-freeze diff, a satisfiability witness, swapping the conclusion for `false`, running compiled code on inputs the delivery's tests avoided, a property sampler, reading the statement, JSON completeness, re-verifying under another seed or solver. Ten deliveries: each catch uses a different instrument (spec §3.14 rule 3), and no healthy delivery may be accused (design §5 E4). The deliveries use BigNat objects; no delivery is a copy of a cheat-corpus fixture (§17.6).

### 17.4 Agentic file (rung 9: the red-teamed contractor, then contract v1)

- **Shape:** green-with-holes bank (spec §3.11.1). Each file's `BANK` holds four lemmas about L1–L4 objects, as holes; **one of the four is plausible but false** (spec §5.1: "the contractor must not weaken it"). The author notes name it; this plan does not.
- **Before the run (the learner, 5 min):** fill the `DIAGNOSIS` block: for each bank lemma, true or false, and for a false one the smallest counterexample you would expect. Copy the bank to `research/q09_bank.dfy` (`.rs`) and commit.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` prove the lemmas of `BANK`; a lemma you find false is not to be proved, weakened or restated: leave it as shipped, add under `REFUTATIONS` a lemma that proves its negation on concrete values, and say so in one line of your report · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` `scripts/vcheck --json <file>` lists as holes exactly the lemmas the report names as false, and no other hole · every other bank lemma verifies · each named lemma has a verified refutation under `REFUTATIONS` · `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q09_bank.dfy <file>` (Verus: `.rs`) reports no change · `scripts/vaudit <file>` reports exactly one finding for each lemma the report names as false, of a hole kind (Dafny: `audit-bodyless-ghost` on that lemma's declaration line; Verus: `hole-assume` on its labelled assume line), and no other finding · `git diff` against the committed bank changes only bodies and the `REFUTATIONS` section · re-verify after every edit. **The CONTRACT never says which lemma is false**, and no acceptance line names a lemma: a correct delivery leaves the false lemma's hole open, so a pinned audit report would name it. Every line was run on the agent twins with the false lemma left as shipped, in both languages, and held (the author notes §R.1).
- **Learner follow-up (8 min):** audit the run: did the contractor find the false lemma, and with what evidence (a refutation that verifies, or prose only)? did it weaken, restate or `assume` its way past it? did it touch a true lemma's statement? Fill `AUDIT` with the transcript moments.
- **Contract v1 (12 min, by hand, no AI session):** write `### Q-v1 — Q09, <date>` below the learner marker in `CLAUDE.md` (spec §5.2's slot format): 3–6 numbered rules, each earned by an observed transcript moment of a Q04–Q09 contract run, and a "Done means" line a third party can re-run. It adds to v0 and the fallback rules; the stricter wins.

### 17.5 Brittleness: the measurement and the repair (25 min)

The **measurement** is a table per repair item: Dafny, seeds 1–3 under the pinned Z3 4.12.1 and under the other bundled Z3 4.14.1 (both pinned by hash, `VERSIONS.md` §1.1); Verus, seeds 1–3 (Verus checks its Z3 version, so it has one solver, `VERSIONS.md` §1.2). Each cell holds the verdict and the resource count from `scripts/vcheck --brittleness` (`--json` for the counts). It needs one tool change (§6 Q29): `vcheck --solver <bundled version>` and `vcheck --brittleness --solvers`, Dafny only, refused under `--gate` (the done gate keeps the pin). The **repair** (proof-split, both languages) must verify in every cell with no resource attribute.

The course's brittleness rule from Q09 on (DV0-REPORT §8 item 18): a proof is *stable* when it verifies under seeds 1–3 and, in Dafny, under both bundled solvers. The resource spread (coefficient of variation) is reported, never gating; the tools change of §6 Q29 flags a spread above 20%.

### 17.6 Forensics (40 min)

Ten specimens (§17.2), each a green delivery of a BigNat fact. The families are the Q half of the cheat corpus (design §9.2; Δ108: the Q half and the R half each cover every X family of their language), applied to new objects, so no delivery is a corpus fixture. Order of work for each: read the delivery and its claimed statement; record the species in its slot (`scripts/firsttry.sh Q09 --check`: the first try); then catch it with an instrument and fill its `DIAGNOSIS` row (statement true? proof valid? theorem useful? instrument? root cause?). The slots are hash-checked; the rows are graded by the rubric and examined at the viva.

**Predictions first, for longer.** `tests/corpus/` (its manifests record which instrument flags which construct) and `harness/tests/q/fixtures/q09/` join the README's *Predictions first* list from Q09 on: leave them closed until the ten forensic first tries are recorded (§6 Q32).

### 17.7 The gate core (40 min) and what it builds on

`harness/q/fvh_q/gate/core.py` is the learner's first gate (gate v1; Q18's gate v2 extends it with crate scale, the option allowlist and the build-tool checks, design §11.2). It is solo (rule 2s). It builds on frozen core code only (design §9.2 rule 4: statistics code lives in the core):

| it composes | from | provided by |
|---|---|---|
| `RunRecord`, `first_attempts` | `fvh.records` (§11.8) | DV3a (built) |
| `wilson_interval` | `fvh.stats` (§11.8) | DV3a (built) |
| the gate words `PASS`, `FAIL`, `UNKNOWN`, `GATE_WORDS`; `XCLASSES` (X01–X18); `GRADER_ONLY` (X07, X08, X14), as plain data | `fvh.verdicts` | DV4, build step 1a (assigned, §6 Q33) |
| `load_half(lang, half)`: the corpus manifests, each fixture a `Delivery` (id, language, half, target, files: the task and its companions) and a `Label` (X class, kind, title, the expected outcome per instrument) kept apart | `fvh.corpus` | DV4, build step 1a |
| `run(instrument, target, *, root=None, timeout_s=None, runner=None) -> ToolResult`: one instrument of `INSTRUMENTS` (the corpus README's names: `vcheck --gate`, `vaudit`, `specfreeze --check`, `witness`, `swap-to-false`, `seed-rerun`) in a fresh process; a kill or a time-out is returned as such (Δ57, Δ58), never as a pass; the provenance rule sends any target of `agent` provenance to the verification container | `fvh.tools` | DV4, build step 1a |
| `replay(fixture_id, instrument) -> ToolResult`: the recorded result of one instrument on one Q-half fixture, for the frozen tests (no solver runs in the acceptance tests); the recorded files hold results only, never a label | `fvh.tools.replay` over `harness/tests/q/fixtures/q09/<lang>/<ID>/` | DV4, build step 1a (the reader and the author-only recorder); the Q09 lane (the recording) |
| `GateVerdict(word, unknown)` (`word` is `PASS` or `FAIL`; `unknown` the grader-only classes left undecided), `GateResult`, `Report` | `fvh_q.gate.core`, given above the holes and frozen by `expected/Q09.freeze` | the Q09 lane |

**The gate decides from instruments, never from labels.** On a real delivery (Q18's gate v2, the labs) no class is known, so the gate is written as it will be used there: `classify` and `gate` see instrument results and the delivery's own files, and only `corpus_report`, the evaluator, reads the corpus labels. The printed verdict of a delivery that every instrument passes is a mechanical `PASS` with its `unknown` classes beside it, never a bare `PASS` (spec §5.6): a grader-only class leaves `unknown` only when the task supplies the oracle that decides it (the block states which).

The acceptance tests (`harness/tests/q/test_q09_gate.py`, frozen) check the four functions on synthetic documents and on the replayed Q-half results: `classify` is called with results only; every Q-half construct whose recorded instruments flag it gets `FAIL`; every control gets the mechanical `PASS`; a grader-only construct that the instruments pass and a healthy control with the same instruments get the same `GateVerdict`, word and `unknown` both; `gate` called on a fixture's `Delivery` returns the same result whatever its `Label` holds; `corpus_report` counts each grader-only construct as held by `unknown` (never as a `PASS`), counts none as held by nothing, and takes its false-positive rate and interval from `fvh.stats`. They replace `fvh.tools.run` by a recording stub and require that `gate` calls it (no instrument re-implemented in learner code). A live run over the Q half (`python -m fvh_q.gate.core --half Q`, about five minutes, one solver job at a time, on the host: corpus fixtures are `course` targets) is the learner's own check, outside the tests.

### 17.8 Itemized minutes (total 295, design §7.5; the tier viva is 30 more)

| line | items | min |
|---|---|---|
| setup | | 5 |
| test-out | T1d, T1v (2 × 5) | 10 |

H1d, H3py and the forensic deliveries are timed first in the calibration run.

### 17.9 `EVAL.md` v1 and the MDE on paper

- **`EVAL.md` v1** (`research/EVAL.md`, section `Q-v1`; design §10.3): the learner's second evaluation report. Claim: the share of the learner's own Dafny proofs of Q04–Q09 (every hole they filled) that are stable in §17.5's sense. Metric and estimator: a proportion with its Wilson interval (`fvh.stats`); n: the number of proofs; exclusions: none (a resource-limit hit counts as unstable); cost: solver time from the runs; disclosure: one machine, one solver pair, three seeds, the learner as author of the proofs measured.
- **MDE on paper** (the pair's B-problem, 10 min): the minimum detectable difference of a two-arm comparison at a given n, by the formula and then by `fvh.stats.mde_two_proportions`, and what that means for Q12's lab (design §11.2). The numbers differ from P4py's.

### 17.10 The detailed-tier gate and viva (30 min, outside the 295)

- **Who sits it.** `viva_mode = "tier"` on Q09 only: `/viva Q09` examines Q07, Q08 and Q09; `/viva Q07` and `/viva Q08` refuse and name Q09, as `/viva Q04` names Q06.
- **The pool.** Every `viva: yes` question of the three detailed-tier banks (`.claude/skills/instructor/banks/Q07.md`, `Q08.md`, `Q09.md`, written with the chapters). Q09's bank tables the draws under *Tier viva draws*, as Q06's does: pairwise disjoint, each with at least one question from each module, at least one verifier-refereed predict, at least one backward edge, no strand twice.
- **The opening** (ungraded, before the draw): the three `EXPLAIN-BACK` blocks (§14.0). The viva asks the learner to defend one of them against a probe the examiner runs through `scripts/vcheck`.
- **The exit** of each module stays its own: `scripts/firsttry.sh <ID> --exit`, 4 unseen items, at least 3 of 4 and at least 1 of 2 in each language.
