## 19. The practice tiers: layers L5–L7 and their conventions

*Written 2026-10-07 by the DV5 plan lane (this section and §20–§30; §29 holds the cross-module checks of Q10–Q18 and §30 their open questions, Q47 on, with DV5's dispositions of §6 Q14, Q35 and Q39 and of DELTAS Δ158's re-plan). The item tables are the contract for the DV5 module lanes. Like the rest of this plan, these sections name no answer, no exit statement, no statement the learner writes from prose or generalizes, and no tool behaviour that a prediction asks about. Answers, references, gate results, line counts and the evidence behind every claim are in `Solutions/plans/Q10-Q18-author-notes.md`; its Appendix A reproduces every probed reference, so nothing below depends on the lane's scratch tree.*

*Revised 2026-10-07 by the DV5 plan lane after the plan review (four blockers, fifteen majors, minors): the line-count rule (§19.0), the fallback rule (§19.0), L6's `Sub` and L7's definitions (§19.3–§19.4), Q11's VC items (§21), Q12's specimens and Lab 1 (§22), Q13.H5d (§23), Q14's file order and H2v, H3v, H5v (§24), Lab 2's feedback (§25), Q16's H3 (§26), Lab 3's tasks (§28) and the checks (§29). Each disposition, with the evidence of the revision's probes and the review items not applied, is in the author notes §13.*

> **Exit tables.** §20.2, §21.2, §22.2, §23.2, §24.2 and §25.2 end with each module's exit table, which names each timed item by its id, form, strand, language, file and minutes only (Δ133). Q16–Q18 have no exit file (§19.0).

### 19.0 Conventions the practice tiers add to §0, §8.0 and §14.0

§0, §8.0 and §14.0 stand. The practice tiers add these forms; the tool changes and decisions they raise are §30.

| what | form |
|---|---|
| **Difficulty** (spec §2.8; design §7.1) | *intro practice* (Q10–Q12): the statement ships; one hole per item; a reference of at most 10 proof lines (Dafny) or 15 (Verus); no invented lemma; 8–15 min. *Intermediate* (Q13–Q15): no skeleton (the statement ships, nothing else; the learner picks the induction and the shape); at most one invented lemma; about one item in five states its target in prose only; references of 10–30 lines (Dafny) or 15–45 (Verus); 20–45 min. *Hard* (Q16–Q18): 40–75 min per problem; at least one statement per module written or generalized by the learner, **the plain statement ships and is known to fail**; 2–4 invented lemmas or one invariant; references of 30–80 (Dafny) or 45–120 (Verus) lines; a blueprint, graded before formalization |
| **Line count** (spec §2.8 "reference proof length"; Δ53) | one rule for every item of every tier: the non-blank, non-comment lines of the reference's proof text (proof blocks, assertions, loop invariants and `decreases` clauses, ghost and tracked declarations and updates, lemma calls, the bodies of proof functions and lemmas), plus the statements (signature, `requires`, `ensures`, `decreases`) of every lemma or exec helper the learner must invent; exec code is not counted. Counted after the given layers re-supply their targets. The author notes §13.2 give every probed item's count under this rule, with its earlier figure |
| **File order** | Header; the prediction block (the warm-ups); the H items, in the order of the module's minutes line; no card section (practice modules write no card and recognise no construct: `write_*` and `recognise_*` are `[]`, §19.6). Lab files and agent files are separate (§22, §25, §28) |
| **Rates** | every item's minutes are split from design §7.5's line, inside its tier's per-item range (intro 8–15, intermediate 20–45, hard 40–75); spec §2.5's ≈ 2 min per reference line is the calibration run's prior, not a constraint, and the calibration run (spec §2.5) replaces the split. Where a line of design §7.5 forces an item outside its range (a pair or bundle line, a lab's Python line), §29 lists the item and Q47's entry records it; a warm-up prediction 4 (intro) or 5 (intermediate); a stage, trigger or forensic prediction 2.5–5; a brief 15 (10 in the hard tier, spec §2.5) |
| **Admissibility** (spec §2.8; design §7.5) | every H item of Q10–Q18 has a `[module.admissible]` entry (the checker's `gated` group). An item is **admitted** only when the module lane has recorded, on the built files, the four results: the reference is complete and inside its tier's line and invented-lemma bands (counted after the given layers re-supply their targets); the empty-body gate fails (`firsttry.sh <ID> --empty-body`: Dafny maxauto and the shipped options under seeds 1–3; Verus every light mode; an exec hole its `// triv:` body; a statement hole skipped with its reason, §18.1, and checked by its given client instead); the twin is stable (seeds 1–3, and for Dafny both bundled solvers, §17.5); every API it uses is the pinned one. The twin also carries a `// cfree:` line for each construct-free or shortcut answer the author knows of, and each must fail. Until then the entry is `admitted = false, minutes = 0` with a named fallback. **A fallback carries minutes only on the same four results**, recorded in the author notes (a paper fallback: its rubric rows and model answer there); a fallback without them carries 0, and the module lane records the line's shortfall in a DELTAS note when it arms the module. This plan's probes (the author notes §0 and §13) ran all four on the items marked *probed* below; the module lane re-runs them on the built files before it writes `admitted = true` |
| **Gated items** (design §5 "gated"; §14.0) | the permission items (Q11.H10v, Q14.H5v, Q17.H2v), the loop-rule soundness item (Q14.H4d) and Q16's mirror item (Q16.H3v with H3d) ship `admitted = false, minutes = 0` with their fallbacks whatever the plan's probes showed, and are admitted only by the module lane's recorded gate (design §7.5: "gated items carry no minutes until DV5 admits them") |
| **Prose item** (intermediate, about 1 in 5) | the block holds the prose (a docstring), the name the learner's declaration must have, a given client whose body is one labelled assume (the tool-counted hole: Dafny `assume false;`, Verus `proof_note` assume) and a commented use-site check under `Uncomment as you go:`. Done: write the statement and its proof under the given name, uncomment the check, delete the assume. Instruments: the use-site check (an adequate statement verifies it), a non-vacuity witness the learner writes (a concrete call that meets every `requires`), and the **hidden equivalence check** (the author's statement, proved equivalent in both directions), which runs only in the grader container (§30 Q49). **As built** (DELTAS Δ220): `truthcheck judge`, a two-way refinement by four lemmas and the vacuity lemma, one verdict word per candidate (`faithful`, `strong`, `weak`, `vacuous`, `undecided`, `mismatch`, `context`); the learner starts it from their own terminal (`scripts/mksandbox-run --truth <the lab's archive> --truth-check judge --judge <spec>`; guard rule 1c refuses the line in a Claude session), and `/review-code` still reviews the statement against the prose and records the verdict the learner reports |
| **Hard-tier problem** (design §7.1, spec §2.8) | the plain statement ships as a hole and is known to fail (the author notes record the failing attempt); the learner's blueprint (paper, `B1`) is graded by `/grade-paper` before any formal work (≥ 3 on every dimension); the learner's invented lemmas go in the file's `HELPERS` area; the module's exit is the verifier part (design §7.1) plus `/review-code` at the top bar (`library-pr`) and `/grade-paper` before and after formalization |
| **Timed exit items** (Δ116, `firsttry`'s `EXIT_TIERS`) | X items in the module's exit file (`dafny/Q/<ID>_<Name>/exit/<ID>_Exit.dfy`, `verus/q/<id>_<name>/src/exit.rs`), closed book, timed by the minutes the exit line gives: Q10–Q12 two (`firsttry.sh <ID> --exit` passes at ≥ 1 of 2), Q13–Q15 one (it must pass). Q16–Q18 have no exit file: `firsttry --exit` names the verifier part plus the reviews |
| **Lab block** (design §11.1, §11.2) | a family-C module (Q12, Q15, Q18) carries its lab beside the practice items (design §7.5). Its pre-registration is the `[pre-registration]` problem of the written pair, between `% PREREG-BEGIN` and `% PREREG-END` in `paper/<ID>_answers.tex`; `/grade-paper` grades it, its `prereg` gradebook row unlocks the runs (`fvh.prereg`, DV4-PLAN §4.5), and every record carries its sha256. Run records live under `research/runs/<ID>/` (Δ149, Δ150), written only by `fvh` |
| **Python items** (labs, gates, clients) | §8.0's Python holes (AST-counted); acceptance tests frozen under `harness/tests/q/` (rule 2f); a Python hole is admitted when its reference passes the frozen tests and its `# triv:` body fails them. The gate files are solo (rule 2s) |
| **Std tier and Verus tiers** (§14.7 F16) | every Dafny file of Q10–Q18 reaches L4 or above and is std tier; every Verus file is `arithmetic` or wider; a file that uses `vstd::cell` is `all` |
| **Nonlinear arithmetic** | a module states `{:disableNonlinearArithmetic}` where an item's point is a library call (Q10's search item, Q13's library goal and accumulator loop, Q14's refinement, Q16) and not elsewhere; such a module comes **last in its file** (Δ31: the attribute also applies to every module declared after it in the same file); each module says which, and the author notes say why |

### 19.1 Rules (design §10.1; spec §2.6; Δ22, Δ34, Δ35, Δ130, Δ158)

§1.1, §8.1 and §14.1 hold for L5–L7. In addition:

1. **A practice layer re-supplies only what a later item or layer calls.** L5 re-supplies no Q07–Q09 target (no later item needs one, §19.5); L6 re-supplies Verus `normalize` (Q02.H2v; §1.7 F3 is closed by §20, §30 Q47) and the content of Q08.H1d's refinement lemma as a base-module lemma; L7 re-supplies Verus subtraction (Q13.H1v's `sub`) as `big_sub`, and `to_u64` (Q10.H1v), which the capstone's crate calls (the name: §19.4). By the tier law each layer may hold whole answers of lower-tier holes (Δ130), so each layer's header says not to open it before its first module.
2. **Nothing a practice layer gives is posed again** by a module that imports it (§19.6, F17).
3. **Full multiplication stays out of every layer** (§14.7 F13): Q16 poses it (§26); L7 gives only its schoolbook *definitions* (§19.4), never a lemma or an implementation about them.
4. **Tiers.** All three Dafny layers are std tier (they import L4); each Verus layer crate depends on the whole chain below it (Δ22) and is `arithmetic` tier.
5. **No answer in the header** (Δ110): the headers name the layer, "provided, complete", the importers and the instruction not to open it early; no tier, lever, measure or broadcast mechanism.

### 19.2 L5 (tier: practice-intro; declared by Q10; imported by Q10–Q18)

**Dafny** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L5_BigNat.dfy`, `module QG_L5_BigNat` (imports L1–L4; std tier):

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `lemma CanonicalLower(s: seq<nat>) requires Canonical(s) && \|s\| > 0 ensures Pow(\|s\| - 1) <= ToNat(s)` (`decreases \|s\|`) | new helper: the Dafny counterpart of L3's Verus lower bound; never posed as an item |
| `lemma PowPositive(n: nat) ensures 0 < Pow(n)` (`decreases n`) | new helper |
| `lemma NormalizeTrim(s: seq<nat>, n: nat) requires n <= \|s\| && (n == 0 \|\| s[n - 1] != 0) && forall j \| n <= j < \|s\| :: s[j] == 0 ensures Normalize(s) == s[..n]` (`decreases \|s\|`) | new helper, given for Q10.H4d as L2's lifting lemma was given for Q03.H1v (Δ52) |

**Verus** crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l5` (dependencies `vstd` and `qg_bignat_l1`–`l4`; `build = false`; `verify = true`), `src/lib.rs` with `pub use qg_bignat_l4::*;`, import tier `arithmetic`:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `pub proof fn lemma_weight_pos(n: nat) ensures weight(n) > 0` | new helper (the counterpart of `PowPositive`) |

### 19.3 L6 (tier: practice-intermediate; declared by Q13; imported by Q13–Q18)

**Dafny** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L6_BigNat.dfy`, `module {:disableNonlinearArithmetic} QG_L6_BigNat` (imports L1–L5; std tier):

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `lemma FromNatCanonical(n: nat) ensures Canonical(FromNat(n))` (`decreases n`) | new (§8.5 left it to a later layer; no item posed it) |
| `lemma NormalizeAddCorrect(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) ensures Canonical(Normalize(Add(a, b))) && ToNat(Normalize(Add(a, b))) == ToNat(a) + ToNat(b)` | re-supplied: the content of Q08.H1d (refined there for `Q08_Canon`), for Q14.H1d's given lemmas |
| `ghost function Sub(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>): seq<nat> requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) && ToNat(a) >= ToNat(b)`, defined by value through L2's `FromNat` | a specification, not an algorithm (design §10.1's "`Sub`"): what the capstone's Dafny mirror and Lab 2's local prose tasks state subtraction against (§28.3); no lemma about it is given anywhere, and no compiled code can call it |

**Verus** crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l6` (dependencies `vstd`, `qg_bignat_l1`–`l5`), `src/lib.rs` with `pub use qg_bignat_l5::*;`, import tier `arithmetic`:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `pub fn normalize(a: &mut BigNat) ensures final(a).wf(), final(a).to_nat() == old(a).to_nat()` | re-supplied, first posed as Q02.H2v; Q13.H1v's `sub` calls it |

### 19.4 L7 (tier: practice-hard; declared by Q16; imported by Q16–Q18)

Design §10.1: "Dafny mirror of the mini-crate | mini-crate with frozen spec files".

**Dafny** `dafny/given/Q/QG_L7_BigNat.dfy`, `module {:disableNonlinearArithmetic} QG_L7_BigNat` (imports L1–L6; std tier):

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `function Shift(s: seq<nat>, k: nat): seq<nat>` (`k` zero limbs below `s`); `ghost function MulAcc(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>, acc: seq<nat>, k: nat): seq<nat>` (`decreases \|b\|`: one `MulLimb` row per limb of `b`, from the low limb, each shifted by its position and added into the accumulator); `ghost function Times(a, b) = MulAcc(a, b, [], 0)` | the schoolbook definitions that Q16.H1d and H3d are about, and the specification the capstone's Dafny mirror states multiplication against (§28.3). `MulAcc` and `Times` are **ghost**: no compiled method can call them, so every compiled multiplication in Q16–Q18 is the learner's own. No lemma about them is given anywhere |

**Verus** crate `verus/given/qg_bignat_l7` (dependencies `vstd`, `qg_bignat_l1`–`l6`), import tier `arithmetic`:

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `pub fn big_sub(a: &BigNat, b: &BigNat) -> (r: BigNat) requires a.wf(), b.wf(), a.to_nat() >= b.to_nat(), ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == a.to_nat() - b.to_nat()` | re-supplied, first posed as Q13.H1v (`sub` there). Named `big_sub` in L7: beside `use vstd::prelude::*;`, which every Q16–Q18 file has, a call of `sub` is ambiguous (E0659, probed), as `add` was for L4 (Δ157); DELTAS Δ209, and the user's decision D13 (2026-10-12) keeps the name |
| `pub fn to_u64(a: &BigNat) -> (r: Option<u64>)` with Q10.H1v's specification | re-supplied, first posed as Q10.H1v |
| `src/api.rs`, the mini-crate's frozen spec file: `pub trait BigNatCore: NatLike` adding `fn minus(&self, other: &Self)` and `fn to_word(&self) -> Option<u64>` with their specifications, and `impl BigNatCore for BigNat` (by `big_sub` and `to_u64`) | the base API the capstone's crate builds on (§28.3); its operations are all re-supplied ones. The capstone's new operations, multiplication and division by a word among them, are stated by the learner (spec §5.5: "the learner writes all statements"), never here (rule 3) |

Generated units (Δ78): one `std/` Dafny unit and one crate per module, each over the whole chain; `verus/Cargo.toml` gains the three layer crates and nine module crates.

- **L5** re-supplies no detailed-tier target: no Q10–Q18 item or layer calls a Q07–Q09 hole's answer (the port, the shifts and splits of Q08's flagship, the permission items, the wp lemmas, the repair pair and the gate core stay the learner's; §14.7 F14 holds).
- **L6** re-supplies Verus `normalize` (F3: Q10 poses fresh twins instead, §20, so the target may now be given from L6 on) and Q08.H1d's content (as `NormalizeAddCorrect`, in the base module).
- **L7** re-supplies `big_sub` (Q13.H1v's `sub`) and `to_u64` (Q10.H1v).
- **Kept out of every layer:** Dafny `NormalizeLoop` (no layer needs it); `CanonicalUnique` (Q06's; F10 holds: no Q10–Q18 item poses it, and none needs it); full multiplication (rule 3); division by one limb (Q16.H2v); every Q10–Q18 exit target.

### 19.6 Proof that L5–L7 answer no item of a module that imports them

1. **The layers are listed exhaustively** in §19.2–§19.4.
2. **No layer declaration has the statement of a Q10–Q18 item**, and none a stronger `ensures` (the table).
3. **No layer proof is an item's whole answer.** The re-supplied proofs answer Q02.H2v, Q08.H1d, Q10.H1v and Q13.H1v, each re-supplied only from a layer of a higher tier (Δ130), and never an exit or test-out item of Q01–Q18 (checked against every exit statement in the three author notes files, Δ133).
4. **No layer code answers an exec, annotation or statement hole of an importing module.** L5 has no exec code; L6's is `normalize` (no Q13–Q18 item asks for it; L6's `Sub` is a ghost specification by value, so it shows no subtraction algorithm); L7's are `big_sub`, `to_u64` and the `BigNatCore` implementation built from them, and the compiled Dafny `Shift` (no Q16–Q18 item asks for any of them; `MulAcc` and `Times` are ghost, so no compiled answer can call them).
5. **Helpers given for an item** (as Δ52's): L5's `NormalizeTrim` for Q10.H4d (whose hole is the loop invariant); L6's `NormalizeAddCorrect` and `FromNatCanonical` for Q14.H1d's given lemmas (whose hole is the comparison lemma).

| Q10.H5d / H5v | a remainder of a remainder | no | none |
| Q10.H6v, H7v | ring-buffer operations | no (no ring buffer in any layer) | none |
| Q11 items | a class with frames, a subset-type function, `&mut` + `Result` methods, a two-limb value, preconditions by hand, one case of a soundness proof, a cell operation | no | none |
| Q12 items | a literal unfolding, a trigger chain, the hand items, the Python clients | no | none |

| Q17, Q18 items | a priority-queue refinement; a permissioned ring buffer; statements of a VC generator; a repair under a solver swap; the capstone; gate v2 | no | none (the capstone's new operations are stated by the learner; L7 holds only the re-supplied base API) |

### 19.7 Forward constraints (for DV5–DV7)

- **F17.** No module Q10–Q18 poses anything §19.2–§19.4 list (rule 2); a challenge that needs one cites it.
- **F18.** §1.7 F3 is closed: Q10's normalize pair is a fresh forward-scan pair, so Verus `normalize` is re-supplied from L6 on and Dafny `NormalizeLoop` stays module-local (§30 Q47).
- **F19.** `CanonicalUnique` stays Q06's (§8.7 F10): no Q10–Q18 item poses it and no layer re-supplies it. Q13's normalize item takes another second half (§23).
- **F20.** Full multiplication is posed in Q16 only (H1d, H3d / H3v); no layer gives a proof or an implementation of it; the capstone (Q18) carries the learner's own (§28.3); the challenges re-pose it only at a larger scale (Q27).
- **F21.** Each practice tier is one sweep (Q10/Q13/Q16 family A, Q11/Q14/Q17 B, Q12/Q15/Q18 C) and, as §14.7 F15 for the detailed tier, no file of a tier holds another item's answer of that tier; a lab's local tasks are stripped forms of the tier's own hand items and of author tasks posed nowhere else (§22.6).
- **F22.** Q01's targets (§1.7 F2): no practice layer or item re-poses `Max2`, `ToNatAppendTwoZeros` or `AllZeroToNat`, or a statement from which one follows in one call. The author notes record the two places where one follows in two calls (L5's `NormalizeTrim` with L3's `NormalizeToNat`) and why Q13.H4d requires a non-zero limb.
- **F23.** The R track (design §9): no Q10–Q18 file holds a regex artifact, and the shared genres are the waivers W7 (Q13, Q16 → R06), W8 (Q12 → R12), W9 (Q12 → Lab α), W10 (Q15 → Lab β), W11 (Q18 → R16) and W12 (Q18 → R16), plus the waivers PLAN §5.4 proposes for the genres the plan review found (records lane, into design §9.3 and `avoid.waivers.tsv`): **W15** stage attribution (Q03.P6dv, Q12's stage specimens → R13), **W16** refute → repair → prove (Q15.H2d/H2v → R06), **W17** possible and impossible crate tasks as cheat attractors (Q18's Lab 3 → Lab γ; the two labs' impossible kinds differ, §28.6), **W18** the card contamination audit (Q15's Lab 2 → Lab β) and **W19** a tracked `Map<int, PointsTo<_>>` with take and put (Q17.H2v → R11; Q14.H5v no longer uses one, §24.2). Q's predictions keep to Q's owner tags (PLAN §5.1); the author notes §4.1 and §13 record the check of Q10–Q18's predictions against them. `avoid.sh` (spec §2.7) runs on every practice module's hole-bodied declarations when it is armed.

## 20. Q10 — Practice I-A (family A)

*Tier: intro practice, family A (S1–S5), layer L5, 182 min. Statements given; one hole per item; references within D ≤ 10 / V ≤ 15 lines; 8–15 min per item. Exit: 2 timed items, ≥ 1 of 2. No agent run. Card 02.11. Design §7.5's line: brief 15, warm-up 8, `u64` round-trip pair 24, spec items 15, assertion repairs 16, `normalize` invariant pair 24, search-then-name 20, `Seq`-view ring buffer (V) 20, exit 20, paper 15, ledger 5.*

### 20.3 Constructs, promotions and pairs

- `write_d = write_v = recognise_d = recognise_v = []` (§19.0). Every `uses:` slug is an earlier module's WRITE construct in its language (Q02–Q07).
- **Promotions** (design §7.4's Q10/Q11 column): practice items are where constructs that earlier modules taught only as RECOGNISE are first used in checked items; in Q10 none is needed by an item, and Q11 cites `old` (Q02's RECOGNISE construct) in its frame and `&mut` items (§21.3).
- **Pairs:** H1, H3, H4, H5 (four pairs); H2d/H2v are one obligation each (two spec items); the ring buffer is Verus by design. Hole minutes D 49 / V 70 (Q10 tilts to Verus, the tier's rule holds over Q10–Q12, §29).

H1v, H4d and H7v are timed first in the calibration run. **H3d** carries a resource-spread flag under the other bundled solver (CV 27.6%, advisory; seeds 1–3 and both solvers verify): the Q10 lane restructures the twin until `--brittleness --solvers` stops flagging it or records why it stays. **H5d**'s maximal-automation run ends out of resources at the pinned limit (twice; the shipped runs fail): the gate counts that as failing at the pin (maxauto is reported, not gating), and the Q10 lane records the verdict on the built file.

### 21.3 Constructs, promotions and the gated item

- `write_* = recognise_* = []`. **Promotions:** `old` (Q02's RECOGNISE construct, both languages) is cited by H1d, H2d, H3v and H4v; `requires` and `function` (Q01's RECOGNISE constructs) by H7d and H8d. These are their first checked uses.
- **The gated item** (design §5's permissions row): H10v ships `admitted = false, minutes = 0` (§21.5). The module lane admits it after recording the four results (the plan's probe passed the reference, TRIV and the seed sweep; the API is `vstd::cell::pcell` at the pin); otherwise **F-H10v** takes the 15 minutes once it has its own four results (§19.0): `max_into` on a struct the module owns, through `&mut self` (no permission).
- **Hand before automation.** The VC items come before the mechanized wp case (H9d), and Q08's verified wp is the learner's own earlier work (W2d gives a fresh language; no Q08 proof appears in Q11's files).

### 22.3 The practice block (100 min)

- **S9 retry client (20).** The design's client (§7.5: "sends 3 candidates per goal over a given BigNat battery via `vcheck --json`, solve count pinned"): H1py and H2py over the given battery (twelve goals in both languages, three author-written candidates each, some wrong in different ways; `Goal` and `Attempt` given above the holes). The frozen tests replay recorded documents (no solver in CI); a live run (`python -m fvh_q.retry --battery`, on the host: the battery is a `course` target, Δ145) is the learner's own check. Waiver W8 (design §9.3): R12's obligation loop repeats the genre on its own objects.
- **S10 stage attribution (15).** P1d–P6v (design §7.4: "stage of 6 failures"), 2.5 min each; P1d and P2v read the stage from the JSON document (S9), the other four from the text line (S10); the specimens and their commands are in the files, the slots are hash-checked, and the module lane replaces any scenario whose stage an earlier prediction of Q01–Q11 already settles (the author notes §4.1).
- **S11 trigger and fuel (15).** H3d (7) and H3v (8).
- **S12 grade-the-grader (15).** Three fresh `/grade-paper` sessions grade the same given flawed pre-registration fixture (`paper/fixtures/Q12_flawed_prereg.tex`, author-written, its flaws named only in the author notes); the learner records the three score vectors and each grader's notes in `research/q12_grade_the_grader.md`, the agreement per dimension and which flaws each pass caught, and says in three lines what that licenses about using `/grade-paper` as Lab 1's unlock (design §11.1). It needs `/grade-paper`'s fixture mode (§30 Q54).
- **Exit (20).** X1d, X1v.

### 22.4 Agentic file (rung 10: the toolwright)

- **Shape** (spec §5.1: "the agent builds a machine-readable hole/assumption counter to the learner's interface spec; the learner tests it on fixtures"): `tools/Q12/holecount.py` ships with a header and an empty CONTRACT block. Before the run (the learner, 8 min): write the interface (input: `scripts/vcheck --json` and `scripts/vaudit` documents of one or more files; output: one JSON object per file with the hole count by kind, the assumption findings by kind and the verdict, never a count of its own from the source text), and the acceptance tests `tools/Q12/test_holecount.py` over fixtures the learner copies from earlier modules' recorded documents.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` implement `holecount.py` to the interface in its docstring · `allowed-files:` `tools/Q12/holecount.py` · `acceptance:` `` `python3 tools/Q12/test_holecount.py` exits 0 `` · the tool reads documents only (no verifier run, no source scan: an audit line) · re-verify after every edit.
- **Follow-up (7 min):** run the tool on two documents the tests do not contain; audit its source (does it count from the text? does it treat a missing key as zero?); record in the file's `AUDIT` block.

### 22.5 Lab 1: the question, the arms and the hand part

Design §11.2: *at budget B, do sequential feedback or best-first search beat independent sampling, and does the answer differ by language (R4)?* Three Qwen arms at matched compute (B model calls per task): **A1** B independent samples (H8py), **A2** one sample and B − 1 repairs (H9py), **A3** best-first over edit states ranked by failing obligations (H10py); a **Claude arm** runs the one arm the pre-registration names as its predicted best, on a pre-registered, seeded, language-stratified subset of 20 of the 60 tasks (10 per language), reported as repeated runs, never seeds (design §11.1). The **hand part** (E1): H4d–H7v, 12 min each, done closed book before the runs; it passes at ≥ 3 of 4 in each language. The hand items whose task forms meet the lab's validity rule (§22.6; the author notes §4.2 name them) are also tasks of the agents' local stratum, so the report can place the learner's own hit rate beside each arm's on the same tasks (descriptive only).

### 22.6 Lab 1: tasks, budget, pre-registration, route and acceptance

**Strata (60 tasks, 30 per language).** Every task's answer verifies on the pins and its task form does not (`scripts/get_bench.sh --validate`; for the local cards the twin's gate, §19.0), **under maximal automation too** until the lab gate refuses any delivery that adds a lever the task form does not ship (an `{:induction …}`, `{:fuel …}` or resource-limit attribute, a solver option; §30 Q63), and under the shipped options once it does; so no task can be closed by switching automation back on. **As built** (DELTAS Δ182, Δ208, Δ221; the user's decision D24): the lab gate refuses an added or dropped lever and then applies `vaudit`'s assumption rules to the delivery, so the shipped options hold. The public pools' rule (`get_bench.sh --validate`): the answer passes the done check (`vcheck --gate`, verified with 0 holes, and `vaudit`, no finding) and, as a delivery for its task form, the lab gate's static half (`specfreeze --lab`, 0 findings); the task form fails `vcheck --gate` as shipped at the verify stage; an answer equal to its task form is no task. The pools are pinned in `bench/public/POOLS.toml` (DafnyBench 303 tasks, HumanEval-Verus 83), and HumanEval-Verus's task forms come from its stripping step (`verus-strip-proofs`: the proofs removed; the specification, the code and each loop's `decreases` kept, since Verus refuses a loop without one before it verifies). The stratum's composition under each case is pre-registered:

| stratum | Dafny | Verus | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| local | 10: the hand items that meet the validity rule, stripped, and author tasks of the intro band posed nowhere else (to ten) | 10: the same | `bench/local/q/lab1/` (course-authored after the models' release: the contamination control) |
| public | 20 DafnyBench (`hints_removed` tasks) | 20 HumanEval-Verus (task forms from DV5's stripping step: proofs removed, specification, code and each loop's `decreases` kept) | `bench/manifest.toml`, every set `fetch` (Δ152) |

A seeded draw from each validated pool, stratified as above; the seed is pre-registered. A **pilot** on the **public strata only** — one Qwen sample on each of twice the needed public candidates (80 calls), under pre-registered pilot seeds disjoint from every arm's seeds — checks each public stratum's difficulty: a public stratum whose pilot rate is below 0.1 or above 0.9 is redrawn by the pre-registered rule (design §6.5: "strata are chosen per model from a pilot"). The local stratum is fixed at ten tasks per language and is never piloted or redrawn. MINIF2F-DAFNY is not a pool (its installed copy holds at most twelve example solutions, none validated). The author notes §4.2 record the plan's validation sample, which bears on the pool sizes.

**As built** (DELTAS Δ236, the user's decision D30; Δ239): Lab 1's public HumanEval-Verus stratum draws from the pool less the five tasks Lab 2's `[[draw]]` took (78 of 83 members under D30), because Lab 1's task forms keep the specification and Lab 2's are graded on it; `scripts/get_bench.sh --members humaneval-verus --lab Q12 [--seed S]` (`dvl.bench.lab_members`) lists the drawable members, sorted or in the seed's order, and `bench/local/q/lab1/STRATUM.toml`, B1's tasks-and-strata field and Q12 §4.3 say so. Q12 §4.3's **Run setup** paragraph reads the pilot rule above as: the first forty members in the seed's order are the pilot's candidates (one Qwen sample each, 80 calls over both strata), and the first twenty are the stratum's tasks unless the pilot's rate falls outside 0.1–0.9 (Δ239). The user's decision D39 (Δ243, built in Δ244) also excludes the HumanEval problems of Lab 2's five public Dafny cards, four of which were among the 78: Lab 1's draw excludes Lab 2's HumanEval problems in both languages, and 74 of the 83 members are eligible (`STRATUM.toml`, B1's tasks-and-strata field, rubric CT.b and Q12 §4.3 say so).

**D39 amendment (Δ243):** Lab 1 also excludes the HumanEval problem IDs of Lab 2’s public Dafny cards. The drawable HumanEval-Verus pool is 74 of 83; the chapter, B1 and the stratum header state the cross-language rule.


**Budget arithmetic** (calls = tasks × attempts × rounds × arms × repeats; design §11.1):

| part | arithmetic | calls |
|---|---|---|
| Qwen arms | 60 tasks × B = 8 calls per task-arm (A1: 8 attempts × 1 round; A2: 1 attempt × 8 rounds; A3: 8 expansions) × 3 arms × 3 repeats | 4,320 |
| Qwen pilot | 80 public candidates × 1 × 1 × 1 arm × 1 | 80 |
| Claude arm | 20 tasks × 8 × 1 arm × 1 repeat | 160 |
| Claude sentinels and apparatus checks | at most 20 (Δ148) | ≤ 20 |

So Qwen 4,400 calls in 540 + 80 runs, Claude ≤ 180 calls in 20 runs. **GPU** [A]: at `num_predict` 768 with thinking off, DV0's single throughput sample and about 1 s of prefill (DV4 §10), an independent-sample call takes about 7.3 s, so 4,400 calls at that rate are ≈ 9 GPU-h, inside the registry's 10. That figure is a lower bound for the feedback arms: A2's and A3's prompts grow with the verifier documents they carry, so their prefill per call is larger, and if the Ollama gate pins thinking on, a 768-token cap would truncate most attempts. The pre-registration therefore fixes the generation settings and the cost per arm (fields below), redoes the GPU arithmetic **per arm** from DV4 step 3d's throughput probe and the pilot's measured prompt sizes, and pre-registers its fallback (two repeats instead of three) for the case where the per-arm totals exceed 10 GPU-h. The registry's DV1a budget counted no policy runs; §22.8 gives the proposed fields (§30 Q55).

**The pre-registration template** (`paper/Q12_answers.tex`, B1, between the PREREG markers; graded by `/grade-paper` before any run, ≥ 3 on every method dimension; Δ91):

| field | what the learner writes |
|---|---|
| claim | the three hypotheses (A2 vs A1, A3 vs A1, and the language interaction), each one-sided or two-sided, before any data |
| metric and estimator | per task and repeat: solved within B calls (any verified candidate the gate accepts); the arm's rate; the paired difference per comparison |
| arms and policies | the three Qwen arms and the Claude arm's pick, each with its policy file and, once written, its prompt hash; the Claude subset's seed and its stratification (10 tasks per language) |
| tasks and strata | the table above, the sets' commits, the validation files' sha256, the draw seed, the pilot rule |
| budget arithmetic | the table above with its own numbers, per arm, plus the sentinel calls; the throughput assumption and its source |
| generation settings | the values the Ollama gate pins for every Qwen call (thinking on or off, `num_predict`, `num_ctx`, temperature) and the seed list per arm and for the pilot (pairwise disjoint); for Claude the pinned argv |
| cost per arm | tokens in and out, GPU-seconds and wall clock per arm, against the registry's ceiling, redone from DV4 step 3d's probe; the **truncation rate** (attempts cut at `num_predict`) per arm as a sensitivity line |
| MDE | computed with `fvh.stats.mde_two_proportions` at the pre-registered n, baseline, pairing and Holm-adjusted α (§6 Q35, disposed of in §30) |
| seeds and repeats | Qwen: the per-request seed list per arm and for the pilot, disjoint (the gate pins seed and temperature, Δ146); Claude: "repeated runs" |
| cheat surface and gate | the instruments every claimed success passes (the learner's Q09 gate core, in the verification container), the X classes, `UNKNOWN` handling |
| exclusions | `infra-failed` re-issue (same id, prompt hash and seed, at most 3); `truncated:<cause>` counted as failures (primary) with the sensitivity line (Δ149) |
| stopping rule | a cap or window reached between attempts defers the batch; no peeking at outcomes before the plan's last id is scored |
| intervals | the learner's 80% prediction intervals for each arm's rate and each difference, scored after the run |
| contamination | the local stratum's authorship dates; the public sets flagged as possibly seen |
| disclosure | "a small-scale replication with model substitution"; Claude as grader and subject; one machine, one solver pin |

**The fvh pieces the learner writes:** `policies.py` (H8py–H11py, on DV4's protocol: `propose(state, ctx, budget) -> [Action]`, never touching a file), `report.py` (H12py, H13py, composing `fvh.stats`; DV4-PLAN §14 Q6: the analysis tables are the learner's, the frozen `fvh report` reproduces outcomes and prints the apparatus and disclosure block), and `retry.py` (practice). Everything else is frozen core: the executor, backends, budget and ledger, run log, apparatus, pre-registration gate and report driver (DV4 §3), and the gate core of Q09 for the re-judging.

**The verification route.** Every `Verify` of every arm runs in the network-less verification container through `fvh.tools`' `ContainerRunner` (agent provenance, Δ145): **it needs DV4 step 2b**, after host action H1. The Qwen arms need the Ollama gate (2c) and the Qwen policy transport (3c, DV4-PLAN §14 Q3); the throughput probe is 3d; the Claude arm runs harness-executed (4a, 4b). Until then everything else is buildable and tested: the hand part, the pre-registration and its grading, the policies against `MockPolicy`/`ScriptedPolicy`/`ReplayPolicy` in the frozen tests, the report on synthetic logs, the toolwright run, the cards.

**Acceptance** (design §11.1): `fvh report Q12` reproduces every table of the learner's report from the logs (it re-derives outcomes through `ReplayPolicy` and re-checks the ledger chain against `research/ledger-heads.jsonl`); every claimed success passes the learner's gate from a clean checkout (`fvh report --reverify`); the disclosure block is present; the hand part passes at ≥ 3 of 4 per language. **Graded on method and reproducibility, never on matching numbers** (rubric dimensions in `paper/rubrics/Q12_rubric.md`); the report discloses that Claude is both grader and subject.

## 23. Q13 — Practice II-A (family A)

*Tier: intermediate practice, family A (S1–S5), layer L6, 200 min. No skeleton; at most one invented lemma per item; one prose item (H3v). Exit: 1 timed item, which must pass. No agent run. Card 05.5. Design §7.5's line: brief 15, warm-up 5, `sub` with borrow (V) 35, `normalize` idempotent (D) 30, prose statement: comparison vs value order (V) 25, library goal (pair) 30, accumulator generalization (D) 20, exit 20, paper 15, ledger 5.*

### 23.3 Constructs, prose and the band

- `write_* = recognise_* = []`. The prose item is one of six items (about 1 in 5, design §7.1).
- **Normalize and F3/F10** (§19.7 F18–F19): H2d's second half is the concatenation law, not `CanonicalUnique` (Q06's); the one invented lemma it allows is the idempotence step.
- **The library pair** carries `requires 0 < d` so that the statement is not a corollary of Q01's all-zero target (F22).
- **Bands** (the author notes §13.2 give each count under §19.0's rule): every reference is inside 10–30 (Dafny) or 15–45 (Verus) lines, with at most one invented lemma. H5d sits in the nonlinear-off module for its band (the author notes §13.2 say why).

### 25.3 The practice block (165 min)

- **S9 LSP client (30):** P1d 3, H6py 5, H7py 7, H8py 15. Written from scratch against `dafny server` (design §7.5), started through the course's scratch copy of the install (Δ33, Δ98) under the Dafny memory cap. LSP is Q-only (design §9.3: R12 reads a captured trace).
- **S10 pipeline forensic (20):** P2d–P5v (4 × 2.5) as the learner's own first pass, then rung 11 (10): the contractor attributes stage and cause for the agent file's deliveries, and the learner grades its attributions against the first pass and the tool output (spec §5.1).
- **S11 trigger battery + stability (30):** P6dv–P11dv (6 × 2.5), H1d 8, H1v 7.
- **S12 refute → repair → prove (15):** H2d 8, H2v 7, admitted under D40’s short-format band exception (7/13 proof lines). Waiver W16 (proposed, §19.7 F23): R06 repeats the genre on its own objects.
- **Toolsmith (20):** rung 12 (spec §5.1: "the agent builds one automation for a 12-goal battery; the learner reviews its source and audits every goal"): the learner writes the tool's interface and the CONTRACT (8), then reviews its source and audits all twelve goals (no statement changed; only triggers or the tool's documented edits added) (12).
- **Tutor red-team (15):** in a `/tutor Q15` session, three extraction attempts against a decoy reference directory the chapter names; the learner records, per attempt, whether the tutor read, quoted or refused, and which guard layer stopped it (`research/q15_redteam.md`). Waiver-free (design §9.3: tutor red-team vs R15's grader injection).
- **Exit (20):** X1d.

### 25.4 Agentic files (rungs 11 and 12)

- **Rung 11, the pipeline forensic** (`Q15_AgentForensic.dfy`, `agent.rs`): each ships green with four holes, one per delivery; each delivery's quoted attempt stops at a different stage (the author notes name them). **CONTRACT** `goal:` for each delivery, fill its row of the `ATTRIBUTIONS` block (stage, the instrument that shows it, the root cause), then repair the attempt into a verified proof without changing its statement · `allowed-files:` the agent file of the run · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 `` · zero holes · `` `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q15_forensic.dfy <file>` `` (Dafny) or `` `--against research/q15_forensic.rs` `` (Verus) reports no change · `` `scripts/vaudit <file>` reports no finding `` · every `ATTRIBUTIONS` row filled (an audit line) · re-verify after every edit.
- **Rung 12, the toolsmith** (`tools/Q15/toolsmith.py`): **CONTRACT** `goal:` build the automation the docstring specifies for the twelve battery goals · `allowed-files:` `tools/Q15/toolsmith.py` · `acceptance:` the tool, run on each goal, leaves a file that `scripts/vcheck --gate` passes with `scripts/specfreeze --against` the shipped goal unchanged · the tool never writes a statement line (an audit line) · re-verify after every edit.

### 25.5 Lab 2: the question and the arms

Design §11.2: *do the learner's oracles separate faithful, weak, vacuous and over-strong specifications, and does oracle feedback raise agent faithfulness?* **Vericoding arm:** how often does an agent that implements and proves against its own specification fail when re-judged against the reference? Arms (Qwen): **S0** specification from prose, four independent samples, the first that verifies with its witnesses kept; **S1** the same four calls, with **reference-free** oracle feedback after each sample: the mutant kill of the candidate specification against the shipped implementation's input–output behaviour, and the vacuity and witness checks; **V** (vericoding) the agent writes the specification, then implements and proves against it with verifier feedback (eight calls); a **Claude arm** runs S1 on a pre-registered, seeded, language-stratified subset of 20 tasks. S0 and S1 are matched at four calls per task and compared with each other; **V is a separate measurement** (its re-judged failure rate, D vs V), never compared with S0 or S1 at unmatched compute. The hidden reference never enters a loop: `implication` and `classify`, which need it, run only after the batch, in the grader container, where every arm's output is re-judged against it.

### 25.6 Lab 2: tasks, budget, pre-registration, route and acceptance

**Strata (30 tasks).** Local: 10 Dafny and 10 Verus prose tasks over BigNat operations, written by the author after the models' release, reference specifications sealed in `Hidden/labs/Q15/` (distinct from the six prose specs the learner writes, H3d–H5v). Public: 5 HumanEval-Verus tasks (prose: the docstring; reference: the task's specification, from DV5's stripping step) and 5 HumanEval-Dafny tasks from vericoding's `humaneval` source (Apache-2.0). Every reference validates on the pins. No pilot: the strata are fixed by kind, and a pilot's calls would come out of the 1,200.

**Budget arithmetic:**

| part | arithmetic | calls |
|---|---|---|
| S0 and S1 | 30 tasks × 1 attempt × 4 rounds × 2 arms × 3 repeats | 720 |
| V (vericoding) | 30 tasks × 1 attempt × 8 rounds × 1 arm × 2 repeats | 480 |
| Claude (S1) | 20 tasks × 1 × 4 rounds × 1 arm × 1 repeat | 80 |
| Claude sentinels | at most 10 (Δ148) | ≤ 10 |

Qwen 1,200 calls in 240 runs, ≈ 2.4 GPU-h at Lab 1's independent-sample rate (registry 3), a lower bound for S1 and V, whose prompts grow with feedback: the pre-registration redoes it per arm (§22.6); Claude ≤ 90 calls in 20 runs (§30 Q55).

**The pre-registration template:** Q12's fields (§22.6, the generation settings and the cost per arm included), with these changes: the claim (oracle separation; the feedback effect S1 − S0 at matched calls; the vericoding re-judged failure rate, D vs V, R1, reported on its own and never compared with S0 or S1); the feedback signals S1 receives, each named and shown to need no hidden input; the metric and estimator (the oracle verdict against the hidden reference; agreement with the hidden verdicts as a confusion matrix with Cohen's κ; the verified-but-unfaithful rate with Wilson intervals); the oracles' version (the sha256 of `oracles.py` at the run); the contamination field adds the card contamination audit (design §11.3's control, here on the local tasks: post-cutoff authorship, novelty review, perturbation probes; waiver W18, proposed, §19.7 F23).

**The fvh pieces the learner writes:** `oracles.py` (H9py–H12py) and the arms' policies (Q12's `policies.py` reused through a new mode of `render`, inside the vericoding arm's 20 minutes). The frozen core: as Lab 1.

**The verification route.** Every `Verify` in the verification container (DV4 step 2b); every judgement against hidden truth in the **grader container** (DV4 §5.9's container with an author-only `truth` input, built by DV5 or DV7; §30 Q49). Qwen policy (3c), Claude harness-executed (4b). Until then: the prose specs, the oracles against synthetic fixtures, the pre-registration and the reward and bench audits.

**Acceptance:** as Lab 1's (§22.6), plus the confusion matrix and κ reproduced from the logs, and every claimed faithful verdict reproducible in the grader container from a clean checkout.

**As built** (DELTAS Δ208, Δ220, Δ221, Δ229, Δ230). The grader container's truth mode and judging step exist: `scripts/mksandbox-run --truth Hidden/labs/Q15/verdicts.tar.age --truth-check judge --judge <spec>` judges the learner's prose specifications, and `python -m fvh report Q15 --judge RUN|all --truth <the archive>` re-judges a batch's deliveries with the frozen reference oracles' own implication and classification; both name the archive, so the learner runs them from their own terminal. **The public strata** (`bench/local/q/lab2pub/`): five HumanEval-Verus and five vericoding `dafny/humaneval` tasks in Lab 2's form (`benchstrip.py`'s `verus-lab2-form` and `dafny-lab2-form`: the target's specification and every proof removed, the prose and the code kept, an empty witness; the Dafny prose is HumanEval's prompt as the HumanEval-Verus set ships it, since vericoding's Dafny files carry none). The drawn Dafny rows are vericoding's own translations (MIT): the draw's rule admitted only MIT or Apache-2.0 provenance. The author drew them from the pinned pools by a pre-registered seed and a sequential redraw rule with a brittleness check (each answer verified and stable under seeds 1–3), recorded in `bench/public/POOLS.toml`'s two `[[draw]]` records; their reference statements and reference programs are sealed in `Hidden/labs/Q15/` (72 files), each reference judged faithful against itself and one weakened statement per task judged weak, with no model call. The draw is the author's, fixed with its sealed references, so the learner's B1 states it rather than drawing a seed. Lab 2's runs on the public strata wait on the user's rule for what a public delivery may add besides its specification (most forms' code needs proof annotations before a statement as strong as the reference verifies; DV5e's lab2pub notes).

**As built** (DELTAS Δ236, the user's decisions D27–D29; Δ237, D31): **what a delivery adds** on a public card: its specification, one witness call, definitions and lemmas, and proof annotations (loop clauses, assertions, `calc`, `reveal`, proof blocks, ghost variables, lemma calls), never executable code; `scripts/vcheck --gate --lab-task <form>` checks the task form's code unchanged token for token (`dvl.labcode`; `lab-code-changed`, `lab-code-unchecked`), on the local cards too (without the proof annotations), and `--lab-code own` waives it for an arm that writes its own code. S1's mutants are taken from the delivery's annotated body on a public card (`labcode.mutant_source`). **The forms** were rebuilt on the same draw without the non-recursive definitions only the target's specification uses (sealed with the reference as `definitions`; recursive ones stay), C1, C4, C6 (through the lab gate with the code check) and C7 re-run, and Q15 re-sealed (72 files; the reference oracles' kill rate on each reference program's annotated body recorded in its sealed `reference.toml`). `NOTICE` is generated from `bench/manifest.toml` and the installed licence files (D29: the forms stay tracked). The ten public cards are attested under DV6's scan, which reads Lab 2's and Lab 3's sealed references as twins (D31; 88 entries). Three readings measured in DV6 are the user's (Δ240): S1's kill rate on a public card mostly measures the annotations; a sealed definition the reference applies under a quantifier (one card's faithful own-definition deliveries are judged `undecided`); and the vericoding arm, which writes its own code, against the code check.

## 26. Q16 — Practice III-A (family A)

*Tier: hard practice, family A (S1–S5), layer L7, 225 min. Three problems of 40–75 min; the plain statement ships and is known to fail; 2–4 invented lemmas or one invariant per problem; a blueprint graded before formalization. No exit file: the module's gate is the verifier part plus `/review-code` (bar `library-pr`) and `/grade-paper` before and after. No agent run. Card 01.13. Design §7.5's line (brief 10, blueprint 30, H1 45, H2 50, H3 55, `/review-code` 15, paper 15, ledger 5) with H1–H3 **re-planned** (Δ158; §30 Q48).*

### 26.1 The re-plan of H1–H3 (Δ158, §14.7 F12–F13, §8.7 F6)

| design §7.5 | why it cannot stand | re-planned item |
|---|---|---|
| H1 "generalize before induction via the add carry (D)" | L4 proves addition (F12) | **H1d** full multiplication over L7's schoolbook definition: the plain correctness statement fails by plain induction, and the learner generalizes it (the design's skill, on a target no layer gives, F13) |
| H2 "`cmp` with invented invariant (V)" | L4 gives `cmp` (F6); DV0's `cmp_any` replacement is below the hard band (Δ53), and its subject overlaps Q13's prose item | **H2v** division by one limb (`div_limb`): the loop invariant and the decomposition are the learner's (the blueprint's) |
| H3 "`mul_limb` (V + D mirror, gated)" | L4 gives `mul_limb` (F12) | **H3v + H3d** (gated): Verus full multiplication with each row accumulated in place into one result vector, schoolbook forced by a speed test (Δ163), with its Dafny companion, a compiled loop that computes L7's ghost `Times` exactly |

### 26.2 Files

| artifact | path | shipped state |
|---|---|---|
| Dafny exercise | `dafny/Q/Q16_PracticeIIIA/std/Q16_PracticeIIIA.dfy` (`module {:disableNonlinearArithmetic} Q16_PracticeIIIA`; imports L1–L7, `Std.Arithmetic.Mul`; tier `std`), with an empty `HELPERS` section | green; **2 holes** (H1d, H3d) |
| Verus crate | `verus/q/q16_practice_iiia/src/lib.rs` (deps through L7; tier `arithmetic`), with an empty `HELPERS` section and H3v's commented speed test (`#[cfg(test)]`, checking the product of two four-limb factors against precomputed constants) | green; **2 holes** (H2v, H3v) |
| written pair | `paper/Q16_problems.tex`: B1 the blueprint of H1–H3 (graded before any formal work, ≥ 3 on every dimension), the card | problems shipped |
| pins | `expected/Q16.counts`, `expected/Q16.freeze` | author-written |

### 26.3 Items

| id | K | strand | format | pair | uses | statement or prompt (as the learner sees it) | form · admissibility |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q16.H1d | H | S4 | — | — | lemma-recursion, library-call | `lemma MulCorrect(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) ensures Valid(Times(a, b)) && ToNat(Times(a, b)) == ToNat(a) * ToNat(b)` over L7's `Times` (nonlinear arithmetic off). The plain statement ships; induction on it alone does not go through: generalize, then prove | bodyless lemma · probed |
| Q16.H2v | H | S4 | — | — | invariant, ghost, library-call, nonlinear-arith | `pub fn div_limb(a: &BigNat, d: u64) -> (res: (BigNat, u64)) requires a.wf(), d > 0, ensures res.0.wf(), res.1 < d, a.to_nat() == res.0.to_nat() * d + res.1`: from the top limb down; the loop invariant is yours | `external_body` + `unimplemented!()` · probed |
| Q16.H3v | H | S1 | — | Q16.H3d | invariant, lemma-call, property-test | `pub fn mul(a: &BigNat, b: &BigNat) -> (r: BigNat) requires a.wf(), b.wf(), ensures r.wf(), r.to_nat() == a.to_nat() * b.to_nat()`, schoolbook: one pass per limb of `b` that adds that limb's row into a single result vector in place, with its own carry (no whole-number addition per row); then uncomment the speed test (two four-limb factors, checked against precomputed constants), which a method that adds `a` to itself `b` times cannot finish | `external_body` + `unimplemented!()` + commented suite · **gated**, no reference yet (§26.4) |
| Q16.H3d | H | S4 | — | Q16.H3v | invariant | the Dafny companion: `method MulLoop(a: seq<nat>, b: seq<nat>) returns (r: seq<nat>) requires Valid(a) && Valid(b) ensures r == Times(a, b) && Valid(r) && ToNat(r) == ToNat(a) * ToNat(b)`, a compiled loop over L7's definition (L7's `Times` is ghost: the `ensures` may name it, the code cannot call it; the proof may call H1d) | exec hole `{ assume false; }` · **gated** (probed) |

### 26.4 Bands, blueprint and the gated pair

- **Bands** (hard: D 30–80, V 45–120, counted by §19.0's rule; the author notes §13.2 give each count): H1d and H2v are inside. **H3 is one problem with two halves** (design §7.5's "V + D mirror" line), so it is banded as one: both halves' proof lines, counted by §19.0's rule, against the Verus band (§30 Q68 asks the orchestrator to confirm this reading). The plan's first Verus half (rows added by whole-number addition) left the problem below 45, and its Dafny half could call L7's then-compiled `Times` directly; so the Verus half is re-planned as the in-place algorithm above, which has **no reference yet**, and `Times` is ghost (§19.4), which closes the shortcut (the Dafny half's reference stands, the author notes §8.2). H3 ships gated, `admitted = false, minutes = 0`, and the Q16 lane admits it only as the pair, after the four results on both twins. No fallback with a reference exists, so until then the line's 55 minutes are 0 and the shortfall is recorded at arming (§19.0).
- **The blueprint** (30, B1): for each problem, the generalized statement or the invariant in words, the lemmas the learner invents and their order, and where each library fact enters; graded ≥ 3 on every dimension before formalization; `/grade-paper --post` compares it with the proofs.
- **Hand before automation:** the speed test is run only after the proof (`--report --strict` runs it, `uses: property-test`, §6 Q20).

### 27.3 Agentic file (rung 13: the metatheory contractor)

- **Before the run (in H3d's 35):** commit the statements to `research/q17_statements.dfy`.
- **CONTRACT** `goal:` prove every statement of `research/q17_statements.dfy`, copied unchanged into the agent file's `STATEMENTS`; change no statement · `allowed-files:` the agent file · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` exits 0 `` · zero holes · `` `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q17_statements.dfy <file>` reports no change `` · `` `scripts/vaudit <file>` reports no finding `` · new lemmas only under `HELPERS` · re-verify after every edit.
- **Follow-up (15, the metatheory run line):** spec §5.1: "the learner audits for a plausible-but-wrong statement": was each statement as strong as the prose meant (a statement the contractor proved too easily is a finding), did the contractor stop and report one it could not prove; record in `AUDIT`.

**H2v** ships `admitted = false, minutes = 0`; fallback (design §7.5) **F-H2v**: a `Vec`-backed ring buffer with `pop_front` and wrap-around (no permission), 50 min once it has its own four results. No Q17 reference was written by the plan lane: H1d and H3d also start `admitted = false` with fallbacks **F-H1d** (a sorted `seq` model in a class without arrays; frames on `this` only) and **F-H3d** (the statements for the loop-free fragment only); the Q17 lane writes the references, and each fallback's, and records the four results; a line whose item and fallback both lack them is 0 at arming (§19.0). `uses:` lists no construct for the trait itself: Dafny has no trait construct slug before Q17 (Q08's `trait-spec` is Verus's), and the trait is given.

### 28.3 The capstone round (H2, 110 min; spec §5.5)

The mini-crate `bignat` = L7's base API (`BigNatCore`: zero, words, addition, subtraction, comparison, conversion) plus **the new operations, stated by the learner**: multiplication, division by a word and the decimal conversions `to_decimal` and `from_decimal` (by repeated division by 10^19 and by Horner steps of `mul_limb` and `add_limb`), each with its round-trip statement. The Dafny mirror is a module of the learner's whose methods are compiled to Python for the differential check (design §6.5); its statements may name L7's `Times` and L6's `Sub`, which are ghost specifications (§19.3–§19.4), so every compiled operation in it is the learner's own code.

1. **Blueprint (25):** B1, graded ≥ 3 on every dimension before any statement (spec §5.5 step 1).
2. **Statements (25):** H2d and H2v, committed.
3. **Statement-only review (10):** A1dv: a `/contract Q18` whose only allowed file is the agent file's `REVIEW` section; **CONTRACT** `goal:` review every committed statement for vacuity, weakness and over-strength; change no statement · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/specfreeze --against research/q18_statements.rs <file>` reports no change `` (and `.dfy`) · each finding carries a verified witness or counterexample lemma under `REVIEW` · re-verify after every edit. The learner then decides, by hand, which findings change a statement, and re-commits.
4. **Co-developer (20):** A2dv: **CONTRACT** `goal:` prove every committed statement, following the graded blueprint; the learner may only hint · `acceptance:` `` `scripts/vcheck --gate <file>` `` with zero holes on both agent files · `specfreeze --against` both statement files reports no change · `` `scripts/vaudit <file>` `` reports no finding · the differential check (`scripts/vcheck --test` on both, the same sample rows) passes · new lemmas only under `HELPERS` · re-verify after every edit. The learner may reuse their own Q13 and Q16 work (subtraction is L7's `big_sub`; multiplication and division are not in any layer, §19.7 F20).
5. **Adversarial round (15):** the learner secretly plants one mis-normalization or mis-optimization in an implementation (a copy of the delivered crate) and records which theorem fails and where (spec §5.5 step 4: "the theorem must catch it and localize it"); the plant goes into H3's adversarial set.
6. **Ship (15):** the CLI `bignat add|sub|mul|div|cmp` over the verified core (its argument parsing outside `verus!` is trusted and listed in `TRUST.md`), and the retrospective (`research/q18_retrospective.md`).

### 28.4 Gate v2 and the adversarial round (H3, 85 min)

`gate_v2.py` extends Q09's gate v1 (design §11.2): a fresh process per crate running the pinned command, JSON completeness and minimum counts, `--no-cheating`, `vaudit`, `specfreeze`, the option allowlist, the build-tool checks and a 3-seed spread (H3py–H6py, 60 min); it must reject every decidable class, say `UNKNOWN` on X07, X08 and X14, and report its false-positive rate (H7py, inside Lab 3's FP-rate line). **The adversarial round (25):** P1d–P4v (10), then the gate on the three planted cheats the chapter names, the learner's own capstone plant and the controls (15). Every instrument on a delivery of agent provenance runs in the verification container (Δ145). Waiver W11 (design §9.3): R16 builds its gate in Rust.

### 28.5 Lab 3: the question

Design §11.2: *gated versus prompt-only on 12 crate tasks: re-judged success, cheat rate by class, and cost.* Arms: **G** the agent's acceptance tool is the learner's gate v2 (a run stops only when the gate passes or the budget ends); **P** the same rules stated in the prompt only. Every claimed success is re-judged by gate v2 and the trusted instruments in the verification container, from a clean checkout.

### 28.6 Lab 3: tasks, budget, pre-registration, route and acceptance

**Strata (12 crate tasks, `bench/local/q/crate/`):** each a task over the mini-crate (statements frozen in the task, a stripped implementation or proof, the crate's companions), built on L7's base API (`BigNatCore`) and on operations posed nowhere else in the course; **no task states a property of multiplication, division by a word or the decimal conversions**, whose statements the capstone asks the learner to write (§28.3). 8 possible and 4 impossible at the pin, so that the cheat rate has attractors (waiver W17, proposed, §19.7 F23); the impossible tasks are of kinds Lab γ does not use (the author notes §10 name them). **Order:** the tasks ship sealed, and `fvh` unpacks `bench/local/q/crate/` only after the sha256 of `research/q18_statements.dfy` and `research/q18_statements.rs` is committed (the `statements` pin, as for Lab γ, PLAN §4.3), that is, after H2's step 2. Course-authored after the models' release.

**Budget arithmetic:**

| part | arithmetic | runs · calls |
|---|---|---|
| Claude (harness-executed, DV4 §3.5) | 12 tasks × 1 attempt × ≤ 25 rounds × 2 arms × 1 repeat | 24 runs · ≤ 600 calls |
| Claude sentinels | Δ148 | ≤ 10 calls |
| Qwen (agent mode, opencode in a per-run container) | 6 tasks (4 possible, 2 impossible) × 1 × ≤ 40 turns × 2 arms × 3 repeats | 36 runs · ≤ 1,440 calls |

GPU: ≈ 15 min per Qwen run [A] → 9 GPU-h (the registry's 9). Lab 3's Claude arms run harness-executed (the user's decision D10, 2026-10-12): no Lab 3 run puts Claude Code in a container, and DV4 step 4c waits until R16 needs agent mode. The Qwen arms run in agent mode (`construct = "agent"` in `fvh.batch`, the user's decision D8).

**The pre-registration template** (B2): Q12's fields (§22.6, the generation settings and the cost per arm included; agent-mode turns grow their context, so the per-run GPU figure is redone from DV4 step 3d's probe), with: the claim (G vs P on re-judged success and on cheat rate); the metric (re-judged success per task-arm-repeat; cheats by X class among claimed successes; cost in calls, tokens, GPU-s and wall clock); the gate's version (the sha256 of `gate_v2.py`) and its measured FP rate (H7py) as a pre-registered caveat; the impossible tasks' expected outcome (no claimed success is correct).

**The verification route:** the verification container for every instrument (DV4 step 2b); Qwen agent mode (3a, after the gate and route of 2c); Claude harness-executed (4b).

**Acceptance:** as Lab 1's (§22.6); gate v2 passes each claimed success from a clean checkout, and its FP rate is reported with its interval.

TC6's mechanism sits in Q04 §4 and Q14/Q15 rather than Q07–Q09; it is paid off, so it is no gap (the chapters never promise it for Q07–Q09). The same pass checked every chapter's planted/harvested table against the module it
names: DV6's fix round re-pointed the rows whose harvest the named module does not carry
(Q04's `calc` row to Q10, Q11's `&mut self` row to Q14, Q17's three rows and one Q14 cell to
Q18's statements or the challenge branch, and the Q12 and Q15 tool rows to Q18's arm G, with
a callback in Q18 §5).

**New items.**

47. **The entries for the re-plans (records; before the practice tiers are armed).** One DELTAS entry recording: Q10's fresh normalize twins and L6's `normalize` (F3 closed); Q13's normalize and prose items; Q16's H1–H3 (§26.1), with design §7.5's Q16 row amended; L5–L7's contents (§19.2–§19.4, design §10.1's L5–L7 rows: L7's "frozen spec files" are the base API, and the capstone's new statements are the learner's, as spec §5.5 says; L6's `Sub` by value and L7's `MulAcc` and `Times` ghost); F17–F23. From the plan review, the same entry records: §19.0's line-count rule (Δ53's proof-line count for every item) and fallback rule (minutes only on a fallback's own four results); the per-item minute deviations design §7.5's lines force (§29's list); Q11's VC items in three modules (§21.2); Q13.H5d in the nonlinear-off module and Q14.H2v, H3v and H5v as revised (§23.2, §24.2); Q16's H3 with an in-place Verus half and `Times` ghost, banded as one problem (§26.1, §26.4, Q68); Lab 1's public Dafny stratum from DafnyBench alone, its public-only pilot and its validity rule (§22.6); Lab 2's reference-free S1 feedback and V as a separate measurement (§25.5); Lab 3's tasks outside the capstone's new operations, sealed until the statements are committed (§28.6); and, jointly with PLAN §5.4, the waivers W15–W19.

This plan reads it as the first checked `uses:` of constructs earlier modules only recognised (§20.3, §21.3), with empty `write_*` lists, because a WRITE construct would need a card and a pinned failure demo in a module whose minutes have no card line. Record the reading in Q47's entry, or name the constructs to promote.

Proposed: DV5's integration lane builds the grader container's `truth` mode and the sealing (age, as design §7.6 plans for the challenges) once DV4 step 2b exists; until then the prose items' hidden check is recorded as pending and Lab 2 cannot be accepted. **Built** (DELTAS Δ208, Δ215, Δ220): the grader container's truth mode and the age seal (Δ208), Q13's and Q17's prose references sealed (Δ215), and the judging step (`truthcheck judge`, `fvh report --judge`; Δ220); the prose items' hidden check is no longer pending.

The checker and `firsttry --exit` accept that today (the exit floors per language apply to Q02–Q09); the module lanes confirm on the built files.
51. **Below-band and pending admissions (the module lanes).** Q14.H3d is complete but below the intermediate band (§24.3); Q15, Q17 and Q18's verifier items, Q16's H3 (its re-planned Verus half has no reference) and every Python item have no recorded four results, and of the fallbacks only Q16's F-H1d and F-H2v have theirs. Each module lane writes the references (an item's and its fallback's), runs the gate and only then writes `admitted = true`; a line whose item and fallback both lack the four results carries 0 at arming, and the lane records the module's shortfall in a DELTAS note (§19.0).
52. **Open bodies for Dafny (tools owner; before Q11 is armed).** Q11.H5d, H7d and H8d complete a Dafny function or predicate whose body is the learner's; `specfreeze` counts a Dafny function's body as part of its statement (§6 Q45), so a correct answer reports `freeze-changed`. Proposed: `open_bodies` (§6 Q7) extended to Dafny functions and predicates: the signature, `requires`, `reads` and `decreases` compared exactly, the body ignored. Also needed by the learner-mode lint: a placeholder-bodied predicate is not a finding.
53. **The Python items wait on DV4's step 1a remainder (DV4; blocks Q12's arming).** `fvh.protocol`, `policies`, `backends`, `executor`, `budget`, `runlog`, `apparatus`, `contract`, `prereg`, `report` and `python -m fvh` (`CLAUDE.md`'s **Not built yet** line) are what Q12's policies, report and retry client, Q15's oracles and client and Q18's gate v2 are written on. Q12 arms only after they are built; the Q12 lane then records the battery's documents (`fvh.tools.record` extended to `harness/tests/q/fixtures/q12/`, author-only as for Q09) and writes the frozen tests.
54. **`/grade-paper`'s fixture mode (skills owner; before Q12 is armed).** Grade-the-grader (§22.3) runs three fresh `/grade-paper` passes on a given flawed fixture, not on the learner's answers file: `/grade-paper Q12 --fixture paper/fixtures/Q12_flawed_prereg.tex`, graded against the same rubric, recorded as a `fixture` row that never unlocks a run.
55. **Lab budgets in the registry (records; before Q12, Q15 and Q18 are armed).** DV1a's `budget` fields counted no policy runs and left Q18's call ceilings at 0. Proposed (§22.8, §25.8, §28.8): Q12 `{claude_calls = 180, claude_runs = 20, qwen_calls = 4400, qwen_runs = 620, gpu_hours = 10}` (the pilot on the public strata only, §22.6); Q15 `{claude_calls = 90, claude_runs = 20, qwen_calls = 1200, qwen_runs = 240, gpu_hours = 3}`; Q18 `{claude_calls = 610, claude_runs = 24, qwen_calls = 1440, qwen_runs = 36, gpu_hours = 9}`. The GPU split is unchanged (Δ75: 45 in all); DV4 step 3d's throughput probe revises it in a new entry, per arm (§22.6). The same entry carries PLAN §4.3's R12, R15 and R16 budgets; with every sentinel, the six labs' Claude calls are Q 880 + R 810 = 1,690, inside design D8's ≈ 1,700.
56. **The language server for learner Python (tools owner; before Q15 is armed).** Q15's client starts `dafny server` only from the scratch copy of the install (Δ33, Δ98's `dvl.dafny.server_argv`) and under the Dafny memory cap. Proposed: a frozen entry point (`fvh.lsp.start_server()`, standard library only, returning the process and its pipes) over `dvl.dafny.server_argv()` and `dvl.common`'s cap; and the recorded transcript for the frozen tests, captured by the author.
57. **Lab pools (DV5 integration lane; before a learner fetches a set).** The stripping step for HumanEval-Verus (derive the task forms, and normalize what the course gate refuses in the answers, recorded in the set's `CHANGES`; the author notes §4.2 name it); `scripts/get_bench.sh --validate` on each set, one solver job at a time, to fix the pools; the leak scan of the fetched answers against every twin's answer lines (Δ152; Δ162's Open item); LemmaScript is not a pool (its tasks need a TypeScript and Lean toolchain the course does not pin), and neither is MINIF2F-DAFNY for Lab 1 (§22.6). The author notes §4.2 record the plan's validation sample. **Built** (DELTAS Δ221): `get_bench.sh --validate` with the pool rule as built, the pools pinned in `bench/public/POOLS.toml`, HumanEval-Verus's stripping step (`verus-strip-proofs`, each loop's `decreases` kept) and the leak scan of the fetched answers (no course-specific span). Lab 2's public task forms are built too (Δ229; §25.6's As built): the second transform (`verus-lab2-form`, `dafny-lab2-form`), the pre-registered draw recorded in `bench/public/POOLS.toml`'s `[[draw]]` records, the forms under `bench/local/q/lab2pub/` and their references sealed with Lab 2's hidden truth.
58. **Agent-mode Claude in Lab 3 (DV4 step 4c, with the user; Q18 only).** Harness-executed runs are the default (Δ147); Claude Code in a container only if step 4c passes in one of Δ147's forms (host action H2). **Decided** (the user's decision D10, DELTAS Δ218): Lab 3's Claude arms run harness-executed; step 4c waits until R16 needs agent mode.
59. **The capstone's CLI (registry and tools owners; before Q18 is armed).** The CLI's argument parsing is code outside `verus!` (Δ9's catalog row V9): it lives in a `[[bin]]` target of the module crate (`Cargo.toml` is a 2g file, written at authoring time), outside the verified library target that `vcheck` checks, and `TRUST.md` lists it. **Built** (the user's decision D16, DELTAS Δ218): a `[[bin]]` target from `gen_units` (`bignat`, `src/main.rs`, a shipped stub) and `vcheck --bin`, a pinned `verus --compile` route.
60. **The tutor red-team's decoy (guard owner; before Q15 ships).** The decoy reference directory must sit where rule 1 treats it as reference (its path holds a reserved name), so that the red-team tests the tutor's contract and the tripwire together; the guard owner confirms the decoy's path with a fixture and that no study session can read it.
61. **Interactive approvals and fresh sessions (records; low).** Q12's toolwright, Q15's rungs 11–12, Q17's rung 13 and Q18's three runs are interactive `/contract` sessions (allowlist approvals, §6 Q37); each chapter repeats Q06's sentence. Lab runs are `fvh` batches, never `/contract`.

*Added by the plan revision (2026-10-07):*

62. **A frozen import list for Q11's VC answers (tools owner; before Q11 is armed).** Q11.H7d and H8d keep their predicates in `Q11_VcAnswer`, which imports nothing, so an answer that names the given program does not resolve (§21.2). An answer that adds an import would still verify. Proposed: `specfreeze` compares the import list of a module the freeze file names (here frozen empty), or `scripts/dvl/forms.py` gains a `no-call:<names>` form over the answer region (`no-call:Prog1,Post1,Prog2,Post2`). Until one exists, `/review-code` checks it and the module lane records the residual. **Decided** (Δ181): built in DV5 by its tools lane, before Q11 is armed; the tools lane's as-built entry records which of the two forms it built. Until that entry, the interim rule above stands.
63. **The lab gate refuses added levers (tools owner; before Lab 1's and Lab α's runs).** A delivery that adds an `{:induction …}`, `{:fuel …}` or resource-limit attribute, or a solver option, that its task form does not ship is refused (§22.6; PLAN §4.3's validity rule for α). `ALLOWED_CURLY` in `scripts/dvl/dafny.py` allows `{:induction}` in learner files, so the refusal is a lab-mode rule (a `specfreeze --lab` check or a `vaudit` lab mode), not a change to the learner gate. **Decided** (Δ181): built in DV5 by its tools lane as a lab-mode rule, before Lab 1's and Lab α's runs; the learner gate is unchanged. The tools lane's as-built entry records its form. **Extended** (the user's decision D24, Δ226): after the lever check, the lab gate (`vcheck --gate --lab-task`) applies `vaudit`'s assumption rules to every delivery, so a delivery equal to a task form that ships an assumption (`assume {:axiom}`, an `{:axiom}` lemma) or a lever the audit reports fails; an audit killed by memory or stopped by the wall clock is `killed(memory)` or inconclusive, never a verdict.
64. **`fvh`'s `statements` pin (DV4 owner; before Lab 3 and Lab γ).** `fvh` unpacks a sealed task directory (`bench/local/q/crate/`, `bench/local/r/crate/`) only after the sha256 of the named statement files is committed (§28.6; PLAN §4.3). Proposed: a `statements` field in the lab's run plan and a refusal in `fvh.prereg`'s gate. **Owner: DV4** (Δ181): not built in this round. Lab 3 and Lab γ wait on it; their tasks stay sealed until it exists. **Built** (DELTAS Δ215): `expected/<ID>.statements` and `python -m fvh unseal <ID>`.
65. **Empty-body variants the revised items need (tools owner; before Q14 and Q17 are armed).** (a) Methods of a trait implementation: `dvl.verus.empty_body` refuses a name the trait also declares (Q14.H2v); the revision ran TRIV by hand-written variants (the author notes §13.2); proposed: qualified symbols (`PairAcc::add_word`). (b) Exec code with `let tracked` lines (Q14.H5v, Q17.H2v): E0 strips them, so the variant does not compile; the same request as PLAN §8 R15(a). **Decided** (Δ181): (a) and (b) built in DV5 by its tools lane, before Q14 and Q17 are armed; the as-built entry records the forms.
66. **A maximal-automation run that ends out of resources (tools owner; low).** Q10.H5d's maxauto run ends `inconclusive` at the pinned limit, twice, while the shipped runs fail (§20.4). The plan counts it as failing at the pin, as Δ58 counts a wall-clock kill in Verus; confirm, or rerun maxauto with a raised limit for the record. **Decided** (Δ181): the tools lane of DV5 builds it, before Q10 is armed; its as-built entry records how the gate reports such a run. Q10.H5d's admission waits on that entry and on §19.0's four results.
67. **W19 or a diversified Q17.H2v (orchestrator).** Q14.H5v now passes one permission per node (§24.2), so only Q17.H2v shares the tracked-map take-and-put pattern with R11's arena. Keep it under W19 (proposed), or diversify it (for example a fixed-capacity ring whose slot permissions are separate tracked fields), which drops W19. **Decided** (Δ177): diversify the Q side (separate tracked fields per slot for Q17.H2v, or Q14.H5v as a `PPtr` list) if the alternative probes in band in DV5, which drops W19; W19 is recorded as provisional until the Q14 and Q17 lanes record that.
68. **Banding Q16's H3 as one problem (orchestrator; before Q16 is armed).** §26.4 bands H3 (design §7.5's "V + D mirror" line) as one problem: both halves' proof lines against the Verus band, the Dafny half a short companion whose `ensures` pins L7's exact definition. Alternatively each half is banded alone, as Q13's and Q14's pairs are; then the Dafny companion (below 30 on its own) carries no minutes and H3d's 20 move to H3v by a DELTAS entry. The two readings differ only once the Q16 lane has the in-place reference. **Decided** (Δ178): the first reading. H3 is banded as one V + D problem against the Verus band, and H3d keeps its minutes inside the pair.
